# Pattern Walk + Pattern Studio

ARCH 540 Assignment 2 (UBC M.Arch). Two linked single-file web tools for cultural pattern work in Vancouver's Punjabi Market and Chinatown. No build step; open the HTML files directly or host on GitHub Pages (like Pocket Studio).

## Files
- `pattern-studio.html` — the main tool. Image → pattern (symmetry detector, colour-layer reading, shape tracing), Text → pattern, **Ombrae tiles** (reflected-light optical tiles), 2D pattern maker (tilings, Hankin star patterns, motif lattices), 3D panel with relief/emboss modes, light study, exports (SVG, DXF, STL, heightmap PNG, tile CSV, JSON). Engine `pattern-core.js` is inlined inside it; three.js r128 loads from cdnjs. Published at https://claude.ai/artifact/X9kjU9vH4RqwFcqBY7NKNW — republish to that URL, never a new one.
- `pattern-core.js` — standalone detector + pattern engine (UMD). `analyze(gray,w,h)` returns lattice, wallpaper group, scores or a stop reason; `makePattern`, `remix` (kaleidoscope under any of the 17 groups), `basisFor`.
- `pattern-walk-map.html` — Vancouver map (Leaflet from cdnjs, City open data embedded: shoreline, streets, buildings/parcels/addresses/trees around the two focus areas, public art, cultural spaces). Explore → Survey → Propose workflow, Google keyless embeds for Street View, opportunity cells, site brief, hand-off text.
- `pattern-walk-guide.html` — how to use both, updated workflow diagram, what to add next.
- `fig1…fig5` — mid-review diagrams.

## Ombrae tiles tab
A second way of making a panel, parallel to the cut pattern rather than part of it: `S.omb.on` switches `generate()` to `ombGenerate()`, which returns `{tiles, cols, rows}` instead of `holes`, and every downstream function (`render2D`, `renderOrtho`, `buildGeometryFrom`, `updateStats`, `svgText`, `dxfText`, `exportBundle`, `pinSVG`) branches on `G.omb`.
- **Which sun works.** The tonal range is `A = sin(tilt)·hypot(Lx,Ly)` — how far the sun is off the panel's own *face*, nothing to do with how low it is. A sun straight at the face (azimuth 0, low elevation) gives no range at all; one well round in azimuth, **or high overhead**, gives the most. An earlier version of this file and of the UI copy said a low sun was strongest, which is backwards; the QA `behaviour` group now pins it.
- **The model.** Each tile is a disc hinged on a diameter and tilted out of the face. With the tilt fixed, the hinge's plan angle θ sweeps the disc normal around a cone, so `n·L = sin α·R·sin(θ−φ) + cos α·Lz` — monotonic in θ over half a turn, which inverts exactly: `θ = φ + asin(2t−1)` for a target tone t. That is why tones are solved for **one** design sun (`omb.designAz/El`) and wash out as the live sun moves; the washout is the effect, not a bug, and `ombStats()` reports how far off the design light you are.
- **Sources** all end as one luminance raster in `OMB.lum` (built by `ombField`): image upload, the photo already loaded in Image → pattern, lettering (`ombLetter`), six built-ins (`ombBuiltin`), or a text prompt that asks `sample` for a greyscale SVG which `ombCleanSVG` strips (script / image / foreignObject / on\* / external href) before `ombFromSVG` rasterises it. No model in the view → `ombKeyword` picks a built-in from the words.
- **Dimensions** are reconstructed from KPS's published figures (Ø12.7 / 25.4 mm, 31.75 mm pitch, 30°, 3 mm proud), not from their process. Ombrae™ / OpticalTile™ / Sculptural Imaging™ are Ombrae Studios + KPS marks; the attribution is in the tab, the README and this file, and must stay in all three.
- **The deliverable is `-tiles.csv`** — one row per tile (x, y, hinge angle, tilt, tone). The DXF/SVG/STL are for reading; the CSV is what a CNC setup or the Grasshopper direction would be driven from.
- `omb.field` in the design JSON is the tone raster as a ≤256 px PNG data URL, so a saved design reloads its image (~19 KB).
- Tile count is capped at 24,000 with a warning; `omb.seg` (3D facets) is the control that makes the 3D view heavy.
- `OMB_BUILTIN` is eleven designs, the first a real photograph (five pictures grounded in real Ombrae subjects, three abstract, two calibration charts) and `OMB_PRESETS` is eight complete worked panels. The maple leaf is an explicit outline, not a polar function — every procedural attempt gave slivers or a blob. Clouds need six noise octaves; fewer and the mask cuts straight diagonal edges that read as broken glass.

## Perforated image tab
A third panel kind, sharing the tile panel's tone field (`OMB.lum`) but encoding it as hole
**diameter** rather than tile rotation. `S.perf.on` switches `generate()` to `perfGenerate()`,
which returns the ordinary `{holes, open, W, H}` shape plus a `perf` metadata block — so
`render2D`, `buildGeometryFrom`, the DXF/SVG/STL exporters and `finish()`-free stats all work
with no branching beyond the backing colour and the stats line. That was the design choice
that made it cheap: a perforated image IS a cut panel, just one whose openings are computed
rather than tiled.
- **The two fabrication rules** are a hole no narrower than the sheet is thick, and a web
  (ligament between holes) no thinner than that either. `perfDesc()` warns on both. `maxD` is
  clamped to `pitch - minWeb` and to `rowPitch*pitch - minWeb` inside `perfGenerate`.
- **Slot length must be bounded by the row spacing** (`Math.min(d*aspect, rp-web)/2`). Without
  that bound slots overlap their neighbours and the reported open area climbs past 100%. The
  `perforated` QA group caught exactly that.
- `S.omb.on` and `S.perf.on` are mutually exclusive; each checkbox clears the other.
- Over 7,000 holes the 3D view builds the blank sheet and says so — extruding twenty thousand
  holes costs a minute and tells you nothing the 2D drawing doesn't.
- The photographic built-in (`gondola`, a 384 px greyscale JPEG as a data URL, ~41 KB base64)
  decodes asynchronously. `photoEl()` is called for every entry in `PHOTO_SRC` **at load**, and
  the branch falls back to mid-grey rather than black, because a lazily-created image meant the
  first build after picking it came out empty. Both the QA `perforated` group and the live tool
  hit that.
- Zahner call theirs ImageWall, Imark call theirs Ocular; this is an independent reconstruction
  of the principle and says so in the tab, the export README and here.

## Fields and brick courses (next phase, 30 Oct 2026)
- **Fields** (`S.field`, `FIELD_KINDS`, `fieldValue(F,u,v,W,H)` with u right and v UP, 1 = light). `ombFieldCanvas()` rasterises the field into the same tone canvas a photograph uses (`OMB.kind='field'`), alone or `multiply/add/mask` over `OMB.picEl`, the picture it replaced; `ombDrawField()` regenerates it instead of refitting when `OMB.kind==='field'`. `fieldUse()` is the single entry (sets `S.omb.src='field:<kind>'`). The cut pattern reads it through `grad.dir='field'` in `finish()` and `reliefHeights()`. Snapshots do NOT save a thumbnail raster for a field design; `applyPreset` rebuilds it from `S.field`. The sun field weighs each quarter hour by cos(el)·cos(az off the normal) and normalises to the face's own sunlight, so a flat face with no overhang reads 1 everywhere; in June at 49° a 600 mm overhang shades the top half of a south face all day, which is right.
- **Brick courses** (`S.brick`, `brickGenerate()` → `{bricks:[{x,y,i,r,len,theta,shift,t}],brick:{…}}`, flagged by `G.brick` the way `G.perf` flags the perforated panel). Running bond closes odd courses with two half bricks (`len`). The bearing rule: a brick may stand out `maxOver × bW`; a turned brick's corner stands out `len/2·sin θ` plus its offset, so θ is clamped and `brick.clamped` counts it. `renderBrick2D` is the shaded elevation, `renderBrickOrtho` the course drawing (red = apparent width `len·cosθ + bW·|sinθ|`, green = turn or offset), `brickGeometry` one `BoxGeometry` per brick merged, `brickCSV` the placement list, `brickSVGText`/`brickDXFText` the drawings. Modes are exclusive: `setPanelMode('brick')` switches the material to `brick` (a `Cast & stone` entry whose gauges are brick widths) and sets `S.thickness = bW`. The picture block (and the Field picker) is shared through `PIC_HOSTS.brick`. A UI `const` must not share a name with a function (`brickTones` vs `brickTone`): the page fails to boot with no message in run-qa; capture it with Chrome `--enable-logging=stderr` on `qa/_build/qa.html` (`--keep`).
- **Serial sections** (`S.serial`, `serialGenerate()` → `{ribs:[{i,c,a0,len,N,v,lo,hi,area,comp}],serial:{…}}`, flagged by `G.serial`). `v` is a Float32Array of depths (op contour) or angles in degrees (op twist) at N+1 stations along the rib; `c` is the rib's position across the panel, `a0` where it starts along. Horizontal ribs run along x at y = c; vertical along y at x = c. **Zero waste** (`zeroWaste`, contour only): rib 2k+1 is `stripW − rib 2k` with `stripW = dMin + dMax`, so a pair is cut from one strip with one line; `comp` marks the complement. `serialNest()` lays strips across the short side of the sheet and pieces end to end along the long side; a rib longer than the sheet is cut in `splice` pieces. `serialNestSVG`/`renderSerialOrtho` draw it (blue sheet and strip, red cut, green label); `serialSVGText`/`serialDXFText` export it (SHEETS / STRIPS / CUTS / LABELS, DXF TEXT entities); `serialCSV` is the schedule with the whole profile per row. 3D: contour ribs are an extruded Shape per rib (horizontal: rotateX(π/2) then translate to y = c + t; vertical: rotateY(−π/2) then translate to x = c + t); twist bands are a ribbon BufferGeometry between the hinge line and the swung tip, both windings. The mode sets `S.material` to `ply` unless it is already timber or sheet metal, and `S.thickness = t`. A field over a spanning set is sampled per panel slice, so a probe that wants the whole tone range on one panel must set `S.arr='single'` first (the twist check tripped on this).
- **Kinetic units** (`S.kin`, `kinGenerate()` → `{units:[{x,y,i,j,u,v,s,duty,stroke}],kin:{…}}`, flagged by `G.kin`). `kinStateAt(K,u,v,t,W,H)` is the whole rule: resting state from the tone, then the motion: `sun` uses `sunAt(lat,month,hour)` (hour = dayFrom + t·(dayTo − dayFrom)) and deploys by cos(azimuth off the face normal), gated by elevation; `walk` moves a point from `walkFrom` to `walkTo` with t and lights units within `reach`; `pulse` is a lagged sine. `blend` says how the picture and the motion combine (max / mul / add). The table (`kinCSV`) re-evaluates every unit at every step, so `steps` sets its width. 3D: a frame of four bars per unit plus petals (triangles hinged on the rim whose tip reaches the centre at s = 1 and lifts off the face as they fold), an iris disc, or a louvre flap turned (1 − s)·90°. Defaults pitch 300 / unit 270; a 900 mm panel at the old 600 pitch gave one column, which is how the walker test first failed.
- **Pavers** (`S.paver`, `paverGenerate()` -> `{pavers:[{pts,cx,cy,cut,area,t,type}],paver:{counts,cuts,areas,palette,heights,dropped,conflicts,pairs}}`, flagged by `G.paver`). `paverCells(P,W0,H0)` builds every layout in pattern space with the origin at the frame's corner (so at spin 0 a 300 mm grid starts with a whole paver at the corner), rotated by `spin` about that corner and clipped with the tool's `clipRect`; pieces under `minPiece` of a paver are dropped and counted. Herringbone is the lattice (w,-w), (L,L) with a horizontal paver at each lattice point and a vertical one at its right-hand end, checked numerically for 2:1, 3:1 and 4:1 before it went in. Cairo pavers are grown by quarter turns about the two right-angle corners, de-duplicated by centroids snapped within 0.3 mm. Voronoi: `paverSites` (jittered grid, or a Poisson disk whose radius follows the tone) then `voronoiCells` (the frame clipped by bisectors nearest first, stopping at the security radius). `paverNeighbours` finds pavers sharing a stretch of edge (collinear overlap, so running bond and herringbone neighbours count). Types: each type's level is its colour's luminance normalised across the palette; quantise = nearest level, dither = error diffusion over the neighbour graph in reading order, scatter = jittered tone; `apart` re-types a paver that matches a neighbour already laid. 3D: one extruded shape per paver at its type's height with a per-vertex colour; `makeMaterialFor` turns on `vertexColors` for `G.paver`. `paverCSV`, `paverScheduleCSV(list)`, `paverSVGText` (a group per type), `paverDXFText` (TYPE_n layers). The mode takes concrete unless the material is already Cast & stone; `S.thickness` = the tallest type. QA group `paver`, 7 checks (all eleven layout cases cover the frame once, by area and by point sampling).
- **Pass 18, the interface** (built by an agent on its own copy, merged after pass 20): nothing in it computes. `ui18.css`, `ui18.js` and `ui18-pic.js` (kept in the scratchpad's sbx-ui folder with `patch18.py`, which rebuilds the file from an untouched copy) are inlined. Every bound control carries `data-path` (its state key), and `curate(name, picHost, sections, spec, folds)` reorders the existing controls of a mode tab into the picture block, two headed sections of primary controls (`ctlIn(scope, path)`) and the folds; no control or state key was removed and every id and tab name is unchanged. `TAB_ORDER` now puts Fabricate & export before Bank. The rail is grouped under Source / Design / Finish / Deliver. `FACADE_MODES=['brick','serial','kin','paver']`: `facadeStart()` gives those modes a gradient field (`fieldDefaults()`, kind gradient, `fieldUse()`, `OMB.auto=true`) when no picture was chosen, `picStart()` gives the others the gondola; `OMB.auto` marks a picture the tool picked itself so it can be swapped on a mode change, and anything a person or a recipe picked stays. A facade recipe with neither field nor picture loads on the gradient (`facadeStart(true)`). The 2D captions are set as 10.5 px text under the drawing (the canvas keeps its own copy for Save image and the exports). Headless Chrome will not go under 526 px wide, so the phone layout is checked there.
- **Pass 40, the stacked tower redesigned from construction** (6 Nov, Opus; replaces pass 35's floating bands). `STACK_DEF` gains `slab` (250), `cant` (3000, the cantilever limit past the glass), `core` (share of the smallest floor), `colMax` (column spacing); `storeyH` default 3200. `stackBase` samples the outline THROUGH its corners (polygon: `ceil(N/n)` per side; rectangle: per side by length) and marks `corner`; normals from the neighbours (the bisector at a corner). `stackOutlines()` returns `floors` (storeys + 1: `E` the glass line tapered and twisted, `N` its normals, `O` the slab edge = E + N x (300 nosing + the field's push, lightly smoothed, held to `cant`; measured AFTER taper so the nosing is true on every floor; the ground slab is a level 600 plinth), `layers` (each slab, roof, then `layers - 1` fins per storey interpolated between slab edges), `cols` (sample indices: every corner plus no more than `colMax` apart), `colD` (250 + 25 per storey, 300 to 1200), `core` (square side), `held`. `stackGeometry` merges by role (`domeMerge`): slab (extruded edge outlines + the roof parapet), core (300 walls `domeRingSolid` one storey above the roof, with a roof slab), col (round, 500 in from the glass, raked with the twist), glass (a quad strip between floors), mull (every sample), bal (glass balustrade 1060 on every slab edge), rail, fin, post (fins on posts fixed to the slab edges, every ~1.8 m). `frameCamera` / orbit target / wall / floor follow `stackActive()`. Back in Compose (no `draft`; `CMP_HOST` revolved, volume, cylinder list it). `-layers.csv` = layer, kind, storey, y, thickness, outline. QA `host` p40-1 replaces p35-1 (nosing 300 to cant, segments under 3 m, a column at all 8 corners, core clear, taper 0.8, twist 40, 9 meshes on the ground, 6 m push held, rectangle corners exact). Rendered and judged (`dev-tools/scripts/stack40-*.png`). Not yet: a unit pattern on the slab edges or the fins, a podium wider than the tower, structure checked beyond rules of thumb.
- **Pass 43, corners and floors for panels on a plan** (6 Nov, Opus; the user: "every single 3d form does not have the proper structurally constructed corners or floor to floor connections"). `planb43.js` before `planActive`: `PLANB_DEF` {on, cavity 600, storeyH 0 = the panel row when 2600 to 4800, else 3200}; `S.planb` in the default state; `planBActive()` = a plan, not brick mode, not switched off. `planBuild(zB,zF)` runs in `rebuildScene` right after the plan panels are placed (zB/zF from `baseGeom`'s bounding box less t/2). Corners: a post at every turn over 2 degrees, its square on the bisector and SIZED to hold the four panel corners of the joint (front and back of both ends) plus the mitre points, never under 160; `covered` counts it. A fixing rail behind every joint along a side. On a closed plan facing out: slabs at every storey line inside the inner glass (the plan offset by zB - cavity, mitred with `meet`), a grating across the cavity at each floor and a coping that closes it on top, a bracket from each slab edge to 15 mm behind the panels at every joint and floor (the skin's floor-to-floor tie), the inner glass wall with mullions, round columns 650 inside the glass at corners and no more than 7.5 m apart, a core when the inner inradius passes 7 m. Merged by role, `userData.planB = role`; `window.PS_PLANB` is the count record; `.planb-note` under Host surface with the switch and the cavity slider. The OBJ names these groups `building_<role>`; `hostSceneBox` in run-qa.py leaves them out of the panel count; the OBJ test finds min/max with a loop. Compose's tower and court are sized to be buildings (a tower's sides hold at least 38 m of perimeter; a court at least 12 m a side). QA `assembly` p43-1. Rendered (`dev-tools/scripts/planb43-*.png`).
- **Pass 42, the tower's joints** (6 Nov): heavier mullions (150) on every plan corner, a transom along each slab top and under each slab, an anchor bracket at every mullion foot and head, a drop panel under every column head (held so its corners stay 100 inside the glass); `anchor` is a tenth mesh role (p40-1 expects 10).
- **Pass 41, the voxel building redesigned from construction** (6 Nov, Opus; replaces pass 36). `VOX_DEF` gains `h` (storey 3100), `cant`, `podium`. `voxCells()` erodes as before, then makes it stand storey by storey: a module bears on the one below (never on a cantilever); with `cant`, a module with nothing below may cantilever one module off a supported neighbour; the core (the inside cell nearest the base centre, never eroded) must be on every storey and every module must be reached from it on its storey, else it is left out (`floating`, `detached`, `cants`, `coreTop`, `kept`, `key`). Faces: `out` (no neighbour), `top`; `party` counts shared faces; balconies on outside faces of non-core modules. `voxGeometry` merges by role: podium pads, floor and ceiling cassettes, posts inside the wall line, party walls, outside walls the full module width (they LAP at an outside corner) with a window sized by the field (door on a balcony face), a floor band over every floor-to-floor joint, a 140 cover strip over every vertical joint to a neighbour, a corner angle (one leg per face) at an outside corner, a 200 trim in every concave corner, 260 steel tie plates at every shared corner node (moved inside the occupied modules), balcony slabs with glass and rails, concrete core walls with an overrun, roofs with parapets. `-modules.csv` adds core, cantilever, outside faces. Camera, wall and floor follow `voxActive()`. Back in Compose (volume, plane). QA `host` p41-1 replaces p36-1. Rendered at building scale and at a corner (`vox41-*.png`).
- **Pass 44, mitred corners, tabs that work over a form, Compose in metres** (6 Nov, Opus; the user: corners "not meeting properly and mitred together, there's still overlap"; "clicking between designs doesn't work properly or glitches"). `planLayout` now lets the sides of a plan meet AT the corner (`cg = 0`; the gap stays between panels along a side). In `rebuildScene`'s plan branch every panel end at a turn is sheared onto the bisector plane (right end x += z tan(turn/2), left end x -= z tan(turn/2), z from the panel's mid-plane, only the vertices on the end face, within 0.6 mm of the bounding box), so the two panels meet face to face: no overlap inside, no gap outside, for every panel kind except brick (the bond turns the corner); `PLAN_MITRES` counts the cut ends. `planBuild`'s corner check is now that the mitred front and back corners coincide within 0.5 mm, and the corner element is a steel angle (two 120 x 8 legs) behind the joint; brick plans get the building behind them too (no angles, no rails). `showTab(name, byUser)` on a panel tab sets aside a whole-building form that is on (stack, voxel, dome, loft) with a toast, then sets the mode and schedules a build: before this a click on Brick courses or Perforated image over a dome changed nothing on screen. `cmpSizeForm(form,r,opts)` runs in `composeDesign` after the mode is set and sizes the open forms in metres from the final panel size (wall 9 to 21 m, row 12 to 24, serpentine 15 to 30, curve its whole length, hall 12 to 24 long and 6 to 12 across, a bowed wall within a half circle, a sphere host at least half its width in radius, wave 12 to 24; storeys 3 m): before this they came out one panel wide and several storeys tall. Two `assembly` checks updated with reason (the court closes at `last.len`, and its depth is two columns, their joint and the mitred corners). Headless Chrome under `--virtual-time-budget` throttles animation frames, so a click-through there shows stale scenes; the real browser pane (`.claude/launch.json`, `python -m http.server 8765`) is the place to judge switching. Audit tools: `dev-tools/scripts/shotgrid.py` (a contact sheet of steps run in one page, `raw` steps click the real buttons).
- **Pass 44b/c** (6 Nov): a mitred end is moved to the panel's TRUE edge (x = W/2 + z tan(turn/2)), not merely tilted, because serial ribs stop at the frame inset and left a notch; kinetic, brick and paver panels are not mitred; kinetic panels get a shared 160 frame bar at each plan corner (`planBuild`). `kinGenerate` lays the lattice over the whole panel with half-pitch margins on a set (`arr==='grid'` with more than one panel), so the unit spacing across a joint equals the spacing inside a panel (continuous when the width is a multiple of the pitch, as Compose's are). Judged in `corners44-per-kind.png`, `corners44-ribs.png`, `kin44-lattice.png`.
- **Pass 47, stacked timber on a fixed-length chain** (6 Nov, Opus; the user: "brick stacked assemblies and wood stacked assemblies with dowel or other wood connectors to create parametric wood generated pavilions or other facades"; synthesised from the fixed-length chain (Mantra), stacked aggregates (brick bond) and threaded sections (Zero/Fold), copying none). `S.crib` {on, d0, d1, dmid (a bulge, 0 none), height, lean, sides (0 round), arc (360 closed pavilion, less an open wall), L (pivot to pivot), w, h, e (overhang past the pivot), dowel, fold 'in'|'out', open, slotH, seed, footing, cap} (`CRIB_DEF`; recipes, snapshots, Compose reset it). `cribCourses()`: m pleats chosen so the widest designed course is already folded >= 22 deg (a chain that starts straight folds violently); each course is built by `build(d,cx)` (outer pivots A at m equal arc lengths of the outline, inner pivots M at depth sqrt(L^2 - (c/2)^2) from each chord: solved from L, never set); the STACKING RULE takes, course by course, the largest step toward the designed diameter and lean for which every pivot moves less than (w - dowel)/2 - 2 from the course below (bisection; `held` counts the courses held). Even courses take members A_k->M_k, odd M_k->A_k+1, so at every pivot a member crosses the one below (a log-cabin crossing at every node). Dowels: where the upper member's pivot lies inside the lower member's end (width less the dowel, overhang e past the pivot); `slips` otherwise. Openings: a whole pleat removed over a height, framed by jamb posts at the two side pivot stacks and a two-course glulam lintel along the pleat (`carried` pivots); the first is the entrance from the ground at the front; windows where the field is dark, never adjacent. An open wall stands on end posts. `unsup` counts member ends with nothing under them. `cribGeometry` merges by role (members by parity, dowels, footing ring, glulam cap ring, frame: jambs, lintels, end posts); `window.PS_CRIB` holds the record; `-members.csv` (one cut length for all) and `-dowels.csv` in the bundle. Controls under Host surface (3D) with `.crib-note`; Compose has 'timber pavilion' (revolved hosts lead with it) and 'timber wall'. QA `host` p47-1: four cases (pavilion, leaning bulging hexagon, curved wall, steep cone) all one member length, 0 slips, 0 unsupported ends, a dowel for every member above the first course, an entrance. Rendered: `timber47.png`.
- **Pass 46, roofs built** (6 Nov): the vault between walls (`roofGeometry`/`roofBuild` replaced; `roofArc` arc-length table, `roofBrickBox`): a 220 concrete ring beam on each wall, steel tie rods with end plates every ~3 m; brick walls carry a reinforced-brick catenary vault (courses along the hall on the curve in running bond) closed at each gable by a rowlock arch flush with the top; other modes carry ribs, purlins every 600 and a timber deck (serial) or a standing-seam skin with 300 eaves. The plan building's roof: the roof slab a parapet below the top (`par` up to 1100, `roofY`), an upstand wall on the inner glass line, a membrane, a coping over upstand, cavity and panels (stone 75 over brick, aluminium 40 otherwise); columns stop under the roof slab. QA `host` p30-1 restated for the ring beam, ties and brick vault.
- **Pass 45, brickwork laid as a bricklayer lays it** (6 Nov): on a plan the wall's BACK face sits on the plan line (`m.translateZ(t/2)`; the quoin and squint rules assume it, and the centred wall overshot every corner by half a brick: a plus sign at a square corner, a squint poking out on a hexagon); a brick set has no frame margins between storeys or columns (the 60 mm slot read as a dark band at every floor) and no gap between panels (`setPanelMode('brick')` sets it 0); `PLAN_SIDE` gives each brick panel its side, and every course is laid along the WHOLE side from its left end (the left corner's quoin rule), the half-brick carried across panel joints, the closer at the right end; each brick drawn by the panel holding its centre: no straight joints, no split bricks. QA `assembly` course check restated (no gap wider than a joint, no straight joint through two courses at a panel joint); `brick` frame check scoped to a single panel. Rendered: `brick45-corners.png`, `roofs46.png`.
- **Pass 48, the planted ground** (6 Nov, Opus; the user named Keio's modular roof pavers and Roche's pocket park; synthesised, not copied). `S.paver.holeBy` 'type' (the old per-type holes) | 'field': every slab whose tone passes `holeCut` gets a hole from `holeMin` to `holeMax` by tone (`holeGamma` favours small holes), shrunk 10 mm at a time until it clears every slab edge by 15 mm (`paverFits`), `holesField`/`holesHeld` counted. The attractor-curve field takes several curves separated by `|`. Planting follows the hole's size at real plant sizes, not scaled to the hole (`garden48.js`, before `paverGeometry`): `gardenTrees(Gk)` picks trees by CROWN SPACING (largest holes first, none within `treeGap`, default 3500; beds join only by `bedTrees`, default 0, so a culled design gets no trees unless it asks); `gardenTree` is 3 to 5 splayed stems with forks and a crown of 10 to 14 masses from 40 % of the height up; 260 to 420 mm a concrete stool (share `stools`) or a grass clump; large holes without a tree a shrub; 150 to 260 grass; under 150 gravel. A culled cell is a bed (`gardenBed`): soil to its edge, groundcover masses, sometimes a shrub, a tree only where the spacing allows. `S.paver.edge` 'none' | 'kerb' (granite, 150) | 'parapet' (roof, 1100 with a coping). Compose's ground (`COMPOSE_GROUND`) is a roof garden (square slabs, two seeded attractor curves, holes by the field, stools, a parapet) or a pocket park (square slabs culled round four attractor points into beds, two paths kept, trees in some beds, a kerb), 15 to 24 m. QA `paver` p48-1 (continuous hole sizes, clearance, both curves draw, the spacing rule). Rendered: `gardens48.png`, `gardens48-eye.png`.
- **Pass 49, the stacked timber built by a robot** (6 Nov): `cribRobot()` divides the base outline into sectors whose chord fits 1.4 x reach (`S.fab.reach`), each robot base 0.45 x reach inside the outline; members are ordered course, station, angle; per member: pick from one feeder (all identical), place on its pivots at its angle, then drill both pivots through into the course below (2h - 10 deep) and drive a dowel in each; `-robot.csv` (op, member, course, station, X, Y, Z, A, depth, beyond_reach) and `-robot.src` (KRL: $OUT[1] gripper, $OUT[2] spindle, $OUT[3] dowel driver, $BASE per station) in the bundle; placements beyond reach are flagged in both. QA `host` p49-1 (every member placed once, a drill and a dowel per dowelled pivot, 5 stations for a 6 m pavilion at 2.7 m reach, a KRL DEF ... END without NaN).
- **Pass 50, brick walls built as walls** (6 Nov): `S.brick.open` (0 to 0.5 of a brick) is an open (hit-and-miss) bond: in a course the bricks stand that gap apart (`gxo`), odd courses move half a pitch, and every course closes against a corner or an end with a closer (a single panel at both ends; a set at its corners and wall ends), so corners and ends stay solid piers and every brick bears at least half its length in all on the course below. A free-standing brick wall (a set not on a plan, not overhead) gets a stone coping (60 thick, 50 over each face; not under a vault) and, above 1.8 m, brick piers bonded in behind it at the ends and at most 3 m apart (headers and stretchers alternating; `userData.pier`). QA `brick` p50-1 (bearing, gaps, piers). Rendered: `brick50-walls.png`, `brick50-open-corner.png`.
- **Pass 51, the stacked timber's fasteners, and the Rhino round trip** (6 Nov; the user: "stacked pieces connected with dowels ... or an option for other types of wood fasteners"). `S.crib.fastener` 'dowel' (hardwood, end 2 mm proud) | 'screw' (fully threaded `screwD`, head on top, one per crossing; robot op `screw`, $OUT[4]) | 'rod' (threaded rods `rodD` up each pivot stack: `cribCourses` makes `rods` segments, each as long as every pivot stays within (w - rod)/2 - 3 of the segment's straight line, the next starting on the last course of the one before so every crossing is pinned; washer and hex nut at each end; robot drills each course h deep and threads the rod, $OUT[5]); schedules `-fasteners.csv` or `-rods.csv`. Rhino: `dev-tools/scripts/dumpcsv.py` writes a design's schedules headless; `grasshopper/timber_from_csv.py` (GhPython or a Rhino script) rebuilds members as solid boxes and dowels as cylinders from `-members.csv` and the fastener schedule; run through the Rhino MCP on 6 Nov: 750 members of one cut length (730) and 1,417 dowels in 0.9 s, 57 of 57 sampled dowels inside a member above and below; pictures `grasshopper/examples/timber-pavilion/timber-pavilion-rhino*.png`. Not yet: the opening framing (jambs, lintels) and the footing in the schedules, so Rhino shows the doorway unframed. QA `host` p51-1.
- **Pass 52, the timber framing in the schedules** (6 Nov): `cribFrameCSV()` writes `-frame.csv` (piece, kind: jamb, lintel, end post, cap ring, footing; a, b, width, depth; vertical pieces run a to b up their axis, horizontal ones along their length with the depth below the top face); `timber_from_csv.py` gains `build_frame()`; in Rhino (MCP, 6 Nov) the 42 pieces frame the doorway and ring the base: `grasshopper/examples/timber-pavilion/timber-pavilion-rhino-door.png`.
- **Pass 54, the timber pavilion as a field of curved walls; pass 53, every brick its own colour** (6 Nov; the user: "it can be a parametrically curved wall or a series of walls with gaps between that give the illusion of a closed space ... a curved wall connected together with wood elements and made by a kuka robot"). `S.crib.walls` (1 to 8), `gap` (deg; negative: neighbours overlap), `stagger` (mm alternate walls step out, so overlapping walls pass each other and leave passages), `turn`. `cribWallsOver()` gives each wall its arc, turn (`rotA`, read by `cribOutline`) and diameter; `cribEach(fn)` runs fn per wall with `CRIB_OVER` set (merged last in `cribCourses`); `cribGeometry` concatenates each wall's `cribGeometry1`; the schedules gain a wall column; one robot program per wall (`-wallN-robot.src`, DEF TIMBER_STACK_WN); `window.PS_CRIBS`. Compose's timber pavilion is a field of 3 to 5 walls 70 % of the time (gaps or overlaps with stagger). Brick: `brickGeometry` writes a per-brick tint (0.86 to 1.04, warmer or cooler, 1 in 18 a darker clinker, seeded by place) and `makeMaterialFor` turns on vertex colours for bricks; piers carry a neutral tint. QA `host` p54-1 (5 overlapping walls, one member length, no slips, every end supported). Rendered: `timber54-wallfield.png`, `brick53-tint.png`. Building the bundle for five walls is slow (each schedule re-solves each wall): a cache of `cribCourses` per wall is the next speed-up.
- **Pass 55, pavers as a module library** (6 Nov, Opus; the user: "more opportunities for unique designs with modular pavers rather than the pocket park and roof garden example ... a sub panel that has the modular elements and the types and they can choose"). Four layouts in `paverCells` before `hex`: `ashlar` (a multi-size set on a half-module grid u = L/2: each free cell takes the first module of a seeded order among 2x2, 3x2, 2x1, 1x2, 1x1 that fits), `planks` (rows L/ratio wide, lengths L, 1.5L or 2L by seed, no joint within L/4 of a joint in the row below), `radial` (rings of segments about the frame centre, each segment near the module length), `tri` (equilateral triangles). `PAVER_KINDS` (slab, hole, grass, seat, bed, groove, light) and `S.paver.kinds` (comma list, one per type; '' = all slabs): in `paverGenerate` each paver takes its type's kind; `hole` carries the largest planted hole the slab clears by 15 (`paverFits`, from 0.9 sqrt(area) down in 10s, at least 60); a `seat` is kept only with no kept seat within 1.5 modules (else a slab: a bench needs space round it); `res.paver.kinds` counts. `paverGeometry`: `bed` -> `gardenBed`; `grass` a concrete lattice (90 cells at 120) with a green fill below the top; `seat` at least 420 high; `groove` and `light` a strip along the longest edge. The Modules sub-panel (`.paver-kinds`, before Lay them): a row per type with its swatch and the kinds as chips. Compose's ground: 20 % roof garden, 20 % pocket park, else a modular ground (a seeded layout from ashlar, planks, radial, tri, herringbone, basket, hex, running; 3 to 5 types; a kind set; kerb, none or parapet). QA `paver` p55-1 (each new layout covers the inset frame by area, every kind present, seats 1.5 modules apart, planted holes).
- **Pass 56, serial ribs held as they are built** (6 Nov, Opus; the user: "develop serial selections more with realistic 3d forms and connections"). `S.serial.join` 'rods' (default) | 'rails' | 'none', `rodD` (16), `bay` (900, the most spacing of rods or rails along a rib), `blade` (150), `base` (true). In `serialGenerate`: the base channel (110) and cap take their height (`yb0`/`yb1`; the rib-count check now reads `Sr.fix.baseH`/`capH`); the shallowest depth is held (`dMinHeld`) to 2 edge + hole for rods (edge = max(20, 1.5 d), hole d + 2) or 30 + edge for a rail notch, and `Sr.dMin` is the held value (zero-waste pairs still sum to `dMin + dMax`). `Sr.fix`: stations at most `bay` apart (`e` = min(150, len/6) in from each end); rods: a back row at edge + hole/2, a front row where the shallowest rib at that station leaves edge + hole/2 in front and at least 2.5 holes of lever arm (`front`, `arm`, `need2` the depth that would give every station one), spacers pitch - t, nuts, couplers over 3 m; rails: 60 x 100 LVL, ribs notched 30 from the back, rails slotted 30 from the front (half-lap), `slots`; a twist is `blades` between posts (60 SHS, 6 gaps), each turned to the band's angle at its middle on a steel spine (OD max(2d, 27)) with a collar each side (`bladeN`, `root`). `serialGeometry` keeps the old ribbon only for join none; otherwise one geometry with `srole` (0 stock, 1 rail, 2 steel, 3 dark) and `color`, `serialRecolour` (also from `updateMaterial`), `makeMaterialFor` vertex colours with a white base. The nest: `serialNestMarks` gives holes (SVG `.serial-hole`, DXF CIRCLE on HOLES; a complement's holes from the strip's other edge), notches (`.serial-notch`, NOTCHES) and blade ends (BLADES). `serialFixText`, `serialFixCSV` (`-fixings.csv`: rods with x and z, spacers, nuts, couplers, holes, base channel, plates, cap; rails, notches, slots, shoes, head rail; spines, posts, collars and one row per blade with its angle). Controls in Stock: 'What holds them' (Held by is a primary control; a fold for the rest). QA `serial` p56-1. Rendered: `serial56-joins.png`.
- **Pass 57, a pavilion of serial timber portal frames** (6 Nov, Opus; the user: "develop the wood assemblies ... inspired by these case studies [wood precedents] ... without copying the designs but synthesizing the logic"; `portal57.js` before `CRIB_DEF`). `S.portal` (`PORTAL_DEF` {on, kind 'gable' | 'arch' | 'shed', path 'line' | 'arc', len, bend (deg), pitch, span, h, vary, varyBy 'height' | 'span' | 'both', dMin, dMax, chordW 45, chordD 195, webW 45, webD 95, purlin, skin 'roof' | 'all' | 'none', deck, brace, footing, stock 4800}; in the default state, recipes, snapshots and Compose). `portalFrames()`: one frame per pitch along the path (arc: radial frames), span and height from the field at (u along, .5); knee depth held to at least span/14 (`held`), foot/crown to span/35. `portalProfile` gives the outer line's nodes (each straight run in panels near the mean depth, an arch resampled by arc length), corners, knees (a gable's ridge is a joint, not a knee) and mitred inward normals; depth is dMin + (dMax - dMin) x a bump at each knee (width .11 of the line). `portalModel()`: chords in two leaves either side of the web, pieces two panels long, the leaves' splices a panel apart (`cuts`: a corner ends a piece in both, and the second leaf restarts with one panel to keep the stagger); a post at every node deeper than the chord, a diagonal in every panel (up toward the crown), a second diagonal half-lapped where the panel is deeper than half the range (`lap`); a bolt at every node of both chords; plywood gussets either side at every corner; feet level on steel shoes (plate, two side plates) on pad footings; purlins 45 x 145 on edge on the outer face at the same share of the line between frames (roof only above half height unless skin 'all'); steel cross-bracing rods in the first, last and every fifth bay between brace lines; a translucent skin; a slatted deck on two bearers between the legs. `portalBox(a,b,w,d,dir,T,o)` is a box along a member, depth along `dir`. Meshes by role (`userData.portal`); `window.PS_PORTAL` holds the record (counts, distinct lengths, longest, stockOver, web slenderness, spans, heights, knee depths, laps); `.portal-note` under Host surface; `-portals.csv` (member, kind, frame or bay, length, section, ends, leaf; brace rods). Compose: 'timber portals' (single-curved-loft hosts lead with it). A panel tab click sets it aside like the other forms. QA `host` p57-1 (gable, arch on a 90 deg arc, mono-pitch: frames per pitch, knees at least span/14, no node spliced in both leaves, a diagonal per panel, members within stock, web slenderness under 50, purlins per bay, bolts per node, three gussets per gable frame, schedule rows). Rendered: `portal57-pavilions.png`.
- **Pass 58, a timber canopy on clustered posts** (6 Nov, Opus; the wood precedents' clustered posts, built-up two-way grid and field-driven studs under a draped skin, synthesised; `canopy58.js` before `CRIB_DEF`). `S.canopy` (`CANOPY_DEF` {on, w, d, bay, clear, postW 89, gap 38, boardT 38, beamD 286, stud 400, step 50, hMin, hMax, net, skin, brace, footing, stock 6096 (20 ft 2x12)}). `canopyModel()`: a column per grid node of four posts round a cross of spacer blocks bolted every 900; beams of two boards either side of the column (gap = column width `cw` = 2 postW + gap) with blocks between them, the lower beams along x on the columns, the upper ones along z on top of them at every half bay (`lines`), pieces two bays long with the two boards' splices a bay apart and every splice over a column (end overhang 150); beam depth held to at least bay/10 (`held`); knee braces at 45 deg, 700 out, from each column head toward each neighbour (x to the lower beam, z to the upper); red brackets where an upper beam crosses a lower one between columns; studs (38 x 89) in the upper beams' gap every `stud` mm, each top at y3 + hMin + (hMax - hMin) x the field at (u, v), rounded to `step` so the saw cuts batches; a noggin between neighbouring studs standing over 1200 free; a cable net through the stud tops both ways and a translucent skin on it; base plates and pad footings. `window.PS_CANOPY` (counts, stud lengths, column and stud slenderness, cable metres); `.canopy-note`; `-canopy.csv`. Compose: 'timber canopy' (double-curved and ground hosts). QA `host` p58-1. Rendered: `canopy58-fields.png`.
- **Pass 59, a corbelled timber corridor** (6 Nov, Opus; the wood precedents' post-and-through-block corridor with a corbelled roof, synthesised; `corbel59.js` before `CRIB_DEF`). `S.corbel` (`CORBEL_DEF` {on, path 'line' | 'arc', len, bend, pitch, width, h, vary, post 140, bw 89, bh 89, blockL 600, step 150, railGap 600, floor, footing}). `corbelModel()`: a pair of posts per pitch along the path (heights from the field at u), on plates and pad footings; wall rails through mortises in the posts every `railGap`, neighbouring bays offset half a gap so a post is never cut twice at one height; a top plate along the posts; then courses alternating across and along: cantilever c at each frame from r0 - c p - bw/2 out by the block length (it projects exactly one step past the inner face of the rail below and keeps the rest of its length behind it), rail c along on the cantilevers' inner ends at r0 - c p; the step is held to a third of the block (`held`); `nC` = ceil((r0 - bw)/p) courses until the sides meet, a ridge tie across at each frame and a ridge block along. Every cantilever is one length; rails one length on a level straight path. A floor of bearers across and slats on edge along with 12 mm gaps. `window.PS_CORBEL` (counts, lengths, mortises, ridge heights, bolts); `.corbel-note`; `-corbel.csv`. Compose: 'timber corridor' (single-curved-loft and site-polygon hosts). QA `host` p59-1 (straight and 120 deg arc: step within a third, one cantilever length, the sides meet, each cantilever one step past the rail below, no post mortised twice at one height, ridge per bay, schedule). Rendered: `corbel59-corridors.png`.
- **Passes 60 and 61, the whole-form picker and a leaning timber wall** (6 Nov, Opus). Pass 60: at the head of Host surface (3D) a `.form-pick` row of `.form-chip` buttons (`data-form`: none, stack, vox, dome, crib, portal, canopy, corbel, lean, loft) turns one form on and the others off; a binding hides every control whose `data-path` starts with another form's key (and the chosen form's own `.on` switch) and every other form's `.X-note`, and says in a line what the chosen form is. No control or state key removed. QA `flow` p60-1. Pass 61 (`lean61.js` before `CRIB_DEF`; the wood precedents' curved crib wall leaning into a canopy, synthesised): `S.lean` (`LEAN_DEF` {on, a, b (plan half-axes), arc, turn, height, mw 89, mh 38 (members laid flat), gap 180, node 600, lean 1200, pow 1.6, vary .5, bench, deck, anchors}). `leanModel()`: nodes along the ellipse arc by arc length with inward normals; each node's lean at course k is reach x (k/K)^pow, reach from the field at u; the steepest step (at the top) is held to a third of a member by scaling the reach (`reachMax`, `held`), so the profile keeps its shape; two layers `gap` apart, members two node-panels long in running bond (alternate courses start a panel along), spacers between the layers at alternating nodes course by course (never one on another), a dowel through each into the course below; ground anchors at every other node of the base; a bench (seat 435, 420 deep, legs) along the inside of the foot; a deck of radial slats from a hub on two ring joists; `lean0` (mean lean of the wall's mass) against the base depth is reported, and the note says the wall relies on its plan curve and anchors when the mass sits past the base. `window.PS_LEAN`; `.lean-note`; `-leanwall.csv`; Compose 'leaning timber wall' (revolved hosts). QA `host` p61-1 (no step over a third, no joint over a joint, spacers alternate, the field changes the top lean, schedule). Rendered: `lean61-walls.png`.
- **Pass 62, the saw's cut list for the timber forms** (6 Nov, Opus; `cut62.js` before `CRIB_DEF`). `timberCut(mem, stock, kerf 4)`: every piece grouped by section (smaller x larger, rounded) and length (to the mm), packed per section by first-fit decreasing with the kerf into standard lengths (8, 10, 12, 14, 16, 20 ft up to the form's stock), trying each single length and the shortest-that-fits mix and keeping the one that buys least timber (pieces over the longest counted as `over`, not packed); per section the boards by length (`by`); returns rows {sec, len, n}, per-section boards, distinct lengths and offcut %, and totals. `timberCutFor(kind)` for 'portal' (its `stock`), 'canopy' (6096), 'corbel' (4800), 'lean' (4800, members and the spacer blocks); `timberCutText` ends each form's note; `timberCutCSV` is `-cutlist.csv` in the bundle (the cut rows, then a per-section summary with the boards to buy). The leaning wall's spacer is now a block in the gap only (it had been drawn across both layers). QA `host` p62-1.
- **Pass 63, the robot for the timber forms** (6 Nov, Opus; precedent 2's pick, cut, place, generalised; `robot63.js` before `CRIB_DEF`). `timberRobot(kind)` for 'lean' and 'corbel' (stacked in place: the arm on a lift at mid-height of the work (`lift`, half the highest piece less 300, in 50s), stations along the path as far apart as it reaches at that height (at most 1.4 x `S.fab.reach`, else 1.6 x the horizontal reach left), the lean wall's base 0.45 x reach inside the wall line, the corridor's on its centre line; each piece to its nearest station) and 'portal' (each frame assembled flat on a table, a station per frame: back leaves, web, front leaves, then drilled through at the nodes; purlins, braces and skin are site work). Pieces sorted by course (lean: course, members before spacers; corridor: posts, wall rails by height, top plates, cantilever and rail courses, ridge ties, ridge, floor; portal: frame then layer), station, then along. Rows: cut (at the saw, to length), place (X, Y, Z in the station's base, A the yaw, B the pitch; posts stood at B 90), then the fixings (dowel or anchor, peg through a mortise, bolt, screw, shoe, drill) with depths; placements beyond the reach are flagged. `-robot.csv` (op, piece, kind, course, station, length, section, X, Y, Z, A, B, depth, beyond_reach) and `-robot.src` (KRL: DEF TIMBER_<KIND>, INI fold, $TOOL, XHOME, a $BASE per station or frame, picks under $NULLFRAME at `S.fab.pick`, $OUT[1] gripper, [2] spindle, [3] dowel or peg driver, [4] screwdriver) in the bundle; a robot line ends the lean, corridor and portal notes. QA `host` p63-1 (every piece cut once and placed once, never before its course below, fixing rows counted, a whole KRL without NaN, a base per station, beyond-reach flags consistent).
- **Pass 64, the timber forms in Rhino** (6 Nov, Opus). The four schedules carry what a rebuild needs: `-portals.csv`, `-canopy.csv` and `-corbel.csv` end with `dx,dy,dz` (each piece's depth direction, unit, square to it) and `ox,oy,oz` (the leaf offset: a portal chord's half web plus half chord along the path; 0 elsewhere); `-leanwall.csv` adds `section_mm,dx,dy,dz` and its spacer rows their two ends across the gap; the canopy's stud note uses `;` (its comma had made those rows one column too wide); brace rods carry 'rod 16'. `grasshopper/timber_frames_from_csv.py` (GhPython or a Rhino 8 script, IronPython 2 and CPython 3) builds a box per row (tool (x, y, z) -> Rhino (x, -z, y)), rods as lines; embedded in `GH_SCRIPTS` (7 readers) and put in the bundle beside the schedule when a timber form is on. Run through the Rhino MCP on 6 Nov from schedules dumped headless (`grasshopper/examples/timber-forms/*.csv`): 1,081 portal pieces and 24 rods, 673 canopy, 930 corridor and 2,982 leaning-wall pieces, every one a closed solid, in 1.9 s; the viewport picture was not captured (CaptureToBitmap returned nothing that evening), so the Rhino result is checked by count, not by eye. QA `host` p64-1.
- **Pass 65, the portal frames analysed** (6 Nov, Opus; `truss65.js` before `CRIB_DEF`). `portalTrussFrame(F,K)`: each frame a pin-jointed plane truss by direct stiffness (outer and inner chord nodes; chords of two leaves, area 2 w d; posts and diagonals as the model builds them; both chords' feet pinned); loads on the frame's tributary pitch: dead 0.35 kPa on the outer line plus self weight (C24, 420 kg/m3), snow 1.4 kPa on the roof's plan projection (outer segments above 0.45 h), wind 0.6 kPa on the windward (x < 0) vertical projection; combinations 1.25D + 1.5S + 0.4W, 1.25D + 1.4W + 0.5S, 1.4D; Gaussian elimination with partial pivoting (a singular matrix = a mechanism, `ok:false`); member force, utilisation against C24 design strengths (k_mod .8, gamma_M 1.3: tension 8.9, compression 12.9 MPa) with EN 1995's k_c (beta_c .2, E0.05 7400) on the thin side of webs and the depth of chords; the service deflection (D + S) against span/300; an equilibrium check (member forces at the supports against the loads on free nodes). `portalTruss()` (`TRUSS_C24` holds the values), `portalTrussText` ends the portal note, `portalTrussCSV` is `-portal-analysis.csv`. The analysis found that panels sized to the mean lattice depth made the shallow crown flexible (a deeper knee had increased the deflection); `portalFrames` now sizes panels to 1.8 x the shallowest depth (420 to 1000). QA `host` p65-1 (every frame solves, reactions balance to 1e-13, a 1400 knee instead of 900 takes the deflection from 2.3 to 0.65 mm; the worst member is a diagonal at 45 %).
- **Pass 66, the portals and the corridor made parametric** (6 Nov, Opus; the user: they "look too similar to precedents ... make them all parametric"). Portals: `ridge` (the apex off the centre as a share of the half span; an arch is warped x + r s/2 (1 - (2x/s)^2), feet fixed), `ridgeVary` (the field at (u, .85) moves it frame by frame, held within 0.7), `twist` (deg end to end: each frame turned about the vertical by twist x (u - .5), so T and A turn and the roof between frames is a ruled, twisting surface); the knees are now the eaves by arc length (`L[1]/tot`, `L[3]/tot`; mono-pitch `L[1]`, `L[2]`), so a moved ridge never becomes a knee. Defaults ridgeVary .6, twist 35. Corridor: `swell` (the field at (u, .2) sets each frame's clear width, `F.W`, `F.r0`) and `meander` (the field at (u, .8) sets each frame's meeting line `F.xm`, within .45 of the half width); one course count `nC` for all frames (the largest needed at the step asked), each side's step `F.pL` = (r0 + xm - bw/2)/nC and `F.pR` = (r0 - xm - bw/2)/nC so the two sides' last cantilevers meet on the line; rails for courses below nC; the ridge tie and ridge follow `xm`; posts, wall rails, top plates, bearers and slats follow each frame's width. Defaults swell .35, meander .6. `corbelVaryText` in the note. Fixes found by the checks: the Rhino depth direction is squared to each piece (a purlin between twisted frames runs skew), and a robot station no piece is nearest to is dropped and the rest renumbered. QA `host` p59-1 measures each cantilever's projection by its own side's step. Rendered: `timber66-param.png`.
- **Passes 67 and 68, the interface: a step bar and matching rail** (6 Nov, Opus; UI-REDESIGN-PLAN-2026-11-06 with the user's go-ahead "do the ui design"). The hidden `#flow` bar is now `#flow.v2`, a sticky two-by-two block of step cards at the head of the control column (`flowVals()`, called from `flowMark` and from a binding): 1 Host (the whole form if one is on, else the surface (Flat / Cylinder / Ruled wave / Sphere) or the plan, and the panel count), 2 Category (the panel kind's tab; 'set aside' when a whole form is on), 3 Finish (material and gauge), 4 Deliver (what the bundle carries: cut list, robot, Rhino for the timber forms; schedule, robot, Rhino for brick, pavers, kinetic; DXF, SVG, STL otherwise); the current step in its colour (Host slate #5B6B7A, Category brick #B5533C, Finish timber #B9874B, Deliver steel #5E7686), hairline borders, 6 px radius, step names in DM Mono, values in Instrument Sans with ellipsis and a title carrying the whole value, motion off under reduced motion. Pass 68: the nav rail's groups are named Host, Category, Finish, Deliver (the `FLOW` labels). No control, id, data-path or tab name changed; QA `layout` and `flow` green.
- **Pass 69, the 2D view for a whole form** (6 Nov, Opus; the photo set had shown a panel's cut pattern in 2D beside a timber pavilion in 3D). `FORM_SHEET` lists the whole forms (portal, canopy, corbel, lean, crib, stack, vox, dome, loft) with their names and bundle files; `formSheetKey()` is the one that is on (not overhead); `render2D` opens with it and draws `formSheetSVG(f)`: a sheet with the form's name, its own note split into sentences and wrapped (what it is made of, the cut list, the analysis, the robot), and the bundle's files at the foot; the 2D hint reads '<form> · the form in numbers'. Until the 3D build has written the note the sheet says so and redraws itself every 500 ms, at most 12 times (`FORM_SHEET.tries`). An SVG, so Save image works as before. Photo: `review/examples-2026-11-06/ui70-stepbar-formsheet.png`.
- **Passes 70 and 71, the tool in order of development; the paver module bar** (10 Nov, Opus; the user's review of v71 and `knowledge/PLAN-2026-11-10.md`). Pass 70: `START_CASES` (brick wall, brick corner, leaning timber wall, stacked timber pavilion, serial rib screen, perforated screen as Developed; brick tower and modular pavers as Developing) are cards at the head of Start (`.scase`, `startCase(id)` after `startCaseReset()`: every whole form off, plan and host back to their defaults, the ground left, a noise field); `CMP_ORDER` groups Compose's list (stacked walls, corners and towers; grounds; other forms; 'Experimental, not yet engineered') with the wall selected (`cmpOptions()`); the timber portals, canopy and corridor carry `exp:true` and `cmpForm` never picks an experimental or draft form itself; the form picker leads with stacked timber and the leaning wall and puts the three experimental forms (dashed, `.exp`) after a label. QA `flow` p70-1. Pass 71 (`pmod71.js` before `CRIB_DEF`; logic in `knowledge/flux-logic/modular-grounds.md`): `paverModBar()` heads the Pavers tab: THE FAMILY (`paverFamily(P)`: every module the layout lays, to one scale and dimensioned: ashlar's five half-module sizes, planks' three lengths, the single module of square, running, stack, herringbone and basket, triangle, hexagon, Cairo, ring segment, Voronoi cell, column ranges, nested A/B and sub-heights) with its own Module, Length : width and Joint sliders; THE SET (`paverSet(P)`: the resolved types darkest first as {colour, height, kind, name}; a card per type drawn in its colour with its kind marked by `paverKindMark`, its count in the design, and colour, kind, height and name inputs; `paverSetWrite(recs)` re-sorts by tone and writes palette, heights, kinds and names together, so they stay on one type; a stepper for 2 to 8 types); THE RULES (`paverRules(P)`: herringbone or basketweave under 2:1 refused, planks under 3:1 warned, planted holes on modules under 300, grass under 240, seats under 300, grooves and light lines on square modules, radial on small grounds, ashlar's no-cut statement, over five types). The pass-55 kinds rows are hidden (the cards replace them); `paverStats` redraws the bar; the step bar's Host says 'Ground · W x H m' for pavers on the ground. QA `paver` p71-1. Photos: `review/examples-2026-11-06/ui72-start-cases.png`, `ui73-paver-module-bar.png`.
- **Pass 72, the Pavers tab holds only paving; the permeable family designed, not yet built** (10 Nov, Fable; the user: the panel "should be focused on developing the best pavers ... it shouldn't include options for timber towers"). In the Pavers tab only (a block before the ratio binding, class `.pv-off` = display none, never the `hidden` class other code toggles): always off: the Assembly (3D), Host surface (3D) and Frame & edges folds, and the `element`, `arr`, `orient`, `clip` controls; off unless the design in hand uses them (`window.__pvSweep`, a binding): `paver.lay` (wall), `paver.pots`/`stem`/`flare`, `paver.tilt`, the Nested modules and Point field folds and their two Pattern buttons, and the `floors` and `sun` field buttons while in paver mode. The size fold reads 'Ground size' with 'Ground width' and 'Ground depth'. Nothing leaves the state, and the other tabs keep every control; `startCase` opens a tab whose form picker is not `.pv-off`. QA `paver` p72-1. The next paver design is in `knowledge/flux-logic/modular-grounds.md` (the shared-foot permeable family: one lattice, one interlocking foot, a family of tops; open share 1 - (d/p)^2; four ways the top varies: shrink, round, turn, slot; route closed to 13 mm; water = open share x the fill's rate against the storm) and tried in a standalone mockup, `review/paver-panel-mockup.html`, published at https://claude.ai/artifact/1EpTVJ2VTTFCHHHfhRWsEr (five steps: Ground, Modules, Pattern, Water, Deliver; a live canvas plan; three starting designs). The mockup is NOT wired to the tool: `paverCells` has no two-level stone yet.
- **Pass 73, the permeable family and the Pavers tab as five steps** (10 Nov, Fable; the user approved the mockup: "Yes, build it"; logic in `knowledge/flux-logic/modular-grounds.md`). Engine (`perm73.js` before `paverGenerate`): `S.paver.tiling='perm'` (first in `PAVER_TILINGS`) with `pLat` 'square' | 'hex' | 'brick', `pMorph` 'shrink' | 'round' | 'turn' | 'slot' (`PERM_FOR` says which a lattice allows; `permMorph` falls back), `openMin`, `openMax`, `pFill` 'auto' | 'gravel' | 'grass', `grassFrom`, `route` (a preset that writes `keep` through `permRoute()`), `routeOn`, `seats`, `vehicles`, and for every ground `storm`, `runon`, `rGravel`, `rGrass`, `rSand`, `base`, `sub`. `permCells` lays WHOLE stones only (an interlocking stone is not cut), centred in the frame (`res.paver.margin` is the strip left; the spin is ignored, the joint is 0). Types are assigned as always; `permApply` then closes the route (stones within `keepW` of a kept path take the lightest type) and the ground under benches, and gives each stone `cell` (its foot, the lattice cell), `pts` (its top), `open`, `fillK`, `grade` (the LIGHTEST type is G1, the closed grade), kind slab, no hole; `res.paver.perm` {lattice, morph, pitch, fam, seats, routeGap, closed, held}. `permTop(P,o)` makes the top for an open share exactly (shrink: scaled; round: corners rounded, the joint narrowed when 6 mm would already be more open than asked; turn: sized by the share, then turned as far as the cell allows; slot: narrowed across), held at 0.6 of the cell. `paverWater(res,P)` runs for every layout (perm: open x cell; others: joints, planted holes, beds, 40 % of a grass grid, culled cells): open, intake = sum(open area x the fill's rate)/area, need = storm x (1 + runon), ok, store = base x 0.35, mins. 2D: the cell in its fill's colour under the top, benches; the setting-out adds the lattice (`.paver-cells`) and labels grades. 3D (`permStone3D`): foot (half height, darker), fill, top; over 2,500 stones no foot; benches; a gravel slab to the frame for the edge course. `-grades.csv` (`permGradesCSV`); the README names the source of the rule. The module bar draws each grade on its wavy foot (`permBarSVG`, `permBarAfter`: Stone spacing 150 to 900, cards closed grade first, fill named in place of the kind) and `permRules` speaks (no cuts, an unusable variation, a held grade, a route over 13 mm, the water verdict). Choosing the family by hand (`permStart`, on the Pattern button's click and in the Start case) sets concrete tones, 4 grades, dither, a winding route and an attractor-curve field along it. Interface (`pvsteps73.js`): `.pvsteps` nav (Ground, Modules, Pattern, Water, Deliver) before the tab's body, a line per step, Back / Next; every control in the tab is tagged `data-pvstep` (0 = replaced by the bar) and `.pvs-off` hides the other steps; new blocks: the ground (size, route, half width, closed switch, benches, vehicles, edge, the build switch), the layout with the family's controls, the fill, the storm with a readout and a fold of rates, the order (a grade or type table, fill volumes, the bundle button). `window.__pvStep(k)`, `window.__pvPanel()`. QA `paver` p73-1 and p73-2; p71-1 restated (the bar is in the tab, no longer first). Rendered: `perm73-lattices.png`, `perm73-close.png`.
- **Pass 74, the paved ground falls; the section; the layout dropdown; a card for the precedent** (10 Nov, Fable; the user: "Finish pavers", then a photograph: "the error message doesn't fit within the panel ui, and some pavers are being cut off in the family"; `fall74.js` before `paverGenerate`). Two faults: a rule in the module bar was a `<p class="warn">`, and the tool's global `.warn` is `position:absolute` (the stage warning), so it floated over the panel: rule classes are now `pr-bad` / `pr-warn` / `pr-ok` / `pr-note` and the water verdict `pvn-ok` / `pvn-bad` (never a bare `warn`, `ok`, `bad` or `note` class inside a panel); the family drawing clipped the ring segment and long labels: the segment is drawn inside its box and labels are clamped by text width. New state `S.paver.fall` (0 to 5 %, default 1.5) and `fallTo` 'N' | 'E' | 'S' | 'W' (the low edge in plan). `paverCascade(res,P,w,x0,y0,x1,y1)` runs after `paverWater`: the ground in bands across the fall, each band's rain plus what the band above could not take against its own intake, so open ground downhill does more than the same ground uphill; `w.taken`, `w.edge` (mm/h reaching the low edge), `w.edgeLs` (L/s for the drain), `w.ok = edge < 0.5`, `w.fall`, `w.fallTo`; a level ground (fall 0) uses the average as before. `permRot(P)`: a brick lattice with slotted tops turns a quarter when the fall runs up or down the plan, so slots run with the water; grooves run with the fall. `paverFallSVG` (class `.paver-fall`, an arrow and the percentage, a blue line on the low edge), `paverDrain3D` (one channel strip, 36 vertices), `paverSectionSVG(Gk)` (stone on its foot, bedding grit 30, stone base at `base` with what it stores, geotextile, subgrade; drawn in the Water step in `.pvsec` and written to the bundle as `-section.svg`), a fall row in `-grades.csv`. Panel: the layout is a grouped `<select class="pv-laysel">` (Interlocking, permeable / Rectangular / Other shapes; a facade layout only while a design uses it) that clicks the hidden segment; `.pvchips` 'Start from' (`PERM_DESIGNS`, `permDesign(k)`: Rounding squares, Pinwheel court of five grades, Slot bond); the Voronoi fold shows only for a Voronoi layout; the run-on slider steps by 0.05. Compose: about a quarter of grounds are permeable (`permDesign` with a seeded size, open share and lattice); a library what-if is applied only when the control offers the value (a card's 'Periodic' had been written into `paver.tiling` and the ground came out empty). Catalogue: card 46, `flyt-permeable-paving` (Snohetta's three stones on one foot, taxonomy not-in-flux, read from the text and three images the user supplied, kept in `catalogue/sources/flyt-2026-11-10/`; the 283 mm lattice spacing is inferred); its recipe loads the tool's OWN family on the same rule, not the collection, and the card's note says so. Pass 74c (`stage74c-ground.py`): a user's click on the Pavers tab over a set of wall panels (the opening design is five 900 x 2400 panels, and paving came out as five strips) makes ONE ground: `PAVER_SET` keeps [arr, cols, rows, width, height], the arrangement goes single and a ground under 3 m a side becomes 9 x 6 m, with a toast; a click on another panel tab brings the set back; `startCaseReset` and the QA reset clear it. `T.showTab` is exposed to the harness. QA `paver` p74-1 and p74-2. Photos: `review/examples-2026-11-06/ui76-paver-rules-in-panel.png`, `ui76-paver-water-section.png`, `ui76-paver-card-loaded.png`.
- **Pass 75, the Pavers tab made easier to work** (10 Nov, Fable; the user: "remove the fall 1.5 % and arrow in blue over the entire design. It can be smaller and included in the title"; "allow you to change the size of the modules parametrically and by ... entering new numbers"; "changing route across it doesn't update the paver tool 2d and 3d panels"; `pmod75.js` replaces `paverModBar`). The fall: `paverFallSVG` draws only the drain line on the low edge (and, on the setting-out, the route's centre line dashed, `.paver-route`); `paverFallTitle()` adds an arrow toward the low edge and the percentage to the 2D title (`hint2d`: 'Pavers · plan · ↓ 1.5 %'), nothing when level. Sizes: `paverDims(P)` lists the family's dimensions for the layout (Length / Width / Depth / Joint; Unit for ashlar; Stone spacing for the permeable family; Side, Across flats, Cell, About for the single-module shapes), each a `.pmod-row` of a full-width slider and a typed number in step; `paverDimSet(P,k,v)` writes them: length keeps the width (`ratio = size / width`, any number to 3 places, held to a whole number for herringbone and basketweave by `paverIntRatio`), `depth` sets every type that shares the commonest depth (`paverDepth`; a seat keeps its own) and `S.thickness`. Multi-size sets: `S.paver.ashlarSet` ('' = all; a list of 2x2, 3x2, 2x1, 1x2, 1x1; the single unit always stays, `paverAshlarAllow`) through `.pmod-chip` toggles, and `S.paver.plankSet` ('' = 1, 1.5, 2 times the module, `paverPlankMults`) through three typed lengths in mm; `paverCells` and `paverFamily` read both. The bar is built in parts (`.pmod-famw`, `.pmod-dims`, `.pmod-set`, `.pmod-rules`) with one set of listeners; while a size control is in use (`host.__busy`, or the focused element) its block is brought up to date, never replaced, so a slider survives its own drag; sizes apply on `input` (live), `refreshAll` on `change`. The route: on every layout that is not the permeable family, pavers within the half width of a preset or typed route take the lightest type as plain slabs (`p.route`, `res.paver.routeN`; kinds and holes cleared), switched by `routeOn`, whose switch now shows for any layout with a route; `paverRouteSet()` (the dropdown's handler) also moves an attractor-curve field that was following the old route onto the new one. Before this a route changed nothing on an ordinary layout, and on the permeable family the pattern kept following the old route. Tool-wide: every slider's value can be clicked and typed (`range()`: `.val` opens an `input.valedit`; Enter or blur commits, held to the slider's range; Escape cancels). A ground made from wall panels starts with a 400 x 200 paver (the opening design's 120 x 60 gave 7,425 pavers). QA `paver` p75-1 and p75-2; judged by hand in the browser pane (drag, type, route, click-to-type).
- **Pass 77, the brick wall and its corners built as a bricklayer builds them** (10 Nov, Fable; the user: "make sure the brick connections are realistic and follow brick wall construction logic ... before moving onto corners. then make sure corners are well constructed"; `brick77.js`, `brick77b.js` before `brickGenerate`). Found by close-up renders and by measuring laps: on alternate sides of every plan corner the joints lined up course over course (lap 0 to 10 mm: the half bat added on odd courses cancelled the offset the quoin already gives), a cut brick stood at each quoin, the butting course touched the running one with no joint, the wall floated above the ground by its vertical centring, and joints were empty gaps. Now: (1) **mortar** (`brickMortar(Gk)`, a child mesh of each panel mesh, `userData.mortar`, `brickMortarMat`, not on a curved host): a bed under every brick only over the runs of brick it sits on (an open bond has mortar where it bears), turning with a turned brick and no wider than it (nothing shows in the gaps a turned wall opens), a head joint between two bricks a joint apart that are not turned, carried down through the bed, the collar joint behind the stretchers of a one-brick wall, the joint at a quoin, the joint to the next panel of a flush side (`flushNext`), a bed for the coping (`capBed`); recessed min(4, 0.35 joint) from both faces. `rebuildScene` disposes children. (2) **Both-ends setting-out** on a straight wall: `brickCourseSym` lays Flemish, English and header courses from both ends toward the middle, so both stopped ends match (stretcher, or header and queen closer) and any cut brick falls mid-wall; a Flemish wall takes a whole number of its repeat ((n - 1) % 3 == 0 stretcher modules) when the brick bonds, so its stretcher courses end in stretchers. (3) **The wall stands on the ground**: `gy = y0` (no vertical centring), brick mode sets the frame margins to 0, `G.brick.gy`, `top`, `x0`, `x1`. (4) **Coping and piers** (`brickWallParts`): the coping is bedded a joint above the top course and is as long as the bricks (a single wall); piers are one-brick square piers in English bond (`brickPier`: two bricks side by side running back, then two running along), coursed with the wall, with mortar inside and a joint to the wall, capped level with the coping; a wall on an OPEN plan (corner, U, curve) is coped side by side. (5) **Corners** (`brickSideSeq(a0,a1,qL,qR,bL,j,prev)` in the `PLAN_SIDE` branch, `sidePrev` = the joints of the course below): at a square corner the course runs through (starts a width before the corner) or butts (starts a JOINT after it: `a0 = j`, `a1 = len - j`), by turns, and that alone is the half-brick lap; between two quoins the bricks are whole and the upright joints are gauged within 3 mm of the joint asked for; only if that cannot close the course is one brick cut, placed where it keeps the best lap over the course below and never beside a quoin; from a quoin to a free end the bricks are whole from the quoin and the cut stands at the free end. Measured after: brick corner and 4-sided tower, every side 124 to 125 mm lap, 0 straight joints, no cut bricks on the tower, half bats only at the L's free ends. Mitred (non-square) corners still meet on the bisector with a straight joint up the arris: not yet bonded. The Start case Brick wall is one wall 4760 x 2100. QA `brick` p77-1; two checks restated with reason (the corner check expects the butting course a joint short; the wave-wall base check now measures a base that stands on the ground). Rendered and looked at: `review/examples-2026-11-06/brick78-face-close.png`, `brick78-wall-mortar-piers.png`, `brick78-quoin-close.png`, `brick78-corner-coping.png`. Not yet: Flemish, English and header bond through a corner (they fall back to running bond on a plan), obtuse corners bonded with alternately lapping cut bricks, piers toothed into the wall, a footing, openings with lintels, movement joints.
- **Pass 76, stacked walls: the brick ladder, rungs 1 to 3 for a straight wall** (10 Nov, Fable; the user: "after this is done, work on the stacked walls"; `brick76.js` before `brickGenerate`; plan in `knowledge/PLAN-2026-11-10.md`). Rung 1, the unit: `brickUnitBar()` heads Brick courses (`.bbar`, mounted before the picture block; `window.__bbarDraw`, redrawn from `brickStats`): the brick to scale as face, end and bed; `BRICK_FORMATS` chips (NF, DF, UK, US modular); Length / Width / Height / Joint as `.pmod-row` sliders with typed numbers, live; the controls it replaces carry `.bu-off`. Rung 2, the bond: `S.brick.bond` gains `third`, `flemish`, `english`, `header` (`BRICK_BONDS`); `brickCourse(bond,r,a,b,bL,bW,j)` lays one course as cells [centre, length, , , kind] with kind 's' or 'h': a header course starts with a header and a queen closer ((bW - joint) / 2) so its joints fall a quarter brick from those below, a header-bond course with a three-quarter bat, third lap steps (bL + joint) / 3 a course, and whatever is left at the far end is a cut brick (or the last brick split in two bats when under 30 mm is left). `brickThickBond(b)`: Flemish, English and header bond are one brick thick: a header is a brick with `kind:'h'`, `dep = bL`; `G.backs` is the course behind the stretchers (shift -(bW + joint)); `G.brick.thick` (bL or bW), `headers`, `bondLaid`. In those bonds a brick is never turned (the course behind is in the way, `rotHeld`) and the field only pushes it OUT (shift >= 0). The new bonds are for a straight wall: on a plan with corners, a wave or an open bond the wall is laid in running bond and `G.brick.bondHeld` says so (rung 4, the corner, is next). `brickLaps(bricks,j)` measures every vertical joint against the course below: `G.brick.minLap` and `straight` (joints within 10 mm of one below). The bar draws six courses of the chosen bond from `brickCourse` itself (headers paler) and speaks in rules (`pr-*`): whether the brick bonds (2 widths + joint = length; a 240 x 140 brick in Flemish bond is caught), the smallest lap against a quarter brick, a straight joint, stack bond's lack of lap, bricks per square metre. Rung 3, the wall in numbers: `brickStand(tk,rho)` = rho g t^2 / (1.5 x 600 Pa), the height a plain wall stands by its own weight in a 0.6 kPa wind with a safety of 1.5 (0.27 m half-brick, 1.19 m one-brick at 1900 kg/m3; a first check, said as such); the pass-50 coping and bonded piers are now `brickWallParts(TW)` inside `rebuildScene`, built for a SINGLE straight wall too (flat host, no mirror), the coping over the whole thickness, the piers behind the back course, and piers appear when the wall is taller than it stands alone (at least 450). `brickGeometry`, `brickStats` (count and weight), `robotUnits` (a whole header is placed as a brick turned 90) and `brickCSV` take the back course and the headers' depth; the schedule gains two last columns, `depth_mm` and `laid` (stretcher, bat, header, closer, back); `grasshopper/bricks_from_csv.py` reads `depth_mm` (parsed, not run in Rhino). QA `brick` p76-1 and p76-2; the CSV header check restated. Rendered and looked at: `review/examples-2026-11-06/brick78-bonds.png`, `brick78-wall-coping-piers.png`, `ui78-brick-unit-bar.png`. Not yet: the same bonds through a corner (quoins for a one-brick wall), openings with lintels, a footing, a curved or serpentine free-standing wall checked for stability, the timber ladder (the unit and dowel bar for stacked timber).
- **Pass 78, the logic of three parametric brick sources in the brick panel** (10 Nov, Fable; the user: "can any of these sources and other brick facade parametric modelling tools be studied, their logic understood then implemented"; what was read and what was taken is in `knowledge/flux-logic/parametric-brick-sources.md`: the published text of T. Yazar's Parametric Brickwork (2013) and Parametric Bricks (2024) and Z. Ebrahimi's Parametric Brick Facade, read in the browser pane; the Grasshopper files were NOT opened, two being downloads and one paid). (1) **A brick at risk is shown** (`brick78.js`, `brickRisk(bs,sup,bW,lim,edge)`): every face brick above the first course gets `bear` (the share of its bed resting on the bricks below, length times depth, less what a turn takes; the course behind a one-brick wall carries too) and `risk` (bearing under `S.brick.bearMin`, default 0.4, or its centre of weight off the support along or through the wall); `G.brick.atRisk`, `minBear`; drawn red in the elevation, hatched (`.brick-risk`) in the course drawing, red in 3D; the unit bar says how many and why, or that every brick rests on at least so much. Bricks within a brick of a flush panel's edge are not judged (the brick below may belong to the next panel); not on a wave. (2) **Charges** (`FIELD_KINDS` gains `charge`; `S.field.charge` {pts 'u,v,strength; ...', reach, waves}): each point pushes or pulls by its sign, the effects add (q / (1 + d^2)), and the sum is read smooth (0.5 + 0.5 sum) or as ripples (0.5 + 0.5 sin(sum x waves x pi)); a field for every mode; a binding gives an older saved field its `charge` block. (3) **A second group** (`S.brick.splitDo` 'off' | 'colour' | 'out' | 'omit', `split` the field value where it starts, `col2`): bricks where the field passes the threshold take a second brick colour (`b.g2`, the 3D tint is the ratio of the two colours since the tint multiplies the material), or stand out by `shiftMax` while the rest stay flat (a relief with an edge), or are left out: only the whole headers of a Flemish, English or header bond on a straight wall, never in the first or top course or at an end, so the wall is pierced and still bears (a stretcher over a missing header rests on 44 % of its bed, both ends; the 0.4 limit was set with that in mind). Controls under What the field does: 'A second group' and the bearing limit. QA `brick` p78-1. Rendered: `review/examples-2026-11-06/brick79-risk-groups-charges.png`. Not done: a gap that varies brick by brick (left out on purpose: the courses above lose bearing), colour mixes by percentage, the bearing number in the schedule, the sources' own definitions compared in Rhino.
- **Pass 79, the brick panel evolved** (10 Nov, Fable; the user: "Don't recreate the logic of the 3 sources ... synthesize ... create new designs"; "the white cap ... can be optional"; "ui logic and panels ... updated to the complexity of the paver panel"; "make sure mortar placement is accurate. Allow the user to change the mortar colour and thickness"; `brick79.js` replaces the pass-77 mortar builder, `brsteps79.js` after the pass-78 controls). **Joints as one list**: `brickJoints(Gk)` -> {boxes [x0,x1,y0,y1,z0,z1,kind: bed | head | collar | cap], pads (a bed that turns with a turned brick), rec}; `brickMortar` builds the 3D from it, `brickElevSVG` draws it in the elevation (`.brick-mortar`; the dark ground had been showing as joints), `brickMortarLitres` and `brickTakeoff(G)` (stretchers, headers, cut bricks, the course behind, total, mortar in litres, at risk) count from it. **Mortar**: `S.brick.mortar` (hex; `BRICK_MORTARS` chips), `S.brick.rake` (the joint face set back 0 to 10 mm, 0 = flush), thickness = `joint` (under 3 mm no mortar is drawn: laid dry or glued); `brickMortarMat(c)`. **On top**: `S.brick.cope` 'stone' | 'brick' (a course of brick on edge across the wall, gauged to the wall's length, on its own bed; mesh `userData.copeBrick`) | 'none' (no coping, no cap bed, and the bar warns the top course takes the rain); `S.brick.piers` 'auto' | 'none'. **The step rule** (`brickStepRule(bs,bW,share)`, `S.brick.step` default 0.33, 0 = off): course by course from the bottom, a brick's push is held within that share of its width of every brick it rests on (a corbel), so a rough field or a second group pushed out comes out stepped instead of flagged;
