`G.brick.stepHeld`. **The calm margin** (`S.brick.calm`, mm): the field's turn and push fade to nothing toward the base, the top and the ends (smoothstep), and the second group stops there (colour excepted), so the wall's edges are plain brickwork. **Designs** (`BRICK_DESIGNS`, `brickDesign(id)`; the rules of the brick case studies and the three sources put together, none of them reproduced; table in `knowledge/flux-logic/stacked-aggregates.md`): Tide wall (English bond stepped out in ripples from three charges, a quarter brick a course at most, brick on edge on top), Lantern screen (Flemish bond, headers left out along a drawn line, solid at base, top and ends), Two-tone drift (third-lap bond, a second brick in rings), Shadow weave (running bond turned toward a line of light, dark raked mortar, calm at every edge), Proud field (Flemish bond, one group standing proud, stepped in). **The tab as six steps** (`.pvsteps.six`, `data-bstep`, `window.__brStep(k)`, `window.__brRead`): Wall (start a straight wall, a corner or a tower; what stands on top; piers; a line saying how many courses and how long the brickwork is; Wall size), Brick (the unit bar's unit part; the mortar: chips, hex, joint face), Bond (the bar's bond part; the open bond), Pattern (the designs; the field; what it does; the second group; the calm margin and the step rule; Tones), Check (the bar's rules; the bearing limit; the bearing fold), Deliver (the order table with mortar litres; the bundle). The unit bar's parts carry `data-bstep` and the bar hides in steps 1, 4 and 6. QA `brick` p79-1 (every bed box under a brick and over a brick, every head box between two bricks, joint thickness, faces, coping kinds, piers off, step rule, calm, five designs with 0 at risk and 0 straight joints) and p79-2. Rendered and looked at: `review/examples-2026-11-06/brick80-five-designs.png`, `ui80-brick-six-steps.png`. Not yet: the same depth for corners and towers inside the steps (they are started from the Wall step and use running bond), a brick-on-edge coping on a plan, openings, movement joints.
- **Pass 81, the brickwork's form is a choice the design survives; joints as deep as the bricks overlap; framed photographs** (10 Nov, Fable; the user's queue: "allow the user to change to another form like a corner or building ... more user control of facades ... proper structural engineering ... photos of designs are properly cropped and framed"; `brick81.js`, `brick81b.js`). **Forms** (`BRICK_FORMS`, `brickForm(id,{L,H,storeys,sides,cols,amp,frame})`, `S.brick.form`, `formL`, `formH`, `formN`, `formSides`, `formCols`, `formAmp`, `formFrame`): wall (single), corner, u, court (facing in), tower (`sides`, storeys), wave (the wave host: base straight, top swinging by `formAmp`; the bricks follow the curve; running bond), bays (`S.brick.bays` = the frame's width = `S.gap`; a grid of panels off a plan). The bond, mortar, field and second group are not touched; `brickDesign` loads as a wall. The Wall step's chips carry `data-bform` (the Host fold's form picker already uses `data-form`); sliders for a side's length and a storey's height, steppers for storeys, sides and bays, shown by form. **Framed bays**: `generate` does not treat them as one long wall (`brickSet && !bays` sets `PLAN_FLUSH`; the frame's width is in the spanning picture), each bay's courses run from frame to frame, set out from both sides with any cut brick in the middle (`BAY`, `brickCourseSym(..., all)`), and `brickFrameGeoms` builds columns between and beside the bays and a beam over every storey (`userData.frame`); no coping, no piers. **Joints** (`brickJoints` rewritten): a joint is only as deep as the two bricks it lies between overlap (`rng(b)`, `zz(a,c)`), so a brick pushed out takes its mortar only where it still sits on or stands beside another; beds are laid per pair of bricks (each reaching half way into the head joint beside it), the collar joint only behind stretchers that have not been pushed out. Before this the mortar was a slab the whole depth of the wall and stood proud of the bricks at the back of any wall with bricks pushed out (seen as a pale wall on the outside of a courtyard). **Check step**: slenderness = effective height (twice the height for a wall standing free, one storey on a plan, three quarters of a bay in a frame) over thickness, warned over 27; the stress at the foot from self weight against 1.5 MPa. **Photographs**: `dev-tools/scripts/photo.py <tool> <out_dir> <steps.json>` renders each step at full size in batches of five (a taller sheet is cut off by the browser), with `{FIT:theta,phi,k}` (the whole design from a direction) and `{AT:fx,fy,fz,theta,phi,dist}` (a point of the bounding box from a distance), and crops to the design with a margin at 3:2 unless `nocrop`. QA `brick` p81-1; p77-1 restated (mortar follows a brick that is pushed out); p79-1's bed check allows half a joint. Photographs: `review/examples-2026-11-10/`. Not yet: mortar on the waving wall (none is drawn on a wave host), Flemish, English and header bond on a plan (running bond there), a frame sized by an engineer, openings, the same form switch for timber.
- **Pass 80, the leaning timber wall with every connection made** (10 Nov, Fable; the user: "work on the wood assembly of the curved wall and make sure all wood connections work together"; `lean80.js` replaces `leanModel`, `leanGeometry`, `leanCSV`). Found by reading the model and by measuring, not by looking: a board two panels long is a chord and the course below is a chord turned half a board along, so on a tight curve a board end hung up to 70 mm off the board under it (and leaning in tightens the radius further: 2.6 m at the foot of the default ellipse is 1.1 m at the top); both leaves broke at the same nodes; board ends were square on a curve; the dowels were counted and never placed; the spacer blocks were held by nothing. Now: **board length follows the curve** (`ND`, the node positions: spacing = sqrt(8 x Reff x sagMax) / 2, with Reff the tightest radius over the next two boards less the lean and the wall's depth, sagMax = 0.8 x (mw/2 - mw/3), between 140 mm and the `node` asked), so boards are long on the flat of the curve and short where it turns (`nodeMin`, `nodeMax`, `nodeHeld`, `Reff`); the field that sets the lean is read through a window about 700 mm wide so neighbouring boards never disagree; measured `bearMin` (the least a joint bears on the board below; 61 of 89 mm on the default wall) and `sag`. **The inner leaf breaks where the outer runs through** (`j = (k + layer) % 2`). **Ends are cut on the bisector** of the two boards that meet, 1.5 mm back each (`q.c`, four plan corners; `leanPrism(c,y0,y1)` moves a box's corners so faces and uvs stay): every joint closes to 3 mm exactly. **Dowels**: at each end of every board above the first course, 90 mm in and again further in on boards 300 mm or longer, down through the board below (2 mh - 4 long), on the MIDDLE of the overlap of the two boards; diameter held to a tenth of the board's width (8 mm on an 89 board, `dd`, `ddAsk`), which keeps 2.5 d to the nearest edge (`edgeMin`). **Spacer blocks** 200 mm long bridge every outer joint (never one on another, as before) and are held by two M10 bolts through leaf, block and leaf, 55 mm either side of the joint (`ties`; heads sunk and plugged, the plugs are what shows). **Base**: packers under the first course at every node, a steel strap across both leaves at each anchor. **Top**: `S.lean.cap` (default on), a board across both leaves and the gap, panel by panel, ends on the radius. The bench is on legs and a cleat on the inner leaf. `-leanwall.csv` lists boards (length less the gap), blocks, cap boards, dowels and tie bolts (as rods, so the Rhino reader draws them as lines) and anchors; the cut list takes the blocks and the cap; the robot drives four dowels a board and two bolts a block. QA `host` p80-1 (bearing at least half a board, leaves never breaking together, ends 3 mm apart, dowel count and edge and end distances, bolts across both leaves, blocks at outer joints and never stacked, cap, packers, schedule kinds, a 22 m radius keeps the 1200 board); p61-1 restated (the schedule lists fixings too). Rendered and looked at: `review/examples-2026-11-06/lean81-connections.png`. Not yet: the stacked timber pavilion (`S.crib`) given the same audit, the dowels and bolts in the timber Rhino reader checked in Rhino, an engineer's numbers for the dowels (they are locating pins sized by geometry), the bench and deck fixings scheduled.
- **Pass 82, the user's review of the brick designs and the timber wall** (10 Nov, Fable; the user: "I don't like the two tone colours as a default and some bricks with gaps and some without in one panel without allowing it to change the locations ... Have those as a type of design option. And I don't like the weird white border or white caps everywhere around brick panels. The parametric brick stacked assemblies are the main designs for bricks. Allow more complex connections beyond dowels for the woods like other mass timber joinery"). **No white by default**: `S.brick.cope` defaults to 'brick' (a course of brick on edge) and every design uses it; a wall on an open plan is coped side by side in brick on edge (`onEdge`, mesh `userData.copeBrick`), in stone, or not at all; the building's coping on a closed plan takes the brick's colour unless stone is chosen; the frame of framed bays is dark (#3A3D41). **Stacked assemblies lead** (`BRICK_DESIGNS` entries now end with a group): main = Shadow weave (turned toward a drawn line), Tide wall (English bond stepped in ripples), Current (turned in rings round three charges), Swell (turned and stepped together by one slow noise); option = A second brick, Openings, A raised group. The Pattern step shows them under two headings. A wall still starts plain (second group off, no open bond). **The second group is placed by the user**: `S.brick.splitBy` 'field' | 'up' | 'down' | 'left' | 'right' | 'scatter' (`splitSeed`), read as `t2` beside the field's own `t`; the group's controls (where, how far, colour, another throw) show only while a group is on. **Timber joinery beyond dowels** (the leaning wall): `S.lean.endJoint` 'mitre' | 'lap' (a half lap of 120 mm or a third of the board: `q.lap` {L, body, lo, up}: the lower half of this board's start under the upper half of the last board's end, drawn as three prisms, the board cut longer by the lap (`q.cut`), and one more pin through both halves into the board below); `S.lean.fix` 'dowel' | 'screw' (8 mm fully threaded screws, scheduled as screws); `S.lean.tie` 'bolt' | 'dovetail' (hardwood ties housed 25 mm into both leaves with a dovetail: `blockW = gap + 50`, no bolts, no plugs); `S.lean.cap` has its switch. QA `brick` p82-1 and `host` p82-2; p77-1 and p79-2 restated (stone is chosen where a stone coping is measured; seven designs). Photographs, framed by `photo.py`: `review/examples-2026-11-10/` (stacked assemblies on a wall, a corner, a building, a waving wall and framed bays; close-ups; the timber wall with half laps and dovetailed ties). Not yet: more timber joints (scarf, finger, mortise and tenon, steel knife plates) and the same joinery choices on the stacked timber pavilion; the user's message ended mid-sentence at "mass timber joinery connections that are", so the list of joints wanted is a guess.
- **Pass 83, Flemish and English bond through a square corner and round a building; courses that run on from storey to storey; mortar on the waving wall** (10 Nov, Fable, in a branched session; the user: "Bonds through corners", the first item of "more user control ... from walls to buildings, mid rises and tower"; `brick83.js` before `brickGenerate`). **The quoin of a one-brick wall** (`brickSideThick(bond,str,LT,RT,len,bL,bW,j,prev)` -> [[s0,s1,kind,flags]] along a whole side, 0 at the plan corner on the left, the outer corners at -bW and len + bW): in one course a wall RUNS (its face course starts with a whole stretcher at the outer corner, the quoin stretcher, `flags.nb` = nothing behind it but the other wall, `flags.q`); in the same course the other wall BUTTS (it starts a joint after that with a queen closer (bW - joint) / 2, a through piece); the next course swaps them (`SD.butt`, the same turn-taking as running bond). English: stretchers when it runs, headers when it butts. Flemish: quoin stretcher, header, stretcher...; closer, stretcher, header... A free end is a stopped end (stretcher first, or header and closer). A side is set out from BOTH ends; where the halves meet it is closed with whole bricks and the upright joints gauged within 3 mm, or with one cut brick in the middle (broken bond), and of all the ways to close it the one that keeps a quarter-brick lap over the course below (`prev`, its joints) is taken. `TKP` = Flemish or English on a flush side whose plan has only square corners turning outward (`PLAN_SIDE.thickOK`, set in `generate`; a straight set off a plan counts) and no open bond; `THK` = that or the single-wall `SPB && TKB`; the wall is then `bL` thick, bricks are pushed out but not turned, the first two pieces at a quoin are held flat, and `G.backs` leaves out the brick behind a quoin stretcher. Header and third-lap bond on a plan, a courtyard facing in, any corner that is not square, a wave and an open bond stay in running bond (`bondHeld`, and the panel says so). **Laps on a plan are measured** (`brickPlanLaps(panels)` puts the panels of a side and all its storeys back together; `G.brick.minLap`, `straight`, `sideLaps`), for running bond too. **Storey on storey**: on a flush set with more than one row the courses are one lattice from the ground (`FS`, `R0` = the first course of this panel, `gy` its offset, `rows` the courses that start inside the panel; `rr = r + R0` decides which wall runs; `G.brick.r0`), so a course never stops at a floor and the bond carries through; the first course of an upper storey gets its bed. **The form suits the bond** (`brickFormSnap(id,L,sides)`: for Flemish or English on a corner, three sides or a four-sided building a side is a whole number of the bond's repeat: a joint more than whole repeats between two quoins, half a brick more from a quoin to a stopped end; of three sides the outer two are made to suit), and choosing a bond in the unit bar sets the form out again. **Joints**: the joint behind the quoin stretcher, the joint between the other wall's inner face and the course behind, the bed where the quoin stretcher lies on the other wall; between two panels of one wall an upright joint is no deeper than the plain wall and a brick lying on a brick of the next panel gets its bed (`flushPrev`); **a bed under a turned brick is the exact overlap of the two bricks** (`brickBedPoly(b,c,bW,rec)`, a clipped polygon, `brickPolyPrism`; it had been a strip the brick's whole depth, which showed as a white plate under an overhang); **mortar on the waving wall** (`brickWaveJoints`: those beds, and upright joints as short blocks between two bricks that follow the curve, `out.obx`). The schedule names `quoin`; the brick-on-edge and stone copings on an open plan sit on the wall's true thickness (`zc`, `TKc`) and the brick one has its mortar. QA `brick` p83-1 (corner, three sides, a four-sided building of one and three storeys, both bonds: thickness, no straight joint, laps of 62 mm, no brick of one wall inside the other at a quoin, a joint between them, one quoin stretcher a course a corner, closers, 100 courses through three storeys, any side length, the fallbacks, the snap) and p83-2 (wave mortar, beds inside both bricks, panel joints). Measured before it was looked at: 77 side lengths from 3 to 6 m, both bonds, corner and building: 0 straight joints, 0 overlaps, smallest lap 30 mm (Flemish, one length), English never under 52. Photographs: `review/examples-2026-11-10/15...22`. Not yet: header bond and third-lap bond round a corner, a courtyard's inside corners and obtuse corners in a one-brick bond, a stopped end for the waving wall, openings with lintels, a footing, wall thickness stepping with height, movement joints.
- **Pass 84, the Timber tab and more timber joinery** (10 Nov, Fable, the branched session; slice 2 of the user's "more user control in a simplified workflow ... Brick and wood walls and pavers"; `stage84-joinery.py`, `stage84b-timbertab.py`, `timber84.js` before `startBuild()`). **The tab**: `Timber` follows Brick courses in the rail (`TAB_ORDER`, `FLOW` Category, `SUB`, `HUE`); it is not a panel mode (no `TAB_MODE` entry): opening it with nothing in timber standing builds the leaning wall, and a click on a panel tab sets the timber aside as before. `timberWhich()` = 'lean' | 'pavilion' | 'cwall' | 'field' | '' (the last three are `S.crib` closed, with an arc under 360, or with more than one wall); `timberFam()` 'lean' | 'crib'; `timberForm(id)` turns every whole form off and the chosen one on, keeping each family's section and joinery; `TIMBER_DESIGNS` / `timberDesign(id)` are eight shapes to start from (Shelter arc, Long screen, Snail; Lantern, Hourglass, Leaning drum; Garden wall; Room of walls) that set shape numbers and a field and never touch the joinery. Six steps (`.pvsteps.six.tm`, blocks tagged `data-tstep` and `data-tf` 'lean' | 'crib' | 'any', `window.__tmStep(k)`, `window.__tmRead`): Form (chips `data-tform`, shapes `data-tdesign`, the shape sliders of the form that stands), Board (section, gap, joint spacing; member length, width, height, fold), Joinery (`timberJointSVG()` draws the joint in hand to scale with its caption under it; board to board, leaf to leaf and to the ground; at every crossing, at the posts and at the ground), Pattern (five field chips `data-tfield`; what the field does), Check (`timberRules()` -> [kind, sentence] as `pr-*` in `.tm-rules`: bearing of a joint, pin edge distance, the step, scarfs made and scarfs refused, the mass against the base with the hold-down force of a straight wall, stress across the grain under the first course or at the foot of a pivot stack against 2.5 MPa; each estimate is said to be a first check), Deliver (counts in a table, what the bundle carries). Controls are new copies bound to the same state paths, with the same limits as the ones under Host surface (3D), which stay. `read()` fills any key of `LEAN_DEF` / `CRIB_DEF` the state lacks. `timberHeader()` (from `updateStats`, `leanBuild`, `cribBuild`) makes the header pill and stats speak of the timber form; the step bar's Category reads Timber and its Finish the section; `buildTabNav` steps over Timber unless a timber form stands and over the panel tabs while one does; the two timber Start cases open the tab. **Joinery, the leaning wall** (`S.lean.endJoint` gains 'scarf' and 'finger', `S.lean.base` 'strap' | 'knife'): every lap, scarf and finger joint is now set out in its own frame, along the bisector of its two boards, so its two halves share one footprint exactly and the board's body runs from the end of one joint to the start of the next (before this the halves of a lap followed their own boards and missed each other at the tips on a curve); a joint's length is the joint's, from the shorter of its two boards (`q.lap.La`, `Lb`, `ka`, `kb`). A scarf tapers through the board's thickness with a 6 mm nib at each thin end (`leanHex`), is glued and pinned, and is only made at 1 in 6 or flatter: where the boards are too short it is a half lap instead (`scarfs`, `scarfLaps`, `scarfSlope`). Fingers: six across the width meshing over 40 mm (`q.fin.a`, `q.fin.b`), glued. A knife plate (`knives`): at every other node a steel plate stands in the gap on a base plate under both leaves, between filler blocks cut course by course to the lean (`fills`), bolted through leaf, filler, plate, filler and leaf at two courses and held by two anchors; it takes the place of the packer and of the blocks of the first eight courses there. No mortise and tenon on this wall (a mortise through a board laid flat would cut it in two), and the tab says so. **Joinery, the stacked timber** (`S.crib.cog`, `frameJoint` 'housed' | 'tenon', `postBase` 'none' | 'knife'): a cog makes the course rise by the member's height less the cog (`R.h` is now the course, `R.hm` the member), so the members key into each other at every crossing; a tenoned member stops inside the jamb or end post (`mm.ta`, `mm.tb`, cut shorter) and is held by a 16 mm peg through the post (`R.pegs`); a post that reaches the ground can stand on a knife plate with two pins, 20 mm clear of the footing (`cribKnife`). Schedules: `-leanwall.csv` lists knife plates, base plates, knife bolts and base anchors; `-members.csv` gains `cog_mm,tenons` when either is used; `-frame.csv` lists pegs and knife plates; `grasshopper/timber_from_csv.py` draws a knife plate (changed, not run in Rhino). QA `host` p84-1, `flow` p84-2. Looked at: `review/examples-2026-11-10/23...30` (the finger joints were rendered and are too fine to photograph well). Not yet: a stopped end for the leaning wall, joinery for the bench and the deck, the cut list and robot rows for the new joints (the robot still drills and pins as for a butt), an engineer's sizes for pins, pegs and plates, the same tab for the portal, canopy and corridor.
- **Pass 85, the brick pattern as a stack of layers; openings; no cap and no piers** (11 Nov, Fable, the branched session; the user: "you dont need this weirdly placed brick cap and back brick supports ... do a much more complex parametric brick design pass ... synthesize the logic of his brick design rhino plugin"; what was read and what was taken is in `knowledge/flux-logic/brick-plugin-and-chishe.md`: the plugin's text attribute lists and command overview only, nothing run, and the archisearch page on Chi She; `brick85.js`, `brick85b.js` before `brickGenerate`). **No cap, no piers**: `S.brick.cope` 'none' and `piers` 'none' by default and in every design; both stay as options in the Wall step. **Layers** (`S.brick.layers`, up to four of {src 'points' | 'line' | 'field' | 'height' | 'noise', pts 'u,v; u,v' as shares of the elevation with v up, decay (reach, mm), wave (ripple length), does 'turn' | 'push' | 'bulge' | 'fill' | 'colour', amt, thr, alt}; `brickLayers(B,FW,FH)` -> [{does, amt, thr, alt, f(U,V)}]): on top of the field's own turn or push each layer adds its turn (deg), its push (mm), a second brick where it passes `thr`, or a **belly**: the wall's surface moves out (`shL`, the bricks turn with it, `thL`, held within 18 deg), held twice so it can stand: `brickBulgeHold` scales the whole belly so no sampled step between courses passes the step rule, and `brickSurfRule(bs,bW,share)` then holds each brick within that step of every brick it rests on (`G.brick.bulge`, `bulgeHeld`, `surfHeld`); a belly is refused on a one-brick bond (`bulgeThick`). `alt` turns or pushes every second course the other way. **fill**: with an open bond, a bat is laid in each gap where the layer is under `thr` (`cells[..][5].fill`, `G.brick.filled`): a pierced wall whose density follows a layer and every brick still rests on two, every bat on the brick under its gap; a stretcher is never simply deleted from running bond (the two bricks over it would meet on a joint over the hole). **Calm** (`S.brick.calm`) now counts the distance to a side's ends, to the base and top of the whole wall and to every opening (`dE`); a belly always fades over two bricks there. `startCaseReset` clears the layers. `BRICK_LAYERED` / `brickLayered(id)`: Belly (a wall 8 m by 4 m bellies 340 mm above its door), Veil (an open bond that closes away from a drawn line), Ripple (two points push in rings and turn row against row), Seam (the wall swells along a line, calm at its window). The Pattern step has 'The pattern stack' after the designs: the four chips (`data-blay`), a card per layer (`.bly`: source, what it does, amount or threshold, reach, points, ripple, alternate courses, remove) and Add a layer. **Openings** (`S.brick.wins` per side per storey, `winW`, `winH`, `sill`, `door`; `brickOpenings(B,len,storeys,H,bL,bW,bH,j,side0)` -> [{x0,x1,c0,c1,c2,door,id}] along a side, courses from the ground): the jambs are put on joints of the opening's first course (`brickSnap`, kept in the side's cache so every panel and storey agrees) and every course is cut at them (`brickCut`: bricks inside go, a brick crossing a jamb becomes a bat or a closer, pieces at a jamb are held flat, `b.jamb`), so the bond runs on unchanged round the opening; over each a soldier course (`G.soldiers`, two leaves in a one-brick wall) on a steel angle bearing 150 mm on each pier, a frame and glass (`brickOpeningParts`, child meshes `userData.window`); `G.ops` (every opening the panel meets, `own` for the one that holds its centre), `G.brick.openings`, `opRefused`, `pierMin`, `opWide`; the soldiers carry the course above in the bearing check and are in the schedule (`soldier`) and the take-off. Openings are for straight walls and plans whose corners are all square (`PLAN_SIDE.sqAll`), not for a wave, an open bond or framed bays. `brickLaps` no longer counts the gap of an opening as a joint. A one-brick side's courses are made once and kept (`PLAN_SIDE.cache`, `thickSeq`). **Taller forms**: `brickForm` to 16 storeys; over 45,000 bricks the mortar is one slab a panel cut round the openings (`brickSlabMortar`, only for brickwork neither turned nor pushed). `S.brick.carry` 'self' | 'frame' (on the frame the bed at each floor is left open as the soft joint under a shelf angle, `G.brick.soft`). **First checks** (`brickFirstCheck`, `brickFirstRules`, `brickSectionSVG`, appended to the Check step's rules): openings and piers, a lintel over 1.8 m, bearing on itself by the old rule of thumb (one brick for the top two storeys, half a brick more for every two below) or hung on the frame, the line load at the foot and a strip footing on 150 kPa, and the wall in section to scale; each says it is a first check. `BRICK_EXAMPLES` / `brickExample(id)` in the Wall step (`data-bex`): wall, corner, low building (two storeys, English bond, bearing on itself, windows and a door), mid-rise (five storeys, Flemish bond, on the frame), tower (twelve storeys, a turned skin hung floor by floor). QA `brick` p85-1 and p85-2; four older checks restated with reason (they now ask for the cap or piers they measure). Measured before it was looked at: walls, a corner, a house, a 3-storey building, the mid-rise (45,000 bricks) and the tower (47,000): 0 straight joints, the wall's own laps, 0 bricks in an opening, 0 at risk; the belly steps 38 mm a course at most however deep it is asked. Photographs: `review/examples-2026-11-10/31...41`. Not yet: a wall whose top follows a roof line (gables, a sawtooth), a belly on a one-brick bond or on a free plan curve, an elevation drawing sheet at drawing quality, a brick sill, the robot and Rhino reader for soldiers.
- **Pass 86, the brick pattern moved on the wall; no red bricks; brick sets seamless** (11 Nov, Fable; the user: "I dont see how a user can change the bricks turn, open bond, spacing, location of swells or tide wall ripples or shadow weaves ... switching from 1 picture panel to 3 looks weird ... some bricks turned red. why?", then "I dont want the bricks to turn red at all"; `brick86.js`, `stage86-handles.py`). **Dots on the elevation**: `brickSources()` lists every source that has a place (the field's points for `attract` and `charge`, its line for `attractCurve`, the centre of `radial`; each layer's points or line) as {key 'f' | 'L<i>', name, line, single, extra, reach, get, set}; `brickHandlesDraw(c,TW,TH,m)` (from `renderBrick2D`) draws them on the brick elevation as `.bh-dot` circles (blue = the field, orange = a layer), a layer's reach as a ring and a line dashed; `brickHandlesInit()` (pointer events on `#view2d`, moves on `window`) drags a dot (60 ms throttle, `fieldUse()` for a field source), adds a point at the next click when a source is armed (`window.__bhArm`), removes one on a double click. **The look, in numbers** (Pattern step, after the designs): what the field does (`brick.op`), turn at the light and dark ends (`d2`, `d1`), push (`shiftMax`), the gap of the open bond (`brick.open`, also still in the Bond step), six field chips (`data-lkf`) and the field's own sliders with typed numbers (`.lkf`: noise scale and seed, charge reach and waves, the reach of points or a line, the size of rings), and 'Where it happens' (one row per source with Add a point). **No brick at risk, none red**: after `brickRisk` a brick that would not bear is eased back toward flat with the bricks under it (its turn, push and surface turn scaled by 0.6, the supports by 0.8, up to five times, then flat), `G.brick.riskHeld`; the red tint in 3D and the elevation and the hatch in the course drawing are gone. `brickReadout()` (from `brickStats`) writes `#readout` over the 3D view for brickwork: every brick rests, how many were eased, the belly, the gaps closed, and what the dots are (it had kept the sheet's light note). A belly's ripple is held to 600 mm or more. **A set of brick panels is one wall**: in `generate`, brick mode turns 'three leaves' into three panels in a row and holds the gap at 0 (the 3D had kept the 60 mm gap). QA `brick` p86-1; p78-1 and p79-2 restated with reason. Tried by hand in the browser pane: a field dot dragged and the wall rebuilt. Not yet: dots in the 3D view, dragging a whole line, rings for field points, undo of a drag as one step.
- **Pass 87, a set of panels is one design; Panels works both ways** (11 Nov, Fable; the user: "in fit and layout, when I change it to a row of 3, the drop down disappears and I cant change it back"; "when switching from 1 to a panel of 2 or more, make sure all designs connect seamlessly together using the same logic whether thats a brick wall, paver design or wood wall design"; "if its a paver pattern and there is more than 1 panel, the kerb and roof parapet need to go around the edge of the entire design"; `seam87.js` before `panelMode`, `stage87-seam.py`). `S.seam` (default TRUE; `SEAM_DEFAULT` is what a preset without the key takes; the QA reset sets both false and the gap back to 24, so the older checks still measure framed sets) and the boot gap is 6. **The control**: `panelsCtl(parent)` (class `.pnl`: chips 1 to 6 `data-pn`, Seamless / Framed `data-seam`, a line saying what the set is, `seamWord()`) heads every Panel size fold and the picture block's Fit & layout fold; `panelsSet(n)` and `panelsNow()` go up and back down (the one-way 'make it a row of three' button is hidden, still clickable by id). **Sheets** (cut, tiles, perforated): cut: `generate` runs the across-the-set branch when `cutSpan || seam` and gives each panel `_set` {ox, oy, TW, TH, l, r, b, t}; an inner edge's margin is half a bar, a motif crossing a joint is kept (`_keep`, a region open on the inner sides) and cut at the sheet edge (`pts.__c0` = its whole centre), and `finish` reads the gradient and the solid share at set coordinates, so both halves of a cut motif agree; the 'Across the set' control hides while seam is on. Tiles and holes: `seamAxis(o,size,total,f0,f1,d,p,extra,lo,hi)` sets the lattice out over the whole set (outer margins only), each panel takes the positions that clear its own edge (a web for holes, 1 mm for tiles), stagger and dither parity follow the set's row (`j0`, `i0`). **Brick**: as pass 86 (one wall, no gap). **Ribs, kinetic units, paving** (`seamMergeMode`): a flat set becomes ONE piece: `seamMerge()` keeps `MERGE_SET` [arr, cols, rows, module width, module height, gap] and makes the panel cols x rows modules big, single; `seamTab(m)` / `seamAfter()` in `showTab` (a user's click) merge on entering and bring the panels back on leaving (`seamRestore`); `seamArr()` on the Arrangement control; Panels n over a merged piece is n modules wide; a plan chosen over a merged piece gives the panels back. So paving on three panels is one ground with one kerb or parapet. Programmatic sets (recipes, Compose, the tests) are not merged. QA `flow` p87-1. Looked at: `dev-tools/scripts/seam87/*.png`. Not yet: a merged piece remembered through undo (the chips read 1 after an undo), rows in the Panels control, framed bays for ribs.
- **Pass 88, the lattice wall of bent laths; the wood book read** (11 Nov, Fable, readers on Sonnet; the user: "deeply and comprehensively synthesize the logic ... of the digital wood design precedent book ... mainly the beyond form definition since Annalisa may be my user ... lattice shell methodologies or timber gridshell ... focus on creating smaller wood creations like parametric walls before working your way up"; `lattice88.js` before `LEAN_DEF`, `stage88-lattice.py`). **The reading**: `knowledge/flux-logic/digital-wood-design.md` holds Beyond Form Definition (Correa, Krieg, Meyboom) read in full by the lead, 37 more chapters digested from six readers' notes (kept in `flux-logic/digital-wood-notes/`), rule tables, a ladder of twelve things to build smallest first, and what the book cannot give (it has almost no design values: E, strengths and limits come from the timber standards and are named as such). Text only (pypdf): figures were not seen. The heritage chapters were not read. The brick paper (Afsari, Swarts, Gentry, ASCAAD 2014) is a section of `brick-plugin-and-chishe.md`. **The form**: `S.lat` (`LAT_DEF` {on, len, height, bow, topBow, waves, lean, belly, angle, pitch, lw, lt, layers 1|2, species, hold, use, stock, node 'bolt'|'dowel', fd, rail, ply, plies, anchor, spare, testSpan, testDefl}; recipes, snapshots, Compose and the off-lists carry it; `latActive()`). `latSurface(K)`: the wall as z over its elevation: a base line and a top line that differ (bow, topBow, waves, lean) and a belly read from the field through a coarse smoothed grid, all as one cubic B-spline (`ev(x,y)` gives z and exact first and second derivatives). `latTrace` integrates the geodesic equation of that surface (midpoint steps of 25 mm) from a start point at `angle` above the level; `latLay(K)` starts both families from equal arc divisions of the base (each foot a V shared by a lath of each family) and of the two ends. `latModel()` (cached on the state and the field): **the stock sets the form**: the tightest radius allowed is E t / (2 x use x f) from `LAT_WOOD` (EN 338 class values, said to be from the standards) or the circle of the user's own three-point bending test (`testSpan`, `testDefl`); with `hold` on the belly is eased by bisection, then the bows and the lean, until the worst lath is inside it (`R.held`); crossings are found level by level (both families climb) and are one hole on each lath (`q.holes`: edge, crossings, edge, by distance along the lath); per lath `kmax`, `share` (of bending strength used by bending alone, the staged check), `twist` (deg/m), `dev` (how far from straight it is when unrolled, from its own 3D line: under 0.1 mm), end cut angles; `over` the stock and the angle that would fit; twin rails and stiles as packs of thin plies with their own bend share; blocks half way between crossings when the lattice is doubled; weight, solidity, the wall as a body in a 0.5 kPa wind and the hold-down on the anchors (a first check, said as one); straight stock packed first-fit; spares at `spare` %. `latGeometry` sweeps every piece with `latStrip(stations, o0, o1, halfWidth, cutA, cutB)` (ends cut on the plane of the edge they land on): laths by family, blocks, bolts or dowels at crossings (up to 2,400), rail bolts at every lath end, rails, stiles, a two-layer plywood sole cut to the plan (`LAT_SOLE` 36 under everything), anchors; merged by role, `userData.lat`, `window.PS_LAT`. `latSheetSVG()` is the 2D view and `-two-states.svg`: the wall as it stands and every lath as it is cut (a straight strip with its holes, against the stock length). `-laths.csv` (length, end cuts, hole positions, tightest radius, bending share, twist, straightness), `-lattice-nodes.csv`, `-lath-drill.csv` (the run of a drill-and-saw bench: cut, drill, cut, mark). Timber tab: Lattice wall is the first form and what the tab opens on; blocks `data-tf="lat"` in Form, Board (timber, section, layers, stock, share, hold, the bending test), Joinery (bolt or dried hardwood dowel: hole 0.93 d; rails; anchors), Pattern (angle, spacing, belly, spares); `latRules`, `latJointSVG`, `latHeader`, `latLines` reach the tab through wrappers of `timberRules`, `timberJointSVG`, `timberHeader`; three shapes (Sail, Ribbon, Swell). QA `host` p88-1; p84-2 restated (the tab opens on the lattice wall). Rendered and looked at: `dev-tools/scripts/lat88/*.png`. Not yet: a robot program for the bench, the wall in Rhino, an opening in the lattice, a closed plan (a ring or a pavilion), the erection sequence, the other rungs of the ladder (the double-layer bent sheet whose finger joints fit only when bent; stock boards on milled ribs; the half-lapped stick screen; a kerfed panel; a gridshell last), and the old timber forms' posts that carry nothing.
- **Pass 89, the look of the approved mockups** (11 Nov, Fable; the user: "I really like the mockups themselves" ... "yes apply the mockups look to the tool now"; `look89.css`, `stage89-look.py`). Nothing computes differently and no control, id, data-path or tab moved. A second sheet, `<style id="look89">`, follows the tool's own and redefines its tokens in all three theme forms (light `:root`, the dark media query, `[data-theme="dark"]`): paper #F4F2EC / #F9F8F4, ink #17161C, secondary #4A4753, hairline #D6D1C4, ONE action colour (indigo #3A34B8; lavender #C9BEF4 in dark), lavender for what is chosen (`--lav`, always with `--on-ink` #17161C text), sky #AFC6EC behind 2D drawings (`--stage`), an aubergine rail (`--rail` #3A1F2A, `--rail-ink`, `--rail-on`), `--field` for inputs; every `--h-*` hue is the action colour. Type: Hanken Grotesk (`--sans`; the name `Instrument Sans` is replaced everywhere, canvas and SVG font strings included), Newsreader (`--serif`) for `.lede` and `.note`, DM Mono for numbers; tab titles are weight 300 at 27 px. Corners: the stage caps every pixel `border-radius` in the file at 2 px (percentages and variables are left, so round dots and slider thumbs stay); the header stats, the step bar and the step navs are joined hairline cells, not pills; segments and buttons have ink borders, the chosen segment and chip are ink, the primary button is the action colour, no gradients. Rules in the sheet carry an `html body` prefix so they win over styles later passes inject. The logo is unchanged (its square is held dark in both themes). RULE from the user's review, kept in the plan: no panel kind is folded away; Pattern, Ombrae tiles, Perforated image, Brick, Timber, Serial sections, Kinetic units and Pavers all stay first-class tabs. Looked at in the browser pane in light and dark (Pattern, Brick courses, Start, Pavers, Timber). Pass 89b (the user, on Opus 5.5): the sky blue moved to the header (`--head`, #AFC6EC; #2B3050 in dark) and the 2D and 3D views went back to neutral paper (`--stage` #ECE8DF, `--stage3d` #E4E0D6); `stage89b-sky.py` replaces the sheet's contents from `look89.css`. Not yet: the mockups' layout moves (materials grouped in the rail with their steps under them, the feasibility strip under the drawing, Surface and Texture as two steps); a stale readout over the 3D view after leaving Brick courses (older than this pass).
- **Pass 90, glued brickwork, prefabricated elements, tolerance and balance** (12 Nov, Opus 5.5; the user: "focus on learning from the flexibrick ... upgrading the tools capabilities"; logic in `knowledge/flux-logic/robotic-touch.md`; `brick90.js` before `startBuild();timberBuild();orderTabs();`, `stage90-flexbrick.py`). New `S.brick` keys (`BRICK90`, filled by `brick90Keys()`): `fix` 'mortar' | 'glue', `tol` (± mm a brick), `elems`, `elemL`, `elemKg`. **Glue**: choosing it sets the joint to 2 mm (kept in `_jm` and given back with mortar); `brickGlueAreas(Gk)` is each brick's bed glued on the course below (plan overlap of rectangles, `brickBedPoly` for turned bricks; the first course its own bed), `brickGlueTotals()` the m2 and litres at 1.5 mm; the placement list gains a last column `glue_cm2` and the robot list a `measure` row after every `every` courses, only while glue is chosen (the CSV checks of older passes see mortar). **Tolerance** (`brickTol`): along a course t sqrt(n), at worst n t, up the wall t sqrt(courses); `every` = floor((1.5/t)^2). **Balance** (`brickBalance`): the centre of weight (Y = shift + bW - dep/2) of the wall and of everything above each course against that course's extent, `worst` in thirds off the middle (over 1 = outside the middle third, over 3 = past the course). **Elements** (`brickElements`): n = the larger of length / `elemL` and mass / `elemKg`; a brick belongs to the element its centre is in, so each edge (`edges`, one point per course) is toothed with the bond; `straight` counts courses meeting on a straight joint (stack bond); per element bricks, length, mass, two lifting points; drawn on the elevation in the action colour with E1, E2 (`.brick-elems`, a wrapper of `brickElevSVG`); `-elements.csv` in the bundle (`bundleFiles` wrapper). All four speak in Check (a wrapper of `brickFirstRules`, before the section) as first checks; the order table adds glue and elements. Controls: 'How the bricks are joined' (Brick step: Joined with, tolerance) and 'Prefabricated elements' (Wall step). Also fixed: the brick note over the 3D view (`#readout`) is cleared in any other mode (`readout90`, a wrapper of `updateStats`). QA `brick` p90-1. Not yet: sliding headers as a layer, the elements drawn apart in 3D, holding a belly to keep the weight in the middle third (it is reported, not held), glue on a plan or a wave checked by eye.
- **Pass 91, the parametric slat wall; the mouse as a design tool** (12 Nov, Opus 5.5; the user: "focus on learning from the flexibrick and the timber slat wall", then "dont do only conventional traditional slat walls, they need to be cool parametric designs ... add or remove control points ... more parametric user control through a mouse"; the Sequential Wall in `knowledge/flux-logic/robotic-touch.md`, synthesised; `slat91.js` before `startBuild();timberBuild();orderTabs();`, its Timber-tab part `slatTabUI()` called after `timberBuild()`, `stage91-slat.py`). A second kind of the lattice-wall form: `S.lat.kind` 'lattice' | 'slat' (`SLAT_DEF`: sbh 95, sbt 21 slat; sLap 30; sStud 600, sSw 45, sSd 95 stud; sDepth 60 the most a slat stands off; sKickMin 4, sKickMax 30; sBmin 1, sBmax 4 bays a slat spans; sWin, sWinW, sWinH, sSill; sFix 'screw' | 'nail'; sStock 4800), so every place the lattice wall has in the tool (camera, wall and floor, bundle, step bar, 2D form sheet, Timber tab) is shared: `latBuild`, `latCSV`, `latNodesCSV`, `latDrillCSV`, `latSheetSVG`, `latLines`, `latRules`, `latJointSVG`, `latHeader` hand over to the slat versions when `slatOn()`; `timberWhich()` says 'slat', `timberFam()` 'lat'; `timberForm('slat')`; bundle names become -slats.csv, -frame.csv, -slat-robot.csv. `slatModel()` (cached): panels no longer than the stock or twice the robot's reach, each with its own studs at no more than `sStud`; a window in the middle panel with full-height jamb studs, a lintel (two 45 x 195), a sill plate and cripples under and over; courses at a pitch of slat height less lap (a last course closes at the top plate); in each course the slats run between supports, each spanning the bays the field asks (at least one, so on two studs), every joint on a stud and never on a joint of the course below (moved a bay, `staggerFix`); courses over the glazing stop on the jambs, courses over the lintel run across; the field sets each slat's packer (`z0`, 0 to `sDepth`, in 5 mm); the DRIP RULE gives each slat a kick at its foot of the most any slat it laps stands proud of it plus `sKickMin`, held at `sKickMax` by bringing the slat forward (`held`); wedge packers per slat per stud; stock by first fit decreasing (kerf 4); a first check of the stud spanning sole to top plate under 0.6 kPa (C24, 14.8 MPa) and the note that a free-standing wall needs posts. Geometry by role (studs, plates, packers, slats in two tones, glass); `slatSheetSVG` (elevation with studs dashed, slats shaded by their stand-off, panel joints, then every length as cut against the stock), `slatJointSVG` (two slats lapping on wedge packers at a stud). Timber tab: a 'Slat wall' chip, four blocks of its own (`data-slat`), the lattice's blocks hidden while it stands (`.slat-off`). PARAMETRIC (`slat91-core.js` spliced in): `sTurn` (mm one end of a slat stands past the other, by the field, held to .35 of its length), `sAlt` (every second course the other way), `sOpen` (bays left open where the field is under it, never two above each other, never in the first or last course or by the window), `sBow` (the wall bows in plan: studs, plates in pieces and slats follow `zs(x)`); `zAt(s,x)` is a slat's back at its top edge; packers at least 5 mm; the drip rule is checked at both ends of every overlap; `SLAT_DESIGNS` / `slatDesign(id)`: Louvre ripple, Twisted weave, Porous screen, Bowed wall (a chip row in the Form step). MOUSE (`mouse92.js`, in the same stage): a bar under the 2D drawing (`.bhbar`: Move, Add, Remove, which points, a tip) for the brick elevation and the slat wall; Add puts a point where you click (into the chosen source; 'A new layer' starts a push layer of the brick pattern there; with no points the field becomes a field of charges); Remove takes a clicked dot away (a one-point layer goes with it); scrolling on a dot changes a charge's strength (to -2..2) or a layer's or attractor's reach; right click removes; handlers in the capture phase so the older drag code keeps Move on bricks; `slatSheetSVG` draws the field's dots on the slat elevation (`SLAT_MAP`); `window.__bhSetMode(m,src)`. QA `host` p91-1, `brick` p92-1. Photographs: `review/examples-2026-11-10/55-57`. Not yet: a Rhino reader for -slats.csv, slats bent or twisted along their length (the Beyond Form Definition prototypes), openings other than one window, corners, the shared cut list (`timberCut`) and robot KRL.
- **Pass 93 (v92), the bent strip shell** (12 Nov, Opus 5.5; the user chose "Bent wood walls"; Beyond Form Definition prototype 1 synthesised, see `knowledge/flux-logic/digital-wood-design.md`; `bent93.js`, `stage93-bent.py`; header numbers one size in v91b). A third kind of the lattice-wall form, `S.lat.kind` 'bent' (`BENT_DEF`: bWmax 600, bWmin 160 strip widths; bT 6.5 ply; bLayers 2; bTol 3 mm the strip may stand off the surface across its width; bFp 90 finger pitch, bFl 40 finger length; bDow 10; bUse .55; bRib 240; bSheet 2440). `bentModel()` (cached): the surface is `latSurface` (the lattice wall's bows, waves, lean and field belly); outer-layer seams walk along x, each strip the widest whose sag across it (k_x w^2 / 8) stays within `bTol` (developable, cut flat); inner-layer seams at the outer strips' middles (staggered); per strip: developed length, tightest radius along it, bending share (E t k / 2 over f, birch ply 9 GPa / 35 MPa), the thickness that would bend there (`tMax`), sag, twist, finger splices past a sheet; per seam: fingers every `bFp` of arc length, each with its two angles from the surface (`angles` counts distinct pairs), owner alternating, the flat mismatch (arc less height: laid flat the fingers do not line up); dowels one per finger; base and head ribs to the plan curves. `bentGeometry`: each strip a thin shell between two normal offsets of the surface (outer and inner layer), fingers as flush interlocking teeth across each seam, dowels through both layers along the normal (to 1,600), ribs as segments. `bentSheetSVG` (the shell in elevation with seams and fingers, then every strip flat with its fingers), `bentJointSVG`, `bentLines`, `bentRules` (bending, developability, fingers fit only bent, stagger, dried dowels, splices, mass and ribs: first checks), `bentHeader`; `BENT_DESIGNS` Sail shell, Leaning canopy, Rippled bend (`bentDesign`). Shared hooks hand over when `bentOn()`, bundle names -strips.csv, -fingers.csv, -strip-robot.csv; Timber tab chip and blocks `data-bent` (`bentTabUI()` after `slatTabUI()`), the lattice's own blocks hidden. QA `host` p93-1. Photographs `review/examples-2026-11-10/58-59`. Not yet: openings in the shell, a closed pavilion of strips, kerfed zones, the diagrid of prototype 2.
- **Pass 94 (v93), the bent shell's joints and cap; the leaning wall's drawings and Notes; the pattern stack deeper** (12 Nov, Opus 5.5; the user: "Expand and go deeper into the bent wood curved plywood designs and finger joints or other joinery options and make the wood cap look better but still structurally sound", then queue steps 1 and 2; `stage94.py` swaps the bent code for the new `bent93.js` and inserts `lean2d94.js`, `stack94.js`). BENT: `S.lat.bJoint` 'finger' | 'dovetail' (neck and head boxes; a dowel every second finger) | 'butterfly' (butted seams, glued bow-tie keys across the outer seams every second station, `keys`) | 'lap' (no cut: the staggered inner layer laps every seam, glued and two screws a station, `screws`); `bClampH` (140), `bCapW` (30): the head and base are clamped between two 24 mm plywood ribs built along the edge in the surface's own frame (Matrix4 basis: along the edge, up the surface, its normal), bolted through every 400 mm (`bolts`), a 30 mm cap board standing `bCapW` past both faces on top and a sole board under the base; the joint drawing and the Check sentences follow the joint; designs Keyed wave (butterfly, narrow strips) and Dovetail sail. LEAN 2D: `leanElevSVG` (every board in elevation, shaded by nearness) and `leanCourseSVG` (outer-leaf courses in plan coloured by height; a section through the middle, one board of each leaf per course) are Render and Course drawing for the leaning wall. NOTES: a third button `#orthoSeg [data-o=notes]` (`window.__notes2d`) on any form sheet or brick wall: `notesSVG()` writes the form's note, its lines, every Check sentence with a coloured mark, what leaves with it and the files; the second button reads Drawing (Course drawing for the leaning wall) on forms. STACK: `brickLayers` is wrapped so a layer of points or a line has `fall` 'smooth' | 'linear' | 'step' | 'ring', `width` (full strength for so many mm), `inv`; points carry a strength (`u,v,s`, negative pulls, `stackPts`); a line may be `smooth` (Catmull-Rom, `stackCurve`) and `closed`; a noise may be `stretch`ed and turned (`angle`) into streaks; the cards gain these controls (`stackCards`, a MutationObserver keeps them through redraws); Shift + scroll on a layer dot changes that point's strength; designs Grapes, Contour, Lace, Dune (`STACK94`). QA `host` p94-1, `brick` p94-2. Not yet: a combine mode between layers (add, max, mask), dragging a whole line, the timber pavilion and the other timber forms reworked (queue items 4 and 5).
- **Pass 95, the form in hand stays; the brick pattern worked on the wall; Pavers above Serial sections** (12 Nov, Fable; the user: "Can the tool stick to the form decided upon in the start tab ... without it defaulting back to a standard wall?", "the control points are few ... confusing what each point does", then a photograph of a wall covered in unlabelled dots; `brick95.js`, `stage95.py`, inserted after pass 94's code before the last `orderTabs();`). **The form**: `brickFormInfer()` reads the form from the state (wall, wave, bays, corner, u, court, tower, or `custom` for anything the Wall step has no name for: a curve or serpentine plan, a cylinder or sphere host, a mirrored hall, a set of several rows); `brickFormSync(force)` writes it and its sizes into `S.brick.form*`; `brickFormNow()` is what the Wall step's controls re-lay (the old `S.brick.form||'wall'` is what sent a composed tower back to a straight wall). `brickForm('custom')` leaves plan and host alone. Forced syncs follow `composeDesign`, `startCase`, `catLoad`, `applyPreset`, `startPlanFrom` and entering brick mode (`setPanelMode` wrapped). `brickDesign` and `brickLayered` are wrapped by `brickFormHold`: `brickFormKeep()` saves the form keys (`FORM_KEYS`, `FORM_BK`) before a design loads and `brickFormGive()` puts them back and re-lays a named form for the new bond, so a design changes the pattern, never the form (a plain single wall still takes the design's own size); `S.brick.keepForm===false` turns that off. `.bform-keep` in the Wall step says which form is in hand. The plan is shared state, so the other panel tabs keep it. **On the wall** (`brickHandlesDraw` replaced): one source is in hand (`BH_SRC`, a click on a dot takes its source) and drawn whole with its reach ring, the others at 40 %; a layer's colour is what it does (`FX_COL`: turn orange, push blue, belly violet, kept open green, second brick pink; the field blue); each source is labelled (`L2 turn 18 deg`). The bar gains **Paint**, **Effect** (`BH_FX`, `bfxSVG`: the elevation tinted where each layer acts, run-length rects) and **Plain wall** (layers removed, `d1`, `d2`, `shiftMax` 0, second group off). A second row `.bhx` (`bhxSync`) edits the layer in hand in place (what it does, amount, reach) or holds the brush. **Probe**: hovering a brick (`bhProbeBrick`, `bhProbeHTML`, `.bh-probe`, `.bh-hl`) says its course and kind, its turn, push and belly as laid, the field's tone there and each layer's share (`L1 push out: 93 % of 60 mm = 56 mm`). **Curves** (bar mode `curve`): a layer `src:'profile'` whose strength is two curves multiplied, `pu` along the wall (7 nodes) and `pv` up it (5 nodes), drawn as two small graphs in the row whose nodes are dragged (`profVals`, `profAt` smoothstep between nodes, `profLayer(make)`, `profGraph`); the first click on Curves adds the layer as a swell in the middle. **Paint**: a layer `src:'paint'` (added to `BRICK_LAYER_SRC`) holds a 96 x 48 grid as one base-36 character a cell in `L.grid` (`paintGrid`, `paintText`, `paintAt` bilinear, `paintDab` with a soft edge, strokes interpolated at half a brush); `brickLayers` is wrapped again to read it and to number layers (`o.n`); one painted layer per effect (`paintLayer(does, make)`), Alt-drag or Erase rubs out; painting 'kept open' opens the bond first. The page's code is not global: in the browser pane drive it by clicks, not by calling functions. QA `brick` p95-1 (335 checks in all). Looked at in the browser pane: a composed six-sided, five-storey building kept through a storey-height slider, two designs, Plain wall and a painted stroke. Not yet: the same direct controls on timber and pavers, profile curves by height and along, dragging the reach ring, dots in 3D, the header's 'turned up to' figure after painting (it reads panel 1), paint resolution following the wall's size.
- **Pass 96, the brick wall worked by hand, deeper** (12 Nov, Fable; the user: "how does a user change the position of the swell or add or remove a swell?", then "section drag so a wall can ... have a belly over a door like at chi she, box select bricks ..., rule by course, two states blended", "Improve brick wall picture option"; `brick96.js`, `stage96.py`; `brick95.js` gained two hooks, `bhxMore(o)` and `bhxLay()`, a `.bh-x` class on its bar buttons and a generic `profTarget(g)` for graph drags). Layers are read up to **eight** (`slice(0,8)` in `brickLayers` and its wrappers; the pattern-stack cards still add up to four). New layer sources, all through one more `brickLayers` wrapper: `boxes` (`pts` 'u0,v0,u1,v1,s;...' with v up, `boxList`, `boxText`: bricks inside a box take the layer's effect times s), `section` (`u0` the station, `w` mm half width, `pv` six nodes base to top: full strength within 0.4 w, fading to nothing at w; made as a belly, or as a step-out on a one-brick bond, which cannot belly), `course` (`n` every nth course from course `off`, `m` every mth brick). A layer may carry `st` 'A' | 'B' and `S.brick.blend` {by 'along' | 'up' | 'field', a, b}: `blendW` fades state A out and state B in. A layer may do a second thing with the same shape (`also`, `amt2`; emitted as an extra entry flagged `x`, not tinted): **Swell is now three dots** (one points layer that steps out 34 mm and turns 12 degrees, plus a faint noise turn), so a swell is dragged, added (Add) or removed (right click). `layer96(src,does,make,def)` finds or makes such a layer. Bar: **Select** (click a brick or drag a box; `.bh-box` rects; scroll on a box for its strength, right click removes; one boxes layer per effect, `BH_BOX`), **Section** (the row holds the section as a graph, base at the left; on the elevation a dot is dragged along the wall and two bars set the width, `.bh-sech`), **Rule**, **Picture** (a built-in picture or an upload through `ombBuiltin` / `ombUse`, what it does, contrast, tone steps, invert). The row for the field (no layer in hand) says what the field is and sets what it does (`brick.op`, `d2`, `shiftMax`). `bhAnnounce(name)` runs after `brickDesign` / `brickLayered`: the first source is taken in hand and the row says what drives the design. The probe adds **Held back** (asked against laid, from the field and the layers; `b.sh0` is the step, `b.shL` the belly, `b.shift` their sum). The header reads every panel (`brickMaxAll`, `brickTurnWords`: 'turned up to X, out up to Y mm'; it had read `B.maxTheta` of panel 1 and never named a step). The elevation of a set draws ONE ground under all panels (`noBg`), so a brick crossing a panel joint is no longer hidden by the next panel's ground (dark stripes every 900 mm on the opening design). The tint skips noise layers. Later the same day: the section is drawn upright in the row (`profGraphV`, nodes dragged sideways, `data-dir="x"`); the row opens with a line that is always in view, **Buildable: every brick rests** with how many bricks the bearing and step rules eased, how much of a belly was built and any opening refused (redrawn after every build through `brickReadout`); the 2D view's bottom padding follows the bar and the row so they never cover the wall; **Wrinkle over a door** (`brickLayered('wrinkle')`, first chip of the pattern stack) is the Belly wall with its door, bellied by one section. QA `brick` p96-1. Looked at and tried with the real mouse in the browser pane (a box drag, the section's dot dragged along). In a bash heredoc on this machine `\\` arrives as `\`: write scripts with the Write tool when they hold backslashes. Not yet: dragging a box to move it, the section drawn as a true vertical section with the door in it, the blend's start and end as handles, cards in the Pattern step for the new sources, undo of a drag as one step.
- **Pass 97, the paved ground on a site** (12 Nov, Fable; the user: "a preselected site with a given grade that the pavers would have to adapt to", "can the pattern and pavers adapt to the slope ... and properly be accessible"; `site97.js`, `stage97.py`). `S.paver.site` ('level' | 'cross' | 'mound' | 'hollow' | 'valley' | 'bank' | 'levels', `SITE_KINDS`), `siteRise` (mm), `siteLevels` (a typed grid of spot levels in mm, a row per line, north first), `siteBy` ('none' | 'slope' | 'height'). `siteZ(P,u,v)` is the height over the plan (u right, v up), `siteSlope` its gradient, `siteRange` its lowest and highest level and its average fall (cached). `paverGenerate` is wrapped: every paver gets `z` (mm above the lowest point) and `sl` (slope %), and `res.paver.site` holds rise, steepest and mean slope, the shares of ground over 5 %, 8.33 % and 12 %, and the route read by `siteRouteRead` (length, rise, steepest running slope, cross fall, lengths over 1 in 20 and 1 in 12, the rise that needs steps; sampled every 250 mm), with the gentlest of the three set routes (`SITE_ROUTES`). `paverGeometry` is wrapped: every vertex is lifted by the site, so the 3D ground is the site (walls stay upright). `paverFallSVG` is wrapped: `siteSVG` draws contours (marching squares, a step that gives ten lines at most) and the route in pieces, green to 1 in 20, amber to 1 in 12, red beyond; `pvDotsSVG` puts dots on the plan for the route (up to seven; dragging one makes the route `custom`; double click adds a point, right click removes) and for the field's points or line. `siteRules(st)` speaks in the Ground step (`.pvsite`, `.pvsite-say`): a walk, a ramp with landings every 9 m, or steps of 150 mm and the ramp length the rise would need; a cross fall over 1 in 50; ground too steep to pave; creep over about 12 %; all first checks against common limits, said as such. `siteApply()` sets `fall` and `fallTo` from the site's average fall (the water check runs down it) and keeps a field of kind `site` in step (`FIELD_KINDS` gains it; `fieldValue` wrapped: flat is light and steep dark, or low to high), so the pattern can follow the ground. `-levels.csv` joins the bundle (`siteCSV`, `bundleFiles` wrapped). Later the same day: where the route is steeper than 1 in 12 its **steps are drawn** across it on the plan, one line per 150 mm of rise, with their count (`st.steps`); contour labels are spread along their lines; a field line that runs along the route is **linked** to it (`pvLinked`): one set of dots, and dragging the route moves the pattern with it (`setRoute(a, link)`). QA `paver` p97-1. Not yet: steps, ramps and terraces built in 3D and scheduled, a route found across the slope, cut and fill, spot levels read from a DXF or a picture, the kerb and drain following the ground's low point.
- **Pass 98, a timber wall drawn in plan** (12 Nov, Fable; the user: "choosing the shape of the wood wall or pavilion"; `plan98.js`, `stage98.py`). `latSurface` takes its foot and head lines from `latPlanZ(K,top,t,Wv)`: the old sines, or with `S.lat.planBy==='drawn'` a Catmull-Rom curve through seven values each (`pb`, `pt`, mm; `latPlanVals`, `latPlanAt`), scaled by `planK`, which the lattice wall's hold now eases with the bows (`eff`). The Timber tab's Form step has 'The shape of the wall in plan' (`.lat-planb`): Draw it yourself (`latPlanStart` samples the sliders' shape so nothing jumps), five shapes (`LAT_PLANS`: S curve, Bay, Hook, Twist, Double wave), Sliders; `latPlanSVG` is the plan with the foot solid and the head dashed, nodes dragged up and down; the line under it says whether the timber takes it (the lattice wall: built at so many % of the shape; the bent shell: strips past the limit, with a button to use the plywood thickness that bends there, `fixply`). It applies to the lattice wall and the bent strip shell (not the slat wall, which has its own bow). The form chips lead with the bent shell and the lattice wall; the others stand under 'Less developed' (`.tm-less`). Found while looking: the bent shell opened from its chip on the lattice wall's default numbers reads 75 % bending against 55 % allowed (the Sail design reads 44 %): the default is not yet a wall that passes its own check. A third drawn curve, `pm` (small dotted nodes), is how far the wall bellies at mid-height along its length (`latPlanBelly`, a sine up the height, added in `latSurface`); a belly drawn too deep shows as bending over 100 % in the header and the Check step. Choosing the bent shell by its chip now sets the plywood to the thickness that bends (a toast says so), so the shell it opens on passes its own check. QA `host` p98-1. Not yet: the same direct controls on the 2D sheet, a closed plan, openings, the stacked and slat forms rebuilt, an engineer's check of any joint.
- **Pass 101, a flat set is one piece however it came about; spot levels on the plan** (12 Nov, Fable; the user, with two photographs: three panels of paving each with its own kerb and benches, then three panels each sloping by itself like a sawtooth: "if you add panels in any 3d panel, they should seamlessly connect together with the pattern continuing between panels", "have the ability to change the elevation marker in plan"; `seam101.js`, `stage101.py`). Pass 87 merged a flat set of paving, ribs or kinetic units only when it was made with the Panels control or by opening the tab; a set that arrived another way was built as separate pieces. Now `generate` is wrapped: whenever `seamMergeMode(panelMode()) && seamOn() && seamFlat()` and the arrangement is not single, `seamMerge(true)` runs before the build, so the set is one piece from any route. Asking for several panels of paving (`panelsSet` wrapped) or giving a set a site (`siteApply` wrapped) turns seamless on, with a toast: framed paving would be separate grounds, and a slope given to each would rise and drop back at every joint. **Spot levels on the plan**: 'Set the levels on the plan' in the Ground step (`.pvsite-lev`) turns any site into a 4 x 3 grid of spot levels sampled from it (`siteToLevels`); `siteMarksSVG` draws a diamond and its level at every grid point (`.pv-lev`, only in Move mode); a marker is dragged up or down (6 mm a pixel) or scrolled on (50 mm a notch) through `siteLevelSet(i,j,z)`; contour labels are left off while markers show. QA `flow` p101-1 (a seamless set made behind the controls is one ground, a framed one stays a set, ribs the same, a sloping framed set becomes one ground): 340 checks in all. A sweep of about 200 brick cases the same evening (every form with every design, every bond with every on-wall tool, openings, the bundle) found no fault. Looked at in the browser pane: three panels of sloping paving as one 36 m ground with twelve markers. Not yet: a set with rows merged and checked by eye, the kerb following the ground's levels, markers for a finer grid, the same for ribs and kinetic units looked at.
- **Pass 102, benches that stay where they are** (12 Nov, Fable; the user: "allow the ability to remove benches in the pavers path. I dont want the benches moving when the control points are moving"; `bench102.js`, `stage102.py`). `permSeats` placed the benches afresh along the route at every build. `S.paver.seatPts` ('x,y,angle;...' in mm, or 'none') pins them: `permSeats` is wrapped to return the pinned list when there is one (`seatList`, `seatText`); `seatPin()` writes the benches as they stand and runs the moment a route point is pressed, added or removed, or a bench is touched. On the plan a bench (`.pv-seat`, through a wrapper of `permSeatsSVG`) is dragged in Move mode or removed with a right click. A change of the bench count, or another set route (`paverRouteSet` wrapped), places them along the route again; a line under the stepper says which state they are in, with 'Remove all benches' or 'Place them along the route again'. QA `paver` p102-1 (341 checks in all).
- **Pass 103, an open bond on the waving wall: the curtain wall** (12 Nov, Opus 5.5; the user's renders of a long folding hit-and-miss brick screen; `dev-tools/scripts/stage103-curtain.py`, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass103.html`). Before this the wave branch of `brickGenerate` ignored `S.brick.open` and divided each course afresh by its own arc length, so as the folds deepened up the wall the courses drifted and the joints broke into jagged seams at every fold. Now, with `hw && gxo>0`: every course puts its bricks on the same PLAN stations (pitch `P = bL + joint + gap`), odd courses half a station along with a closer at the start, a closer at the end; each brick sits on the curve at its station, turned with the slope (`mk`), so the bricks stack in columns and the gap takes up the extra arc (`res.brick.gapMax`, `res.brick.curtain = P`). A first bearing check for this case only (the wave had none): each brick's plan overlap with the course below, reduced by the difference in depth of the two bricks' lines at the overlap, against `bearMin` (`atRisk`, `minBear`, `straight` and `minLap` away from the ends). The bricks are not eased; a fold too deep or tight shows as bricks at risk. Closed-bond waves are unchanged. Example: 9.6 x 3 m, waves 3, a0 120, a1 360, open .25: 1,280 bricks, 0 at risk, least bearing 43 %, 0 straight joints, lap 139, widest gap 137 mm; waves 3.5, a1 300, open .3 leaves 4 bricks in the top course at 39 %. Pictures `review/examples-2026-11-10/83...85`. No QA check yet for pass 103; not republished. Not yet: easing a fold to keep every brick bearing, mortar for the open wave checked, the plinth and roof slab of the reference.
- **Pass 104, the waving wall's folds drawn, rolled or taken from a sound; swells on the wave** (12 Nov, Opus 5.5; the user: "make the wave less standard and more unique / customizable ... include swells", then "can the parametric brick wall take a sound wave of an audio snippet ... for the curve of the wall?"; `dev-tools/scripts/stage104-folds.py`, run after stage103, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass104.html`). `curtainCourse(h,W,H,y,xa,xb,B)` replaces `hostWaveCourse` in both waving-wall branches (identical when `S.host.fold` is 'sine' and there is no swell). `S.host.fold` 'sine' | 'drawn': `S.host.pb` (foot) and `S.host.pt` (head) are offsets in mm through any number of points spread along the wall (`curtainVals`, Catmull-Rom `curtainCR`); a course is the straight interpolation between them by height. Swells: every brick layer src 'points', does 'bulge', not off, adds amt x s x (1 - (d/reach)^2)^2 round each point (`curtainSwells`), so the pattern stack's points layers and their dots on the elevation drive the wave's swells. Wall step (`.curtain104`, after 'How far the top swings', only for the waving wall): Even folds / Drawn folds, `CURTAIN_SHAPES` (Curtain, Drift, Pleats, Gather), Roll new folds (`curtainRoll(seed)`, neighbours within 360 mm), From a sound (an audio file decoded by Web Audio; `curtainFromSound(samples, 25, W)`: RMS loudness in 25 windows traced along the wall, head +-340 mm, foot a third, neighbours held to a slope of 0.5 so swells still fit; `S.host.sound` keeps the file name), an SVG plan with the foot solid and the head dashed whose points are dragged (redraw live, rebuild on release), Add a swell. Measured (9.6 x 3 m, open .25, two 220 mm swells): every shape 0 at risk, 0 straight joints, least bearing 41 to 51 %; a synthetic six-note clip 0 at risk, 47 %. A field turn left on (e.g. after Shadow weave) puts hundreds of bricks at risk on the wave, and the check says so: a curtain wants d1 = d2 = 0. QA 341 green; no pass-104 check yet; the file picker and the drag were not driven by a real mouse; not republished. Pictures `review/examples-2026-11-10/86...88`.
- **Pass 105, the pleated brick curtain pinned in Start** (12 Nov, Opus 5.5; the user: "Pin the design in the tool so I can get to it easily"; `stage105-pin.py`, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass105.html`). `curtainPleats()` loads the pass-104 contender (waving wall 9.6 x 3 m, open bond .25, Pleats folds, two 220 mm swells, field turn off); `startCase('pleats')` runs it and opens Brick courses; the card 'Pleated brick curtain' is the SECOND Start case (QA `flow` p70-1 wants the brick wall first). One click: 1,280 bricks, 0 at risk, least bearing 44 %. QA 341 green. The Bank is the viewer's own browser storage, so the design is pinned as a Start case, not a Bank entry. Not republished.
- **Pass 107, the brick panel simpler and more honest** (12 Nov night, Opus 5.5; the user: the on-wall tool bar "within the 2D portal" should move "into the brick courses panel into a new tab because there are too many controls ... the workflow is way too complicated"; "an audit of the brick panel ... restrictions for the thickness, and length of the brick unless its 3d printed ... no certain 3d form option because its not possible"; `stage107-hand.py`, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass107.html`). (1) The bar (Move ... Plain wall) and its row (the Buildable line, the layer in hand, the layer chips) are created in, or moved to, `#bhHost` in a new Brick courses step **5 By hand** (`data-bstep` 7, shown fifth: `ORD` [1,2,3,4,7,5,6], so Check and Deliver keep their `data-bstep` 5 and 6 and `__brStep(5)` is still Check); `bhSync`/`bhxSync` look the bar and row up document-wide and no longer pad the 2D view; the drawing keeps its dots and drags. (2) `S.brick.unit` 'clay' | 'cast' with chips 'Fired clay brick' / 'Cast or 3D-printed block' heading the Brick step; `DIMS()` gives the sliders' limits: clay 180-300 x 85-120 x 40-90 mm, joint 8-15 (1-4 glued); cast the old 150-500 x 60-250 x 30-150, joint 0-20. Unless the person chose the unit (`unitSet`, set by the chips or a standard format), it is worked out from the sizes (so catalogue blocks such as 152, 356 or 915 mm load as cast); a chosen clay unit is held to its limits with a toast. (3) Brick courses hides the whole-form picker (`.form-pick`) and the element, orientation and clip controls (`.br-na`). The Bank already kept a design whose pin is closed in the header (unpinned, `pinned:false`, deleted only in the Bank): checked in a browser, pin, close, reload. QA: two checks restated with reason (the width drag now 110, a clay brick being at most 120 wide; the nav has seven steps). 341 green. Not republished.
- **Pass 104b, Add a swell places swells where the design wants them** (12 Nov night): the folds block's Add a swell puts its n-th point at `SPOTS` [.3,.62], [.72,.5], [.5,.4], [.15,.45], [.86,.58] (u, v up) and a new layer is 220 mm (was a seeded random spot and 260 mm), so pressing it twice reproduces the pleated curtain's swells. Changed in `pattern-studio.html` and `stage104-folds.py`. Found while recording the tutorial: after Add a swell the pattern-stack cards (`.bly`) are not rebuilt in the Pattern step, so the swell's fields can't be edited there until something else rebuilds the cards (not fixed). QA 341 green. A from-scratch tutorial film of the design is in `review/tutorial/` (`tut_drive.py` drives the real tool with Playwright and the installed Chrome; `tut_comp.py` edits it; `review/tutorial-curved-brick-wall.mp4`). NOTE for any driver: the Brick courses step buttons scroll under the sticky Host / Category / Finish / Deliver bar, and a click there lands on Host, which opens Start: scroll controls to the centre before clicking.
- **Pass 106, the waving wall laid along a line drawn in plan** (12 Nov, Opus 5.5; the user: "Can a curved wall be moved in plan with control points? Or a drawn line be the inspiration for the wall?"; `stage106-path.py`, after stage105, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass106.html`). `S.host.route` 'line' | 'drawn', `S.host.rp` = plan control points 'x,z;...' in mm; `curtainPath(h)` (cached on `rp`) is a Catmull-Rom line with `L`, `at(s)` (point and unit tangent), `R` (tightest bend radius), `pts`. In `brickGenerate` a branch BEFORE pass 103's: each course is the line offset along its left normal by the folds (Even or Drawn, read at s / L) plus the swells (in s and height); bricks stand on stations along the line (pitch brick + joint + gap; odd courses half along; closers at both ends), at the course point, turned to its tangent (any angle); each brick carries `pv` [s0, s1, offset, d offset / ds, s] (added to the `res.bricks.push`), so pass 103's bearing check measures overlap in s and depth along the normal; `res.brick.tight` counts stations whose spacing on the course is under brick + joint (the inside of a bend), added to `atRisk`; `res.brick.path` (m), `pathR`. `curtainPathFit()` sets `S.width` (and `formL`) to the line's length. Wall step: `.path106` after the folds block: Straight / Drawn line, `PATH_SHAPES` (S-curve, Arc, Bay, Snake), a plan whose points are dragged (the view's scale held during a drag; 50 mm steps), Add a point (splits the longest segment), Remove a point, the length and tightest bend, a warning when bricks crowd. Measured with the pleated curtain (Pleats folds, two swells, open .25): S-curve 13 m 1,720 bricks, Arc 14.4 m 1,880, Bay 16.2 m 2,120, Snake 16.3 m (bend 1.2 m) 2,140: all 0 at risk, 0 crowded, 0 straight joints, least bearing 41 to 53 %. The 2D elevation draws the bricks by plan x (a projection, not unrolled), so on a curved line it is foreshortened and the swell dots sit at s along the wall, not under the bricks they move. QA 341 green; no pass-106 check; dragging not tried with a real mouse; not republished. Picture `review/examples-2026-11-10/89`.
- **Pass 108, the Timber tab worked by hand; a wall of timber blocks** (13 Nov, Opus 5.5; the user: "add the by hand panel of controls ... to the timber panel tabs ... create an identical design to this brick wavy wall in the timber tab so the robot places small wood blocks"; `timber108.js` and `stage108-timber.py` in `dev-tools/scripts`, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass108.html`). Timber has seven steps: **By hand** is `data-tstep` 7 shown fifth (`ORD` [1,2,3,4,7,5,6], so Check and Deliver keep 5 and 6). `#bhHost` (the brick bar and its row) is MOVED into Timber's `#tmHandHost` when that step opens (`tmHandShow`) and back to `BH_HOME` when Brick courses or its step nav is clicked. **Lattice family** (`tmLatOn()` = `latActive()` and not the block wall; `bhSlat` now returns it, so the lattice wall and the bent shell get the dots and drags the slat wall had): `latSheetSVG` is wrapped to set `window.SLAT_MAP` for the lattice (`m = W/2 - L/2 sc`, `y0 = m + 34`) or the bent shell (`m = 24`, `y0 = 30`) and draw the field's dots and the paint (`tmOverlay`); Paint out / Paint in / Rub out (`TM_PAINT`, brush `TM_BR`) dab two 96 x 48 grids `S.lat.paintO` / `paintI` (`tmDab`, the brick brush's `paintDab`), and `fieldValue` is wrapped so that while a lattice form stands the main field gets + 0.5 x (out - in), or only the paint with `S.lat.paintOnly`; the first stroke raises a belly under 300 to 400 mm; Clear the paint, Plain wall; the brick-only buttons and row are hidden (`#bhHost.tmlat`). **Block wall** (`blocksOn()` = brick mode, `S.brick.unit === 'wood'`, no timber form): `S.brick.unit` 'wood' is a third unit chip (Timber block: 120-400 x 45-150 x 30-120 mm, glue line 0-4); `timberBlocks()` = `curtainPleats()` then oak blocks 200 x 95 x 45, glue 2 mm, the Arc line (`PATH_SHAPES.arc`, `curtainPathFit`), `S.material = 'oak'`, every timber form off. `tmWhich()`/`tmFam()` give 'blocks' to the Timber tab (its 'any' blocks hide for it); blocks `data-tf="blocks"` in every step: line in plan, fold shapes, height; species and block size; joinery in words (glued bed, a 10 x 90 hardwood dowel into the course below, a glulam sole plate); gap and swells; Check (`tmBlockNums`: count, cut, at risk, least bearing; slenderness and weather notes); Deliver (blocks, cut, dowels, 4.8 m boards, m3, glue kg at 200 g/m2). Form chip 'Block wall', Start case 'Wavy timber block wall' (`tblocks`, after the brick one). The header reads Timber blocks. Measured headless: 3,654 blocks in 63 courses along 14.35 m, 0 at risk, least bearing 56 %, 94 cut blocks. `window.PS_T108` {bundle, blocks, nums, fam, latOn, paintAt}. QA: p84-2 restated (seven steps); 341 green. The robot film of the block wall: `grasshopper/examples/timber-block-video/` (`make_core.py` writes `film_core_t.py` from the brick film's core: 95 x 45 blocks in oak, measure rows skipped, call-outs by share of the courses; `arc-*.csv` exported with `fab.reach` 1500 for seven stations: 0 of 3,654 out of reach with `use_kr120(1300, 1250, 450)`); the frames were NOT rendered on 13 Nov at night (Rhino's viewport capture returned nothing, the screen being locked).
