Later the same night: `robotCSV` is wrapped so the block wall's `-robot.csv` has a `dowel` row (90 mm, at the block's place) after every block above the first course (3,596), and `designName` names the bundle `timber-blocks-*`. `robotKRL` is wrapped too: after each block above the first course, the spindle (`$OUT[2]`) drills 90 mm from the block's top through it into the course below and the dowel driver (`$OUT[3]`) drives the dowel (a three-tool end effector is assumed and said so in the program's header): 3,596 drill cycles, no NaN. `grasshopper/bricks_from_csv.py` run in Rhino 8 through the MCP on the block wall's `-bricks.csv` (BW 95, BH 45): 3,654 closed boxes, 13.1 x 3.1 x 2.96 m.

Pavers has six steps: **By hand** is `data-pvstep` 6 shown fourth (`ORD` [1,2,3,6,4,5]; the nav is rebuilt with class `pv6`, its clicks and the foot's Back/Next are caught in the capture phase and the old `__pvStep` is called for steps 1 to 5; `window.__pvStep` is the new `show`). The pass-100 `.pvbar` is MOVED into the step (`#pvHandHost`, class `pv-in`, static; the view's bottom padding is cleared). `pvPaintDab` is replaced so a stroke paints up to `PV_STR` (Strength, 10 to 100 %), and a second bar (`.pv-box`) draws boxes: drag a rectangle on the plan (`PV_BOXM` 'light' | 'dark' | 'field', `pvBoxPaint` fills the paint grids over the box) to make every stone in it closed, open/planted or the field's again. - **Pass 110, describe it, and pictures, for brick, timber and paving** (13 Nov, Opus 5.5; `describe110.js`, `stage110-describe.py`). A 'Describe it' box heads Brick courses (step 1), Timber (step 1) and Pavers (step 1): `brickDescribe`, `timberDescribe`, `paverDescribe` read a sentence with keywords (no model): the kind (wavy/pleated -> `curtainPleats`; tide, current, swell, second brick, openings, raised, weave -> `brickDesign`, on a plain wall unless a building, corner, court or bays is named; lattice, bent shell, slat, leaning, pavilion, blocks; permeable family or a named layout: tiling ids `herring`, `basket`, `hex`...), the line in plan (arc, S-curve, snake, bay, straight; `descScalePath` scales a drawn line to a length), sizes (`descLen`: '14 m long', '3 m tall', 'rising 900 mm'; `descBy`: '18 by 12 m'), open or closed bond, swells (one to five at the curtain's spots), bonds, glue, waves, twist, belly, species, a site (bank, mound, hollow, valley, cross; a ground with no slope words is set level), benches, fill; what was read is said under the box ('Read as: ...'). Text -> pattern gains 'Or make it in Brick / Timber / Paving' under its brief; Image -> pattern gains 'Use the picture in a material' (brick: turn and push from the picture; timber: `S.lat.pic='hand'`; paving: types from the picture). Timber By hand gains a Picture select (`S.lat.pic`: '' the field, 'hand', or a built-in loaded by `ombBuiltin`): `fieldValue` returns the picture's tone (plus the paint) while a lattice form stands. Timber, step 4 Pattern, for the block wall: 'Designs in timber' (`TM_BLOCK_DESIGNS`, `tmBlockDesign(id)`): Wavy curtain (`timberBlocks`), Shadow weave, Tide, Current, Swell. Each is the brick design on a plain wall (`brickForm('wall')` first, since pass 95 keeps the form in hand), then the timber given back: unit wood, the block (the current one, or 200 x 95 x 45 when the wall was not timber), a 2 mm glue line, the species; a whole-block bond (English, Flemish, header) takes a block two widths and a glue line long so it still closes; every design but Tide gets an open bond of at least 0.2 so the blocks have room to turn (with a 2 mm glue line and no gap they were held to about 9 degrees; open, 22 to 23). - **Pass 112, the twisted brick tower** (13 Nov, Opus 5.5; the user sent the Ceramic Constellation Pavilion, HKU 2017, about 2,000 unique clay bricks on a timber frame, and asked for "that twisted brick form in the tool and in a video"; `twist112.js`, `stage112-twist.py`, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass112.html`). The wall laid along a drawn line (pass 106) takes `S.host.twist` (degrees foot to head, eased: theta = twist (1 - cos pi t) / 2, so seams run as S-curves), `waist` (k = 1 - waist sin(pi t^waistAt)) and `waistAt`: in the drawn-line branch of `brickGenerate` the point function `Cc` is turned and scaled about the line's centroid (`twistOf`, `twXf`), and each brick's bearing interval is moved by how far the twist and waist carry it relative to the course below (`twPv`, with the running sums A, B and the offset's slope). The along-the-line check still read the turning course badly, so `brickGenerate` is wrapped: with a twist or waist on a drawn line, bearing is measured EXACTLY in plan (`twBear`: each brick's bed clipped against every brick of the course below, Sutherland-Hodgman, `twPoly`, `twClip`), replacing `atRisk` and `minBear`. The wave host only runs with a fold amplitude and waves, so the tower uses flat drawn folds (`pb`/`pt` zeros, `a0 = a1 = 1`, `waves = 1`). `twistedTower()`: a 2.7 m square plan with 500 mm eased corners (`TWIST_PLAN`, a closed line), 3.9 m high, open bond 0.3, twist 55, waist 0.14 at 1.2: 1,638 bricks in 52 courses, 0 at risk, least bearing 59 %. Film: `grasshopper/examples/twisted-tower-video/` (`film_tw.py` exec'd after the brick film's `film_core.py` in one namespace: one robot station OUTSIDE each face, four lifts of 13 courses going round the faces, the tower's own cameras, call-outs on the waist and a twisting corner; `base_z` set to 450 / 1,300 / 2,100 mm by height so every placement is in reach; `compose_tw.py` rebuilds the film's order for its counter; `TW_FR`, `TW_OUT` choose the frames and the output).

(a, b in slab units of `size`; hole mm; use plant | seat; count a number or 'fill'), `S.paver.invVoids` = 'name|diameter|use|count;...' (round voids larger than a slab), `invTurn`, `invShort` 'bed' | 'gravel', `invC0`/`invC1` (tone ramp), `invMix` (jitter on the ranking), `invMin` (least share of a cut slab kept), `invEdge` (stone kept round a hole, 60) (`INV_DEF`, `invKeys`). `invRows(P)` is the modules in RANK order (most open first, then plain ones small to large); `invSync(P)` (run before every `paverGenerate`) writes one type per module in that order on the tone ramp (palette, names, heights, types), so the rest of the paver code works by type. `invLay(P,W0,H0,toneAt)` (through a wrapper of `paverCells`): a centred grid of whole slabs; round voids first at the darkest places clear of the route, the edge and each other (1.5 slabs between), cells inside a void left out, cells it crosses cut with `invCut` (a rectangle less a disc: each run of the rectangle's edge outside the circle closed by the arc; four corners when the disc sits on the slab's middle), each cut piece taking one plain 1 x 1 slab from stock, pieces under `invMin` left as gravel collar; edge strips cut the same way when the ground is not whole slabs; holed modules most open first, each at the darkest free place its shape fits (both ways round when `invTurn`), never on the route, until its count runs out; plain modules largest first at the lightest places (route first), the fill module last; what is left has no slab (`short`) and is planted or gravelled. `INV_LAST` -> `invAfter` -> `G.paver.inv` {u, nx, ny, cells, rows [{name, asked, placed, cut, left, why, moved, tone}], voids, vrep, short, cutVoid, cutEdge, noStock, follow (correlation of openness and darkness), need, voidCells}. Pavers carry `inv`, `use`, `hole`, `cut`, `mod` (the module's name); voids, short places and collars go in `G.paver.planted` with flags (`inv`, `dia`, `seatV`, `gravel`, `short`). Wrappers: `gardenTrees` (a tree in every planted void of 900 or more), `gardenPlant` (a seat hole of 300 or more holds a stool cylinder; no chance stools), `gardenBed` (gravel, a round bench with a timber top, a steel edging ring, one grass clump on a short place), `paverElevSVG` (`.inv-over`), `paverFamily`, `bundleFiles` (`-inventory.csv`, `invCSV`). Panel: `.inv128` in the Modules step (body class `pv-inv` hides the module bar): slab unit and thickness, a card per module (name, size, hole, use, count or 'fills the rest', what was laid), the voids, Fit the counts to the ground (`invFit`), Counts from the pattern (`invFromField`), turn, where stone runs out, loosen, tones, and the report as sentences (`invRules`, `pr-*`). Start case 'Counted roof garden' (`invroof`: 21 x 14 m, 500 slabs, five holed modules, long and plain slabs, three tree pits and two round benches, planting along two attractor curves, a winding route kept closed, a parapet): 1,176 places, 1,069 slabs, 28 cut, every count met. `window.PS_T128` {gen, geom, start, lay, cut, rows, csv, rules, fit, fromField, report, stat}. - **Pass 129, the pocket park on the inventory rule** (18 Nov, Fable; T2; `park129.js`, `stage129-park.py`; the engine changes are in `inv128.js`). A plain module may be `use` 'path' (laid only on the route, largest first, a 1 x 1 fill one last to close what the slabs leave) or 'edge' (a 1 x 1 laid on every free place beside a bed or a round void); rank order is holed, edging, plain small to large, path (lightest). `S.paver.invBeds` = planted places: the darkest free places off the route (un-jittered tone), tidied so single places go and notches fill, each a whole slab place (`bedCell`, `deep` when all four neighbours are bed; trees only on deep places, by `treeGap`), so nothing is cut at a bed's edge. `S.paver.invSeats` = bench pairs: at even stations along the route, one each side at half width + 380, turned to the route, backs outward (`invBench3D`, `invBenchSVG`; the places under a bench take no bed, void or hole). Modules larger than one place are ranked in ten bands of tone and laid row by row inside a band, so they pack without single gaps. `invKerb3D`: the kerb is units of about 900 with a joint, added through the bed hook inside `paverGeometry` (a marker in `planted`, `P.edge` set to none for the call), so on a site every unit takes its own level (a 10.8 m side rises 744 mm on a 1.2 m cross fall). `parkStart` is replaced: 15.9 x 10.8 m at 300, `PARK2_INV` (edging sett fill, sett fill, half slab 120, plank 70, square 80, large 56, path sett fill, path slab 48: five sizes), 300 bed places at four field points (attract field, `invert:true` = dark at the points; drag a blue dot to move a bed), two bench pairs, kerb: 685 pavers, every count met, nothing cut. The old `.park123` block hides for this layout. QA `paver` p129-1.
- **Pass 130, the topography layer** (18 Nov, Fable; T2b first slice; `topo130.js`, `stage130-topo.py`, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass130.html`). **Ground**: `S.paver.site` 'spots' with `sitePts` 'u,v,z;...' (z mm): `topoSurface` is a thin-plate spline through them (inverse distance if it cannot be solved), cached on a 48 x 32 grid; `topoToSpots()` turns any site into twelve spots; `topoFromImage` reads a picture as a levels grid (light is high). `siteZ0` is the ground as surveyed; `siteZ` is the PAVING (what pavers, 3D, route reading and water use): the same, or with `S.paver.grade==='plane'` one least-squares plane with its fall held between `gradeMin` and `gradeMax` (`topoPlane`, fitted on cell midpoints so cut and fill balance). `topoGrid` (cached): existing and proposed on a grid of about 250 mm, cut and fill in m3, the share of ground in five slope bands. Each paver carries `z0`, `zp` (mm above the lowest point) and `fd` (the bearing it falls toward). **Water** (`topoWater`, through a wrapper of `paverCascade` when a site is on): a grid of about 400 mm; each cell's intake is the capacity of the pavers and planting whose centre lies in it; `topoRoute` gives each cell its receiver (steepest neighbour below; a level cell hands on toward the nearest way down or the edge; a closed level floor drains to its own middle) and an order with donors first; rain plus inflow less intake is handed on; what reaches an edge leaves by that side, what reaches a cell with no receiver ponds, unless a drain is there (`drains` 'auto' puts up to `drainN` at the worst low points, `drainPts` are the user's). `w.flow` {rainLs, infLs, drainLs, edgeLs by side, pondLs, drains [{x,y,z,ls,n}], ponds, lines, low, bal}; `w.ok` = nothing ponds. `topoBare` is flow accumulation on the bare ground, and a field of kind site with `by:'water'` (`P.siteBy==='water'`, the By water button) is dark where water gathers. **Drawings**: `topoPlanSVG(G)` is a grading and drainage sheet (surveyed contours dashed and proposed solid, a step that gives 26 lines at most, every fifth heavy and labelled along the line reading uphill, spot levels as a cross in a circle with the level in metres above `datum` and the surveyed level in brackets, fall arrows with % and 1:n, flow lines, drains with cover and invert levels, the channel on the low edge, the route by slope, section line A-A and the section drawn below with its exaggeration, scale bar, north point, legend, title block); the 2D view's third button **Grading** (`window.__grade2d`, shown for paving on a site); `topoDXF` (OUTLINE, CONTOURS_EX, CONTOURS_PR, SPOT_LEVELS, FALLS, DRAINS, FLOW, ROUTE, SECTION_LINE, TEXT); bundle: `-grading-plan.svg`, `-grading-plan.dxf`, `-ground-section.svg`, `-drainage.csv`, and `-levels.csv` now paver, x, y, existing_m, proposed_m, cut_fill_mm, slope, bearing. **On the plan**: spot markers (`.tp-spot`) are dragged, scrolled on (50 mm, Shift 10) and removed with a right click; Add a spot / Add a drain arm the next click (`TOPO.arm`). **Panel**: `.topo130` after the site block (spot buttons, a picture, typed levels, datum, grading with least and most fall, drains, section, channel, and `topoRules` as sentences: slope bands, grading with cut and fill, the storm in L/s and where it goes, ponding, a pipe size per drain by Manning as a first check). **3D**: a grate at each drain; the gravel bed under the permeable family is now a subdivided sheet (it was one box, level between its corners, and hid a hollow). A **Glass** tab after Perforated image (`TAB_ORDER`, `FLOW` Category, `SUB`, `HUE`, `LEDE`; not a `TAB_MODE`: `S.glass.on` stands the glass up like a whole form, the panel mode is set to cut and `generate` returns a plain sheet `{glassG:true}` while it stands; `glassEnter()` / `glassLeave()`; a user's click on a panel tab, Timber or Start sets it aside; the tab nav steps over Glass unless it stands). `S.glass` (`GLASS_DEF`): `treat` 'etch' | 'blast' | 'frit' | 'kiln' | 'film', `cols` (pane widths, left to right) and `rows` (heights, bottom to top) as typed lists so panes need not be alike, `mull`, `doors` (panes of the bottom row), `t`, `contrast`, `bright`, `invert`, `fritShape` 'dot' | 'line', `fritPitch`, `fritMin`, `fritMax`, `fritStag`, `levels` (1 to 3 tones or stages), `cut`, `blastDepth`, `kilnDepth`, `kilnSoft`, `filmMax`, `bird`, `birdGap` (50), `birdDot` (6), `face`, `tint`. `glassLayout(K)` -> {W, H, panes [{n, name, i, j, x, y, w, h, door}]}; `glassSync()` makes the panel the opening. `glassModel()` (cached on the state and a signature of the tone field; the cache is fields of `GLASS`, never a new object, because the tab's hooks live on it): a grid of about 5 mm over the WHOLE opening (`A` how much each place is treated, `pane`, `tone`), so the design runs behind the mullions; light is treated, dark stays clear. Etch and blast quantise the tone; film is the tone; kiln is the tone box-blurred twice within the panes (`hmap`, 0 to 1 of `kilnDepth`); frit is ONE lattice over the opening (60 degree stagger for dots), each mark sized by the tone, kept 8 mm inside its pane, the spacing opened if more than 130,000 marks. **Birds**: `glassDist` is a chamfer distance from every clear place to the nearest treated one or pane edge; the widest clear gap is twice its largest value; with `bird` on, markers go on a lattice of 0.86 gap where the glass is clear, then up to three finer passes where a gap wider than `birdGap` remains, never two closer than half a gap. Per pane: area, treated share, widest gap before and after, marks, weight (2.5 kg/m2 per mm). `glassCanvas(M,maxW,mode)` draws it ('' the look, 'print' black on white, 'gap' red where clear glass is too wide); `glassSVG(isOrtho)` is the elevation (a dark room behind by day, lit at Night) or the pane drawing (numbered panes, sizes, treated share, doors, the red gap map); `glassBuild()` after `rebuildScene`: a glass box per pane, the treatment as a `CanvasTexture` plane on face 1 or 2 (emissive at Night), a displaced plane for a kiln relief, aluminium mullions 120 deep, a room plane behind; `userData.glass`, `window.PS_GLASS`. `glassRules()` (first checks, said as such): bird gap, frit fired in toughening, frit over 60 %, a dot under 2 mm, dots that touch, etch masks, carving over a quarter of the thickness, carved glass not toughened, kiln relief and kiln size, doors as safety glass, the largest float sheet, panes over 150 kg. Bundle (`glassFiles`, replacing the panel's files): `-panes.csv`, `-elevation.svg`, `-pane-drawing.svg`, per pane `-Pn-frit.svg` (vector marks, groups frit and bird-markers) or `-Pn-mask.png` / `-Pn-heightmap.png` (seen from outside, up to 4096 px, the dpi in the README), `-Pn-mould.stl` for kiln (`glassMouldSTL`: the relief turned over on a 20 mm base, a closed binary STL), `README-glass.txt`. Four designs (`GLASS_DESIGNS`, `glassDesign`): Entrance in frit, Etched drift, Kiln-formed ripple, Carved rings; Start case 'Glass entrance' (`glass`). The picture block is hosted in the tab (`GLASS.picHost`). `window.PS_T131`. Not yet: glass on a plan or a facade of several storeys, laminated build-ups, a double-glazed unit drawn in section, a solar and light transmission figure, frit colours, text set on the glass from the tab.
- **Pass 132, the attractor framework** (18 Nov, Fable; T4 and T5; logic in `knowledge/flux-logic/attractor-curves.md`; `attr132.js`, `stage132-attractor.py`, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass132.html`). A field kind `'curves'` ('Attractor (shaped)' in `FIELD_KINDS`), so EVERY material reads it as its field with no code of its own. `S.field.curves` (`ATTR_DEF`, declared before `fieldDefaults` because the defaults are read while the controls are built): `pts` (up to six curves 'u,v;u,v|...', v up), `wts` (a weight per curve, -2 to 2; minus pushes), `smooth` (Catmull-Rom through the points), `closed`, `measure` 'dist' | 'along' | 'side' | 'tangent', `combine` 'nearest' | 'sum' | 'sumd' | 'max' | 'mul' | 'diff', `norm` 'reach' | 'bounds', `reach` (mm), `fall` 'linear' | 'ease' | 'gauss' | 'ripple' (`wave` mm) | 'step' (`steps`) | 'noise' (`rough`), `lo` (value far away) and `hi` (value at the curve; swap to invert), `region` (a polygon 'u,v;...'), `fade` (mm), `out`. `attrCache(P,W,H)` measures every curve on a grid of about 14 mm (signed distance, t along, tangent angle); `attrRead` interpolates (distance by its size, except right at a curve where the signed values give the true distance); `attrValue(P,u,v,W,H)` is the chain normalise -> falloff -> combine -> range -> region; with any negative weight the result is centred on mid grey so a push lowers what a pull raises. `attrAt(u,v,W,H)` -> {k, d, side, t, ang, w, val} for an act that wants a direction (nothing uses it yet). `attrControls(parent,onSet)` in every copy of the Field picker: a pad showing the field with the curves on it (drag a point, double click adds one to the curve in hand, right click removes, a row per curve with its weight, Add / Remove a curve, Draw a region), then the chain in order as controls; `attrNote` is the field's note (and warns beyond three attractors, a flat range, a range that reaches 0). `brickSources` gains the first curve, so the dots on the brick elevation, the paver plan and the timber sheets move it. Choosing a field kind now refreshes the controls at once (the segment only scheduled a build, so a kind's own controls did not appear until something else refreshed). Bundle: `-attractor.csv` and a line in the README. `window.PS_T132`. QA group `attractor` (added to `ALL_GROUPS`), p132-1. Not yet: acts that use the direction (a unit turned to the tangent, facing), per-channel curves and ranges (one attractor driving two things differently), blend modes between two attractor fields, on-drawing editing in the cut, tile, perforated, rib and kinetic views (they use the pad), distance and t columns in the schedules.

- **Pass 134, a model of the building to make** (18 Nov, Fable; T7b FIRST SLICE only; `model134.js`, `stage134-model.py`, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass134.html`). `S.mass` (`MASS_DEF`): `shape` 'box' | 'L' | 'U' | 'tower', `w`, `d`, `notch`, `sides`, `storeys`, `storeyH` (real mm), `scale` (50, 100, 200, 500), `t` (sheet), `kerf`, `sheetW`, `sheetH`, `tab`, `view` '' | 'sheet' | 'assembly'. `massFoot(K)` is the footprint, counter-clockwise; `massModel()` (cached): each corner is `square` (90 degrees inside), `inside` (270), `open` or `reflex`; a wall a side with its outer face on the footprint and its thickness inward: at a square corner the wall ending there owns the EVEN fingers and the wall starting there the ODD ones (`fe:'even'`, `fs:'odd'`; `nF` odd, at least 3, about three thicknesses each), at an inside corner the first wall runs past by `t` and the next butts, at an open corner both are cut back by t / tan(angle / 2); a floor plate a level (`levels`: F0 a thickness above the foot, the floors, ROOF two thicknesses below the top) = the footprint offset inward by `t` with a tab at every slot; slots in every wall at every level. Kerf: a tab is drawn `tab + kerf`, a slot `tab - kerf` by `t - kerf`, a finger half a kerf wider each side. Engraved: panel joints every `S.width` along each wall, and the storey lines; a name on every part with the wall it meets. Parts are shelf-packed on sheets (turned if that fits; a part larger than the sheet is named). `massSheetsSVG` (the 2D view), `massSheetSVG(M,k,true)` for the cutter (red hairline CUT, blue ENGRAVE), `massAssemblySVG` (plates stacked, walls drawn out, five numbered steps), `massDXF` (CUT, ENGRAVE, LABELS, SHEET), `massCSV`, `massSTL` (the massing as one closed solid at scale), `massRules`, `massFiles` (added to the bundle while a model view is showing; 'Download the model files' zips them alone). Slat wall: a joint moved off a joint of the course below could land on another; it now goes to the nearest stud with no joint below (a bay or more either way, the slat still on two studs). Bent strip shell: seams are walked with the sag (k w^2 / 8, k the worst of five stations and 25 heights) measured over the whole width of the outer strip AND of the inner strip it closes (inner strips span from the middle of one outer strip to the middle of the next and had never been checked), held to 97 % of the tolerance; a strip's reported sag is the worst of its edges and middle. Two tests restated with reason: the slat foot is measured where two slats overlap through `R.zAt` (slats are turned in plan by default since pass 91, so their middles say nothing), and the brick pattern stack's closed smooth curve is probed on the curve (it bows about 310 mm off the chord between two points, which is right). - **Pass 146, the path is graded; stairs are a choice; the outline holds everything** (19 Nov, Fable; the user: "dont have the stairs be a default ... the pavers need to naturally slope down the path. ideally its a 1:20 accessible slope ... if someone changes the shape of the paver corners, adjust the path ... dont have benches outside it. dont automatically add so many benches"; edits in `steps141.js` and `bound144.js`, no new file). `S.paver.walkMode` 'off' | 'grade' | 'steps' (`walkMode(P)`; '' falls back to the old `walkOn` = steps). **grade**: the route's levels are eased (repeated 1-2-1 smoothing, ends held) until nothing is steeper than 1 in 20 or the line is one even fall end to end, so the result is the gentlest path the ground allows (`E[0]` {kind 'grade', max, over5, over12}); the paving is level across the route's width and the ground beside it is blended back over 0.9 to 3.5 m (six times the move, smoothstep); no walls, rails or steps; the site's own route reading (`st.route.seg`, max, over5, over12) is rewritten from the graded levels so the plan's colours and sentences tell the truth; `walkRules` says walk / ramp / too steep. The segment 'The route on a slope' replaces the old switch, with 'Run the route straight down the slope' (a straight line through the middle along the gradient). The Start case 'Paved ground on a slope' is a cross slope of 600 with a corner-to-corner graded path at 1 in 30, no steps, no benches. `bndFit()` (on every outline change: preset, drag end, drawn, typed): route points and the field's points or line that lie outside are moved to the nearest point of the outline, 3 % in (`P.route='custom'`); benches outside the outline are dropped (`permSeats` wrapped, `INV_LAST.seats` filtered); a paving Start case opens with `seats` and `invSeats` 0.

- **Pass 149, the building's faces further; spot levels by Shift-drag** (19 Nov, Fable; the user chose 'Building faces', then: "make sure the 3d model changes when elevation markers change"; `faces149.js`, `stage149-faces.py`, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass149.html`; the spot-level part is in `left148.js`). Elevation markers were measured headless by comparing the 3D picture before and after: scrolling or dragging either kind (`.tp-spot` spot levels, `.pv-lev` grid levels) rebuilds the 3D ground. A plain drag of a spot marker MOVES it and only a scroll changed its level, which can read as nothing happening, so Shift + drag up or down now sets its level (6 mm a pixel, rebuilt every 140 ms while dragging; `L148.lv`), and a line under the list says so. Faces: kept designs are saved in this browser (`localStorage` `ps-kept-faces`: the tile as a PNG data URL with its size, colour and kind; `faceXSave`, `faceXLoad` at boot, `faceXForget(k)`; one over 900 KB is not stored). `faceXMake()` ('Make the picture's panels') turns the first run of picture faces into the tool's own set: perforated (or tiles if that is the mode), `arr` grid, columns = the panels along the run, rows = the panel rows, the picture across the set, seamless; never more than 48 columns or 96 panels: beyond that each made panel stands for several building panels and the toast says how many; the hole spacing is opened to about sqrt(area x 1.15 / 22,000) so the picture reads at the building's scale and the view can draw it (the mid-rise: 37 x 2 panels of 1800 x 10800, holes 274 apart, 16,638 holes); `FX149.made`, `-panels-on-faces.csv` (which face each column is on). `massOBJ` is wrapped once more: a face group is `face_W<n>_picture | composite | screen | kept1..3` and a screen face has `glazing_behind_W<n>` (a quad a bay a storey, 200 in front of the wall). `window.PS_T149`. - **Passes 150 to 153** (19 Nov, Fable; the user's list: one face at real panel size, the paver ground outside an outline, serial sections with an opening and along a drawn curve; `faces150.js`, `water151.js`, `open152.js`, `curve153.js` with `stage150-face.py` ... `stage153-curve.py`, backups `pattern-studio.before-pass150...153.html`). **150, one face of a picture run at its real panel size**: `faceXMakeFace(i, firstStorey)` ('Make this face at real size', a select of the picture faces and a first storey): columns = the face's own bays, panels = the building panels, at most 240 at once (a taller face is made in bands of whole storeys from the storey asked); `S.omb.crop` {cols, rows, pw, L, H, u0, u1, v0, v1} is the face's share of the run's picture, valid only while the set matches (`faceXCrop()`); `uvFor`, `fieldW`, `fieldH` and `fitKeyNow` are wrapped so the picture is still fitted to the WHOLE run and the set reads only its share; `faceXMake` clears the crop and both give the hole spacing back to what it was before widening it (`FX150.p0`). QA `assembly` p150-1. **151, an outlined ground**: `topoWater` is restated (the text of pass 130's with a mask: cells whose centre is outside the outline get no rain and no intake, water reaching one has left the paving by the nearest side, totals are over the cells inside; `flow.inside` is the share); a last `paverFallSVG` wrapper puts a path `.pv-out` (the rectangle less the outline, in the stage colour) under the overlays, so the plan no longer shows the rectangle outside as ground. `window.PS_T151`. QA `paver` p151-1. Not done: the 3D gravel bed of the permeable family may still reach the rectangle. **152, an opening through the ribs** (`S.serial.open`, `oU` share along the wall, `oW`, `oH`, `oSill`; contour ribs; `opnApply(st,res)` after `serialGenerate`): in rib terms (a along, c across) the two ribs that edge it (`iA`, `iB`, on a strip pair with zero waste: iA even, iB odd, so each trimmer's strip partner is a cut rib) run on as trimmers at the full depth `Dl` across it (`r.trim`, their `v` changed, so nest and lists follow); the ribs between are `r.cut` [A0, A1] with `r.pieces`; at least two whole ribs stay each side, a piece under 300 is not made (upright ribs: a door to the base; an opening as tall as the ribs keeps a 300 head); a fixing station is added 120 outside each jamb and more until every piece has two; rods at stations inside the opening are counted in two lengths (`O.segs`, `rodsFull`, `rodsPart`, nuts, spacers), rails the same; `Sr.opening` {iA, iB, A0, A1, cA, cB, Dl, cut, pieces, boards, inside, added, weak, w, h, door, screws} or {refused}. `serialGeometry` is wrapped: the original draws the uncut ribs and the stations outside, the wrapper adds the cut pieces (with rail notches), a jamb board across each row of cut ends, and the split rods or rails with a nut and washer at every end. `serialElevSVG` draws the opening; `serialFixText` says it (`opnText`); `-opening.csv`. Block `.opn152` in the tab. QA `serial` p152-1. **153, ribs along a line drawn in plan** (`S.serial.path` 'line' | 'drawn', `S.serial.rp` unit points 'x,w;...', w toward the front; `crvPath(R,W)`: Catmull-Rom scaled so its length is the wall's width, turned so its ends lie along the wall, centred, nowhere behind the wall's plane; `at(s)`, `kap(s)`, `Rmin`, `chord`, `bow`). `serialGeometry` is wrapped last: the finished geometry is split (`hostTess`) and bent (`crvBend`: x is distance along the line, z is off it to the left), so everything follows, the opening too. `crvApply` writes `Sr.curve`: for upright ribs a row a rib (place, angle, radius, tip gap = pitch (1 - k depth) - t, back and front spacer lengths), `clash` (tips under 6 mm apart: the FRONT must be the inside of the bend for tips to close), rods rolled in lengths with couplers and a warning under 50 rod diameters (a rule of thumb, said as one), rails sawn to the line in pieces; for level ribs `cutCurved` and the blank width (the nest no longer applies, and says so). The elevation stays unrolled. `-ribs-on-line.csv`. Block `.crv153` (Straight, Arc, S curve, Bay, Hook, a plan whose points are dragged, Add / Remove a point). `window.PS_T153`. QA `serial` p153-1. Not done: the nest for level ribs cut to the line, holding a bend so tips never meet (it is counted, not held), the base channel drawn faceted, a closed line, a set of several panels. 364 checks. Looked at headless only: `review/examples-2026-11-18/45...47`.
- **Pass 154, the rib leftovers** (19 Nov, Fable; the user chose 'Rib leftovers'; `ribs154.js`, `stage154-ribs.py`, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass154.html`). **Cross-cuts on the nest**: `r154Xcuts(Gk)` finds every cut rib's place on its strip (side 1 = the zero-waste complement, measured from the strip's far edge); `serialNestSVG` is wrapped to shade the part that comes out (`.serial-waste`) and draw a dashed saw line where a rib is cut through (`.serial-xcut`, `R.xcuts`); `serialDXFText` gains a CROSSCUTS layer. **The base channel at a door**: `serialGeometry` is wrapped outermost and now does the bending itself (it calls the older chain with `curve:null`, so pass 153's wrapper stands aside): with a door, rods and a base, `r154Clip(g, x0, x1, yMax)` splits every triangle below the ribs' feet at the two trimmer faces, drops what lies between and closes each cut end with a plate (`O.baseCut`); the text says the channel stops at each trimmer. **A bend is eased** (`S.serial.holdBend`, default true; a switch in `.crv153`, upright ribs only): when rib tips would meet, `serialGenerate`'s wrapper bisects `r154Ease(rp, k)` (each point moved toward its place on the straight line between the ends) for the largest k with no clash and re-runs `crvApply` on it; `Q.held` (share of the bow built), `Q.asked` (ribs that would have met), `Q.rpUsed` (the line built; `r154Path(Gk)` reads it for the 3D and the nest). p153-1 sets `holdBend:false` where it wants the clash counted. **Level ribs on a line are nested** (`r154Bands(Gk)`): each rib is a curved band (back = the line, front = back + depth along the left normal) in as many pieces as the widest band needs to fit the sheet's length; bands of one piece are stacked back to front, each raised until its lower edge clears the upper edge of the one before by 8 mm (64 bins across), in columns while the sheet has room, then a new sheet; rod holes, rail notches and an opening's cross-cuts ride along; `Q.nest` {sheets, pieces, left, tall}, and `Sr.sheets`, `Sr.sheetLeft`, `Sr.cutWaste` 0 and `Sr.zeroWaste` false follow, so the header and caption tell the truth; `r154BandsSVG` / `r154BandsDXF` (SHEETS, CUTS, HOLES, NOTCHES, CROSSCUTS, LABELS) replace the strip nest in the Nest view and the bundle. Sample (2.4 m wall, S curve, 57 ribs): 15 sheets, 60 % of the sheet left, which is what curved bands cost. `window.PS_T154`. QA `serial` p154-1. Looked at headless: `review/examples-2026-11-18/49...51`. Not done: a lintel sized, more than one opening, a closed line, a set of several panels, curved bands turned or mixed to pack tighter, the assembly sheet naming cut pieces (its weights already follow the cut ribs' area).
- **Pass 155, a real building as the massing example; the improver agent** (19 Nov, Fable; the user: "create an agent that improves the tools capabilities, design output quality and USE 9_2226_Emmendweid.3dm FOR THE massing model example"; `site155.js`, `stage155-site.py`, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass155.html`). The Rhino file (in the user's ARCH 533 Tutorial 9 folder, 73 MB) was opened through the Rhino MCP and measured once; `rhino3dm` cannot load on this machine (an Application Control policy blocks its DLL), and the tool does not read a .3dm. `MASS_SITES.emm`: a 41 x 16 m block turned 5 degrees, two storeys of 4 m, windows per face as [x from the corner, width, sill, head] (long faces ten of 1558 every 3895 from 2193; short faces four of 1400 every 3500 from 2050; 600 to 3400 in each storey: 56 in all), a hipped roof (`roof` {ix 12300, iy 4800, rise 4000, deck 1000}: four slopes from the eaves to a rectangle, a deck sunk 1 m in it), the eight nearest neighbours as [height, outline in metres] and six trees, all in the building's own frame (x along the long face). `S.mass.site` 'emm'; `siteNow()` is live only while the massing still is that building (box, the same sizes and storeys), so changing the massing sets the example aside without an error. `massModel` wrapped (it must NOT call `faceXCodes`, which calls `massModel`): the bay windows of a glazed face are replaced by the building's own on EVERY face (`slots` flagged `win` and `site`, clear of fingers and floor slots), `fc.windows`, `fc.cutPanels` (panels a window crosses) and `fc.gonePanels` (wholly behind glass), `M.site` {wins, cut, gone}; a key change forces pass 135's body to run again (`M.p135G={}`). `faceXCanvas` wrapped: windows are painted on every face picture except a screen (`siteWinPaint`; `rec.w155`), so the 3D and the unfolded sheet show them. `massBuild` wrapped: glazed and plain faces get a wall picture with the windows, then the roof (`roof`, `roof-deck`), the neighbours (`context`), trees with see-through crowns (`tree`) and a wide ground; the camera is set once a design (`SITE155.camK`). `massOBJ` wrapped: `roof_slopes`, `roof_deck`, `windows_W<n>`, `context_<n>`. `siteWinCSV` is `-windows.csv` (every window, then per face the panels cut); `massRules` says where the building came from and how many panels the windows cut (warned over half, with the windows' own rhythm). `siteLoad(id, own)` (snap off, 1:100, 3D on, faces d,c,s,c; `own` sets a 1000 x 2000 panel), `siteLeave()`. Start case 'Building from a Rhino model' (`emm`, placed before the first Developing case: p70-1 wants every Developed card first), and 'Rhino example building' / 'Leave the example' in the model block (`.site155`). `window.PS_T155` (with `view(theta, r, phi, targetY)` for pictures). With the perforated screen at 1000 x 2000: 456 panels, 256 cut by a window. QA `assembly` p155-1. Pictures `review/examples-2026-11-18/52...54`. The agent: `.claude/agents/pattern-studio-improve.md` (repo root; a running session only knows the agents loaded when it started, so call it by name from a fresh session, or give a general-purpose agent its file to read first): it picks the weakest thing, builds it as a numbered pass, measures, renders and looks, tests, writes the entry, and does not publish. Not yet: panels actually cut round the windows (they are counted and painted, the panel generator does not yet know the openings); a panel width chosen to follow the windows; the roof in the laser model; the unfolded-faces sheet draws faces taller than true (older than this pass); the tool reading a massing file itself (OBJ or DXF); the interior walls, floors and glazing frames of the Rhino model (left out on purpose); only headless pictures were looked at.
- **Pass 156, panels cut round the windows of the example building** (19 Nov, Opus 5.5, the improver agent with its target given; `cut156.js`, `stage156-cut.py`, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass156.html`). Before this the panels of a design face ran straight through the glass and the windows were painted over them. New `S.mass` keys (`CUT156_DEF`, filled by `cutKeys()`): `winClear` (20, mm clear of the window), `winWeb` (0 = by the sheet: the larger of 8 mm, 2.5 x thickness and `perf.minWeb`; `cutWeb()`), `sliverMin` (100). `cutRet()` is the return (`S.lock.ret`, 25). `cutTrace(W,H,rects)` is a rectangle less rectangles as closed loops (outer counter-clockwise, holes clockwise; a grid of the cut lines, boundary edges chained, collinear points merged), so a notch, an L, a panel split in two or a panel with a hole all come out of one routine; a panel with no material left is behind glass and left out. `cutPlan(width)` is pure (no design): every face at a panel width, each window x storey expanded by the clearance, every panel it crosses with its `rel` openings, loops, the narrowest strip beside the opening (`strip`, `sliver` under `sliverMin`) and a signature (panel size and openings to the mm). `cutModel()` (cached on the site, sizes, keys, face codes, web, return and `G`): blank types over the DESIGN faces only (`d`; the screen `s`, composite and glazed faces are not cut), named B1... by count; per type the blank (`cutBlank`: the cut line moved into the opening by the return on every edge inside the panel, fold lines on the finished edge, `clash` when the opening is narrower than two returns), holes kept (bounding box clear of every opening by the web) and holes left solid (within the web, not inside the opening). `cutFit()` tries every whole number of panels from 600 to 2000 mm wide on the long faces and keeps the one with fewest slivers, then fewest types, then fewest cut panels; it says what it costs on the short faces; `cutFitApply()` sets `S.width` (the button 'Panels that follow the windows'; never applied by itself). The face picture (`faceXCanvas` wrapped, `cutPaint`, `rec.w156`): re-tiled from `massTile`, a band the web wide left solid round each cut (sheet designs), the opening dark, the window drawn in it (frame, glass, mullion), the return as a lit band on the jambs and sill and a shadowed one at the head, every cut panel outlined; the 3D and the unfolded sheet both use it. `massRules` wrapped: pass 155's 'are cut by a window' sentence is replaced by the cut, the types, the panels behind glass, the holes left solid (`pr-warn` when types pass the larger of 12 and a third of the cut panels), and a sliver warning with the width that removes them, or a `pr-ok`. `siteWinCSV` wrapped: its per-face table reads the clearance count with types and slivers. Bundle: `-cut-panels.csv` (face, column from the left corner seen from outside, row from the ground, x0, y0, blank type, size, outline loops in the panel's own mm, window ids `W1-1.3` = face, storey, window, clearance, return, holes kept and left solid, strip; then a row per type), `-cut-blanks.svg` (each type flat: red cut, blue dashed fold, grey holes, the opening dashed; holes drawn for the first type only when there are over 45,000), `-cut-blanks.dxf` (types side by side, layers CUT, FOLD, OPENINGS as CIRCLE where a hole is round, LABELS), a README paragraph. 2D: `S.mass.view==='blanks'` draws the blanks sheet. Controls `.cut156`, a SIBLING after `.site155` (that block treats any other button inside it as Leave): a line of numbers, Panels that follow the windows, Cut blanks, three number fields. `window.PS_T156` {model, plan, fit, applyFit, csv, svg, dxf, trace, area, canvas, face, px, files, file, rules, probe}. Measured on the Start case (perforated 1000 x 2000, design on W1): 164 panels, 104 cut round 20 windows in 42 types (the windows repeat every 3,895 mm and 1000 does not divide it, so most cuts differ), 0 behind glass, 8 slivers (narrowest 68 mm); the fit chooses 820 mm (50 panels a long face, 4.75 to a window bay): 120 cut in 18 types (B1 and B2 x 20), no sliver; on the short faces 20 panels of 800, no sliver, 18 types if they carried the design (at 1000: 32 slivers, narrowest 30). The DXF at 1000 is about 15 MB (every kept hole of 42 types). QA `assembly` p156-1 (outlines equal the panel less the opening, no outline point inside an opening, no kept hole within the web, types sum to the cut, the screen face untouched, glass in the picture at a window, 8 slivers at 1000 and none after the fit, fewer types, the three files, the rules, standing aside after Leave); p155-1 unchanged and passing. Pictures `review/examples-2026-11-18/55...58` (close-ups at 1000 and 820, the blank types). Looked at: the 3D close-ups and the blanks sheet; the picture across a whole face from a distance is mostly hidden by the site's trees. (2) **Serial ribs as real fins** (`k160Ribs`, `k160RibsGeom`): on every design face, upright fins of `S.serial.t` at the facade pitch `S.mass.ribPitch` (`K160_DEF`, default 300; 'Rib pitch on the building' slider), centred along the face and at least 150 mm from each end; depth `dMin..dMax` from the field read across the WHOLE face (`k160Tone`, u along, v up, the serial tone controls applied); one piece a storey with a 20 mm joint at the slab; steel channels 50 x 60 at `out.levels` (150 in from each storey end, and mid-storey when a piece is over 2.3 m, so no more than about 2,000 mm apart) standing on the backing board, the fins 50 mm off it in front of them; in round mode a fin crossing a window (the window plus `winClear`) is cut at its head and sill, pieces under 300 mm dropped, the channel stops at the window, and a piece crossing fewer than two channels gets two brackets of its own; in over mode (`overOnFace`) fins and channels run past and the face quad is hidden so the wall behind shows. The design face is a backing board (`k160Backing`, also returned by `revColour('d')` for the corner strip, so the corner keeps one colour). Meshes `rib-fin` (the stock's colour), `rib-rail`, `rib-bracket`. Measured on the Start case (W1): 136 ribs in 322 pieces, 26 channel runs, 100 ribs cut at windows, 64,080 vertices; all four faces design: 378 ribs, 892 pieces, 178,704 vertices. `-facade-ribs.csv` (`k160RibsCSV`: face, rib, x, storey, bottom, top, length, thickness, depth min and max, the profile at its stations, channels crossed, extra brackets, the windows it is cut at; then the channels) joins the model files; the OBJ gains `facade_ribs_W<n>` and `facade_rib_rails_W<n>`; two rules sentences (count, depths, how held, the facade pitch against the panel's own 40 mm, a first check for an engineer). (3) **The plaza** (`S.mass.plaza`, `plazaD` 12000, `plazaSide` 0): `k160PlazaG` runs `paverGenerate` with `S.width`/`S.height` swapped to the face length by the depth (frames 0, single, the paver flag on), cached on the paver state, the field and the source; `k160PlazaGeom` runs `paverGeometry` (capped at `K160_CAP` 12,000 pavers, said in the rules); `k160PlazaPlace` puts it on the ground in front of the face, starting cavity + 440 mm from the wall (clear of the plinth), by one Matrix4. 'Paved plaza in front' (`.kinds160` after `.over157` in the faces block, `data-k`) turns it on and, the first time, lays `K160_PLAZA` (the permeable family, square lattice, rounded tops, 400 mm, four concrete tones, a straight route along the face, four benches, a kerb) without touching the field; a depth slider, a face select, 'Work on it in Pavers'. While the plaza is on, a click on Pavers keeps the building and makes the Pavers ground the plaza (`S.width` = the face length, `S.height` = the depth): the facade's mode, size, material, thickness, frames and `G` are kept in `KINDS160.fac`, and `k160Ctx` swaps them back in for every building function (`massModel`, `massTile`, `faceXCanvas`, `cutModel`, `siteWinCSV`, `massBuild`, `massRules`, `massOBJ`, `massFiles`); leaving Pavers for a kind tab puts the facade's sizes back (`k160Restore`, also on Leave). - **Pass 161, the ribs on the example building as serial sections of one surface, carried storey by storey** (19 Nov, Opus 5.5, the improver agent with its target given; the user: "thoroughly do the ribs on the facade, allow them to properly go around windows. make sure they're properly connected to the structure", then of pass 160's picture 75: "this looks so stupid ... no construction, structural engineering or architecture logic", then "the serial sections doesn't look anything like the serial sections logic in flux"; `ribs161.js`, `stage161-ribs.py`, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass161.html`; fixing rules from `knowledge/flux-logic/operable-panels-and-fin-fixings.md` section B, general practice, said as first checks; laser sheets untouched). Pass 160's `k160Ribs`, `k160RibsGeom` and `k160RibsCSV` are REDEFINED in this script (`kinds160.js` is not edited); its rib sentences are replaced in an outer `massRules` wrapper and its OBJ rib block is held back (`RIBS161.objHide` makes `k160Ribs` return no pieces inside the 160 wrapper) and written whole by an outer `massOBJ` wrapper. **The surface first**: `r161Surf(K,Fc,x,z)` is a depth over each design face, continuous along it and up both storeys: a body (`surfBase`), a reveal that pulls it to the minimum in front of every window (`revealA`), a hood swelling over each window head and a small lip under each sill (`hoodA`), a long diagonal wave (`waveA`, `waveL` in window bays), an entrance canopy over the middle window of the face (`canopyA`), a pleat along the shaped attractor curve (`pleatA`, read through `attrValue(S.field.curves)`, so the Field pad moves it), swells round points (`swellA`, `swellR`, `swellPts` 'u,v,s;...'), any field or picture (`fAmp`, `fMid`), calmed to the minimum toward the corners (`calmX` 1500) and the eaves (`calmZ` 700), held between `surfMin` (100) and `surfMax` (900). `R161_DESIGNS` / `r161Design(id)` (a design no longer changes the window choice): Hoods over the windows, A long wave and an entrance canopy, A pleat along a drawn curve (sets `S.field.kind='curves'` with `R161_CURVE` and `fieldUse()`), Swells round three points. **Then it is sliced** on the window module: a jamb rib of twice the thickness each side of every window column, each pier divided evenly at no more than `ribPitch` (default now 130, migrated once from pass 160's 300 by `K.rib161`; the slider goes down to 80), end piers at the same pitch to the calm corners; with `ribWin` 'veil' (default) ribs also run past each window at the pier's spacing, shallow over the glass; 'open' leaves the window bays clear; 'follow' takes pass 159's Round / Over per face. Each rib is one piece a storey (`finJoint` 20 mm movement joint at the slab line), its front edge the surface's section at stations every `out.sp` (120 mm, coarser only past a vertex budget), the profile continuing across the joint; a piece whose depth passes the depth:thickness limit for its material (`r161Mat`: sawn timber 8, engineered timber 12, aluminium or steel plate 20) is doubled. **Carried**: carrier rails 100 x 50 at the base (380), every slab line and the eaves; stay rails at sill and head height (dropped within 300 of a carrier), which over a window column are the head and sill trimmers spanning jamb rib to jamb rib (`out.trimmers`, no bracket inside the window); tie rods (16 mm, sleeved, through the ribs) wherever a span between rails passes 40 x thickness, with their own brackets in the piers only; rails in lengths of no more than `railMax` (6 m) with 10 mm joints mid-way between ribs, (at first mitred round a corner; replaced by the corner fan below); brackets (6 mm pad, plate, two 8 mm cheeks) at no more than `brkMax` (1200), at least two a rail length, moved off any window, the middle one fixed and the rest slotted. Every piece hangs at its head on a fixed knife-plate cleat (two bolts) on the carrier above and is held at its foot by a slotted plate (one bolt in a slot) on the carrier below; ribs crossing stays and trimmers carry a clip (`p.rs`). Plates and bolts are held inside a shallow rib (0.6 and 0.35 of its depth). 3D (`r161Emit`, one emitter for the 3D and the OBJ; indexed strips, `r161GB`): fins (and trimmers' landings) in the stock colour, rails and bracket steel grey, pads dark, a fourth mesh `rib-clip` in stainless; clips and bolts are dropped past 560,000 vertices; the corner post (`userData.mass==='corner'`) takes the backing board's colour while ribs stand. `window.PS_T161` {ribs, csv, rails, details, rules, design, surf, stats, designs, clip (a clipping plane at a plan x, for section pictures), rebuild, cam (target in plan mm, theta, r, phi)}. **Numbers that tell the truth**: `updateStats` and `flowVals` are wrapped (and called after every rib build): the header reads 'N ribs on the building', pieces and faces, thickness and pitch, depth, rails and brackets, trimmers and ties, the weight; the step bar reads the example building, 'Serial ribs on n faces', the rib stock, what the bundle carries. **Deliverables**: `-facade-ribs.csv` (face, rib, x, mark, storey, bottom, top, length, thickness, depth min and max, the profile as height:depth pairs, kind, jamb rib, doubled, top fixing with its rail, foot fixing with its rail, restraints, window, weight; then target and pier pitch, station spacing, stand-off), `-facade-rails.csv` (rails with mitres, bracket counts and the fixed bracket; trimmers with their window; tie rods; every bracket fixed or slotted; cleat counts), `-facade-rib-details.svg` (`r161DetailSVG`, also the 2D view with `S.mass.view==='ribdetail'`, button 'How the ribs are carried (details)'): A section through a rib at a slab line, B plan through a rib head, C one window bay in elevation, D a corner in plan, numbered callouts with keys, start-anchored labels, the rules of thumb at the foot); OBJ groups `facade_ribs_W<n>`, `facade_rib_rails_W<n>`, `facade_rib_brackets_W<n>`, `facade_rib_cleats_W<n>`, `facade_rib_trimmers_W<n>`, `facade_rib_ties_W<n>`; a README paragraph. Rules (`r161Rules`, first checks): what the ribs slice and how they are set out; how they are held; trimmers or open bays; lateral stability (40 x t, ties); depth to thickness against the material's limit (doubled pieces); wind 0.8 kPa per rib and per end, bending about the thin side between restraints against roughly 12 to 14 MPa for timber, the heaviest piece on its fixed cleat with 1.35, a carrier bracket's dead and wind load; timber kept off the steel (A4 bolts in sleeves, EPDM washers, pads), weights. Controls (`.ribs161` inside pass 160's Facade ribs block): the line of numbers, the four designs, sliders for the surface (shallowest, deepest, body, hood, swell, pleat, swells, canopy, field), At the windows (run past / open / follow), rib thickness and the movement joint, the details button. Measured (Start case, W1 only, hoods): 312 ribs at 129 mm in the piers, 624 pieces, 100 to 541 mm deep, 84 rails, 261 brackets, 30 trimmers, 2 tie rods, 120 pieces doubled; all four faces: 882 ribs, 1,764 pieces, 240 rails, 744 brackets, 84 trimmers, 8 ties, 437,196 vertices; open bays: 546 ribs, 64 ties; relief hoods 441, wave 800, pleat 675, swells 619 mm. QA `assembly` p161-1 (both window modes: every piece's head on a fixed cleat on a carrier over it and its foot on a slotted cleat on the carrier under it, brackets at least two a rail and no more than 1,200 apart (130 allowed for moving off a window), none in a window, no rib in a window with the bays open, a head trimmer at every window and every rib over a window clipped to one when they run past, two jamb ribs at every column, no piece under 450, no jump at the slab joint, no rib under the surface minimum; no rib meets a rib of the next face; a rail turns a corner; four designs with relief; schedules, details, files and OBJ without NaN and without pass 160's block; first-check rules; under 600,000 vertices; the header). p160-1 restated with reason: its rib checks run with the bays open (`ribWin='open'`, since ribs now run past windows by default and its 'none inside a window' meant the open case), and its rule pattern accepts any pitch (the default is no longer 300). The first pictures (hoods, W1 only, bays crossed) were replaced by the follow-up's below. Not yet: the cut picture (82) is legible but dim: the bracket sits in shadow behind the rail, and the clipping plane leaves the cut parts hollow; at whole-building distance the swells design reads faintly; the deep canopy and pleat ribs are doubled rather than given a stiffener; the stay clips and bolt heads read as dotted lines across the face in a close view; the ribs are not nested on sheets for the building (the per-rib profile is in the schedule; pass 154's band nest is for the panel); the window behind a veil is not given an opening light or a cleaning route; a corner where the next face is not ribbed stops the rails at the last rib with no end cap drawn; sizes for an engineer throughout. **Follow-up the same day (the user's review of pictures 83 and 86: "have them properly meet at corners in 3d and be properly connected in 3d to the wall ... I like the curve of the wood, have it be on all sides and nothing covering windows as a default and covering windows as the option"; "allow the ui to select the building face to control attractor curves by hand in the panel").** **Defaults**: `ribSurf` 'pleat' (the curve) and `ribWin` 'open'; a user's click on Serial sections over the example building sets every face to the design once (`K.ribAll`, cleared by `siteLoad`), so all four faces carry ribs; the pleat is read along the UNROLLED perimeter (`Fc.off`, `Fc.per`; `R161_CURVE` starts and ends at the same height, so it closes round the building) and the calm toward a corner applies only beside a face that is not ribbed (`calm0`, `calm1`), so the curve runs on round the corners. **Nothing covers the windows by default**: the ribs of each window column are spandrel pieces (`kind:'span'`) above and below the glass only where the band takes a whole piece of `finMin` (450) or more, each with one fixed end (hung from the carrier above, or bearing on the carrier below) and one slotted end on the head or sill trimmer; a shorter band (the one under the ground-floor windows) is left clean; the doubled jamb ribs frame each window and the reveal term eases the surface down to them; 'Ribs run past, shallow over the glass' and 'Follow each face's Round / Over' are the options. **Corners**: at every corner of a ribbed face the ribs FAN round it (`out.fans`): radial plates about the building's corner, as many as keep the front spacing near the pitch (`m = round(pi/2 (ob + 0.6 dmax)/(1.1 pitch))`), each turned `90/m` degrees, depth blended from one face's edge value to the next face's; a root that would crowd its neighbour at the back edge is pushed out (`roots[k]`, `fan.short`; rule: at least 15 mm clear at the back edge, `rootClear`), its knife plate longer; the rails turn on a faceted quarter-ring corner piece (`kind:'corner'`, `adj` = the two face rails it is spliced to, carried by their end brackets either side of the corner); every fan rib is a storey piece on its own fixed cleat and slotted plate on the corner piece, clipped at the stays, tie arcs (`out.cornerTies`) through it. Next to a face that is NOT ribbed the fan is a return closed by a doubled end rib whose root reaches the neighbour's cladding (`P + 8`) and caps the rail ends; no rail end in the open. Face ribs run evenly to the corners (the end piers divided to the corner, not stopped at a margin) and no rails are mitred any more. **Quieter fixings**: no bolt heads or slot washers drawn on the building (they are on the detail sheet), knife plates only in the gap behind the rib, stay clips behind the rib's back edge, rails a lighter galvanised grey, clips a quiet grey. **The attractor drawn against the building** (`r161FaceMap(face)`: face -> its share [u0, u1] of the unrolled perimeter, one function for every kind; `r161PadAttach`, wrapping `attrControls` and attached at load to the existing pads): while the example building is live every attractor pad gets a row All round | W1 41 m | W2 16 m | W3 41 m | W4 16 m and is replaced by a pad drawn on the building (windows outlined, the storey line, the ground, face divisions named in All round); points are dragged, added by a double click, removed by a right click, stored in perimeter coordinates (`r161PadSet`), the 3D rebuilt on release; off the building the original pad shows as before. **The plaza** of pass 160 is hidden for now (`.kinds160 [data-k=plz]` display none by CSS; ids, handles and code paths kept, p160-1 still turns it on by its handle). **Detail sheet**: D is now the corner fan in plan (fan ribs, the faceted corner rail, a bracket each side, the return and end rib where the next face is not ribbed); C shows the open window bay with jamb ribs, spandrel ribs and trimmers and says the reveal; sheet text is set in the ink colour with the tool's text halo turned off (`style="fill:..;stroke:none"`). `PS_T161` gains `padMap`, `padSet`, `padFace`, `clipZ(z, z2)` (a horizontal slice, for plan pictures). Measured (Start case, Serial sections clicked: four faces, the curve, bays open): 922 ribs, 2,180 pieces (1,008 spandrel pieces), 264 rails, 772 brackets, 84 trimmers, 64 tie rods, depth 100 to 889 mm, four fans of 8 ribs at 12.9 degrees with roots 15 mm clear at the back edge (4 roots pushed out each), 516,524 vertices; W1 alone (the other faces composite): two returns of 4 ribs each closed by a doubled end rib. QA `assembly` p161-1 now also checks: one fixed end per piece; four fans with roots at least 15 mm clear and the face ribs clear of them; corner pieces spliced to both face rails; W1 alone ends in returns with a doubled end rib at the cladding; the defaults (all faces, the curve, nothing over a window, spandrel ribs present); a point moved on W2 through the pad's mapping changes the surface on W2 (570 to 100 mm at the point) and not on W4; the plaza controls hidden. Pictures replaced: `review/examples-2026-11-18/79...88` (the curve on all four faces, eye level with the windows open, a window bay with its spandrel ribs, from below at a slab line (the spandrel ribs' feet on the trimmer), the corner fan at eye level, the corner fan cut in plan, the corner from below, the detail sheet, the attractor pad on W2, a return and end rib cut in plan); all looked at. Still not right: at the corner, from eye level and from below, a slim light seam shows down the arris where the fan's pushed-out roots leave the corner backing in view; the bolts are on the sheet but not in the 3D; the original pad's own help line still shows under the building pad; on screen the sheet's smallest text is softened by the 2D view's scaling; the brick and other kinds do not use the face map yet.
- **Pass 162, serial sections cut from a body** (19 Nov, Opus 5.5, the improver agent in a sandbox; the user: "the serial sections doesn't look anything like the serial sections logic in flux and nothing has been synthesized to create new designs as complex as the case studies", "possibly fabricate the designs through laser cut or 3d printing"; the audit `knowledge/LOGIC-AUDIT-2026-11-19.md` changes 1 and 2, design SA; `section162.js`, `stage162-section.py` (takes the tool's path; needs pass 160, not pass 161), backup `pattern-studio.before-pass162.html`). A second source for the Serial sections mode, for the free-standing piece only (the building facade ribs are pass 161's). `S.serial.body` (`BODY_DEF`, filled by `bodyKeys()`; off = the relief as before, unchanged): `on`, `design`, `tBase`, `tTop`, `lean`, `wave`, `waveL`, `swell` (by the field, both faces), `seatOn`/`seatH`/`seatD`/`rake`, `ledgeOn`/`ledgeH`/`ledgeD`, `canOn`/`reach`/`rise`/`drop`/`neck`, `passOn`/`passU`/`passW`/`passH`/`passSill`, `cell` (20), `splice`, `maxPiece` (1300), `foot`, `footD`, `scale`, `mT`, `mKerf`, `mBedW`, `mBedH`, `view` ('' drawings, 'nest', 'model'), `cv` {seat, ledge, canopy, lean, top: five nodes each, along the length}. **The representation**: each upright rib is the zero set of a 2D signed-distance field in its own (z, y) plane (z out of the front, y up), not a height field, so a canopy can overhang a seat and a passage can pierce the piece. `bodyParams(B,x)` gives the parts at a station: a wall spine of ten tapered capsules (thickness foot to top, lean by the Lean curve to the power 1.6, plan wave, both faces pushed by the field and calm at the foot), a seat and a ledge as tapered capsules whose top is level (iq's uneven capsule, `sdUC`), a canopy spine of four capsules from the wall top forward, up and down to the lip, each smooth-unioned (`smin`, fillet scaled with the part's strength, so parts grow in without a step), and the arched passage taken away (semi-ellipse, door or window by `passSill`); strengths fade by the curves (`cvAt`, Catmull-Rom) and seat and ledge die away 80 to 420 mm from the passage jambs. The canopy's reach is held to six times its neck. `bodySections` samples each rib on a 20 mm grid, `msLoops` (marching squares, saddles by the centre value) chains closed loops, nesting decides outer and holes, `loopSimp` (RDP 0.6 mm), `cleanLoop` (no repeated or collinear points: earcut drops them from a cap and the solid opens); slivers under 90 mm are left out and counted. **Splice in bond**: a rib taller than `maxPiece` is cut across its wall (`splitAt`, a level line crossing the outline exactly twice) at levels 0.7 x maxPiece apart, every second rib's half a step higher, never through the seat, ledge or canopy root, so every joint is bridged by whole neighbours through the rods (the nest went from 74 sheets at 25 % to 38 at 48 % for the bench). **Rods** (`bodyRods`): the grid's inside nodes are labelled by piece (flood fill, split parts by their cut levels); candidates every 25 mm, each a straight line along the stack through a run of at least three ribs where it keeps edge + hole/2 of wood (and clears every cut); chosen greedily so every piece has at least two (one more per 800 mm of height), every pair of overlapping neighbouring pieces shares two, the seat ribs (seat 220 deep or more) have one in the cantilever, the canopy ribs one within 150 mm of the root, the ledge one in the ledge; spread by a factor on the distance to rods already chosen. **Checks** (`bodyRules`, `pr-*`, each said as a first check): seat root depth at least the depth over 2.5 and bending at the root from 1 kN over 400 mm plus 2 kPa against 15 MPa; ledge 0.5 kN; canopy neck from its own weight, 1.0 kPa of snow and 0.5 kN at the lip; the passage's cut pieces hanging on the rods through the trimmers as a beam, the bottom rod's tension against 140 MPa; standing on the channel (middle third, anchor tension with 0.6 kPa of wind); the strip footing against overturning (1.5) and its bearing pressure; the nest, with the sentence that zero waste does not apply to a body. **3D** (`bodyGeometry`, wraps `serialGeometry`): each piece extruded, rods, spacer tubes between every pair of ribs, nuts and washers, a steel channel on a 50 mm concrete upstand under each run of feet (it stops at a door), M12 anchors, the footing top; vertex colours by role (role 4 concrete, through a `serialRecolour` wrapper); the room's wall hidden (`openForm126` wrapper), the light note hidden (`body.b162`), a three-quarter camera on the piece (`bodyCam` through a `frameCamera` wrapper). **Fabrication**: `bodyNest` packs closed profiles on the sheet (1525 x 3050 when a body design loads) by a skyline per 10 mm column, four turns, first fit over the last three sheets; `bodyNestSVG`/`bodyDXF` (SHEETS, CUT, HOLES, LABELS, labels `S017b` = rib 17, second piece from the foot); `bodyCSV` is `-ribs.csv` (piece, rib, station, area, rods, hole positions, sheet, whole outline); `bodyFixCSV` is `-fixings.csv` (every rod with z, y and its ribs, spacers, nuts, couplers, channel, anchors, footing); the assembly sheet and CSV (pass 143's, wrapped) carry the order of work; **model kit** `bodyModelKit()`: the body sliced again at the pitch that keeps the model board's thickness in proportion (`pitch x mT x scale / t`), scaled 1:5 / 10 / 20 / 50, dowels for the rods (the next of 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8 mm over the rod at scale), nested on the laser bed: `-model-1toN-nest.svg/.dxf`, `-model-1toN-parts.csv`; `bodyPrintSTL()` is `-print-1toN.stl`, every rib a closed solid (thickened to 1.2 mm where the board at scale is thinner), spacers solid sunk 0.3 mm into the ribs, the channel a block, shells overlapping (not a boolean union; said so in the README); `-sections.svg` the drawing sheet. **Designs** (`BODY_DESIGNS`, `bodyDesign(id)`, chips in the tab): Bench wall into canopy, Arched passage wall, Leaning screen with a ledge, Wave bench; Start case `sbody` 'Sectioned bench and canopy' (before the first Developing case). **2D**: `bodySheetSVG` (front elevation shaded by how far each rib stands forward, plan of rib tops with the footing and rods, five sections with the rod holes and the splice cuts, the five curves), `bodyNestPage(model)`; the tab's Drawings / Nest at full size / Model kit buttons set `view`. **Tab** (`.sb162` after the lead): the switch, the design chips, Length, Height, folds for the wall, seat and ledge, canopy and passage, a drag graph of the five curves (`svg.bcv`, nodes dragged up and down, rebuilt 260 ms after a move), model scale, board, kerf, bed, footing width, the kit line and the checks; while a body is on, the relief's controls (What varies, direction, station spacing, smoothing, blade, zero waste, the opening and drawn-line blocks) carry `.b162-off`; nothing removed. Wrapped (none redefined): `serialGenerate` (returns `bodyResult` without calling the older chain, so passes 152 to 154 stand aside), `serialGeometry`, `serialRecolour`, `serialNestSVG`, `serialSVGText`, `serialDXFText`, `serialCSV`, `serialFixCSV`, `serialFixText`, `serialReadme`, `serialStats`, `serialDesc`, `renderSerial2D`, `renderSerialOrtho`, `asmSheetSVG`, `asmCSV`, `asmModel`, `bundleFiles`, `openForm126`, `startCaseReset`, `startCase`, `frameCamera`. `window.PS_T162` {model, kit, design, designs, keys, on, geom, stl, csv, fix, dxf, sheet, nest, rules, files, sdf, params, view(t,r,p,ty,tx,tz), G}. Measured (sandbox, headless): bench 120 ribs, 275 pieces on 38 sheets (48 %), 18 rod lines, 0 loose pieces, 0 of 426 neighbouring pairs short of two rods, every seat and canopy rib with its rod, 1,037 kg, model kit 1:10 72 ribs of 3 mm at 8.3 mm on 7 beds with 2 mm dowels, print STL 121,668 triangles with no open edge; arch 144 ribs, 22 cut over the passage, 2 lintel rods, channel in two runs; lean 108 ribs, 1 of 445 pairs short (said in the rules); wave 140 ribs, 7 rods, 24 sheets; each model 0.25 to 0.9 s. QA `serial` p162-1 (closed outlines, no flat back, the canopy over the seat, rods inside every section with their wood, every piece two rods and every pair two shared, nests without crossings, containment or overhang in both kits, model pitch in proportion, the DXF, CSV and rod schedule, the five extra files, the print STL closed, the passage empty with its channel in two runs, and the relief back when the body is off). No older check restated. Pictures `review/examples-2026-11-18/90...101` (the bench from the front and behind, the end section, rods and spacers through the passage, the seat, the four designs, the drawing sheet, both nests, the four on one sheet), looked at. **Not yet**: ribs along a drawn plan line (pass 153's bend is not applied to a body) and a closed or wrapped form; two interleaved series and the waffle (audit SB, SD); a different rib angle (sections always square to the length); a rod line that bends or steps (rods are straight and stop and start, so a strongly snaking plan wave runs short of rods); the true front-of-one-is-back-of-the-next zero waste (it does not apply to closed sections and is said not to); a nest that rotates freely or fills a hook's hollow (skyline packing, 48 % on the bench); the splice joint's own detail (a butt held by the neighbours and rods; no biscuit or plate drawn); the elevation drawing is weak at a distance; engineering values are first checks only (plywood 15 MPa, rod 140 MPa, wind 0.6 kPa, snow 1.0 kPa); not checked by eye in the real browser pane, and the curve graph's drag was driven only by code.
- **Pass 163, the brick pattern by rule, by chance and by curve** (19 Nov, Opus 5.5, the improver agent in a sandbox; the user: "rotated bricks, recessed, or pushed out brick patterns ... tested on the massing and basic walls", "turned 45 or 30 degrees ... push out or recess in by a rule or random, or by changing attractor curves"; `brick163.js`, `stage163-brick.py` (needs pass 159 only), backup `pattern-studio.before-pass163.html`). Measured first, what existed: a field or layer turn was held by 'stands out no more than 0.4 of the width' (out = len/2 sin th + |push|), so a whole brick turned at most about 22 deg (a field asked for 45 gave 17.4 in a closed bond, 45 only on closers in an open one); a whole-course 45 deg layer came out 35; every-third-brick at 45 was eased by the neighbour rule to 14; the pass-96 `course` layer chose bricks by an approximate index; recess (negative push) already worked through layers in half-brick bonds (-30 measured) and was refused in one-brick bonds; turn and push together existed (`op:'both'`, `also`); a seeded share did not exist (only `noise`, a smooth field); the attractor layer with a side existed (pass 137) but was held to about 22 deg. Now: two layer sources, `src:'rule'` (`L.rule` 'course' | 'nth' | 'checker' | 'diag' | 'diamond' | 'pleat' | 'along' | 'up' | 'corbel' | 'curve'; `R163_DEF` {amt, n, k, s, w, a, b, P, R, heads, cn, cf, cw (a course filter: cw courses of every cn from cf), amp, wave, pow, sw, face}) and `src:'random'` (`share`, `seed`, `sign` 'out' | 'in' | 'both'), each `does` 'turn' (a FIXED angle, deg) or 'push' (mm, negative = set back), `alt` flips by course. `brickLayers` is wrapped so the base pipeline ignores them (f = 0); `brickGenerate` is wrapped (`r163Apply`) to lay them on the exact bricks after the field: the course is `b.r + brick.r0`, the place along the side `b.x + PLAN_SIDE.x0` (so a pattern runs on across panels and storeys), the half-brick index `round(2 (X - Xs - bL/2) / pitch)`; diamonds are two families of diagonals a period apart with the brick (or header, `heads`) nearest each line taken course by course; chance never picks a brick touching another chosen one in its course or the course below. Then the holds: calm (within a brick of an end or corner, the first and top course, `quoin`/`jamb`/`nb` pieces, within half a brick and a course of an opening: flat; `calm`); one-brick bonds not turned and not set back (`refusedTurn`, `refusedRecess`); turns capped at 45; `brickStepRule`; neighbours: where a turned brick meets the next, the whole RUN of turned bricks it belongs to is scaled by one factor to the largest that clears (stepping single bricks made the run cascade to odd angles); bearing by EXACT PLAN OVERLAP (`r163Clip` Sutherland-Hodgman of each brick's plan quad against every brick, back-course brick and lintel below; share at least `bearMin` and the centre inside the hull of the contact, `r163Hull`, `r163Inside`), easing rule bricks or the rule bricks under a brick at risk (turn down 5 deg, push x 0.7) up to 16 times; `res.brick.atRisk`/`minBear` are the exact figures and `res.brick.r163` {laid, asked, calm, eased, held, stepHeld, refusedTurn, refusedRecess, minBear, atRisk, maxTurn, maxOut, maxIn}. Geometry found on the way: in a CLOSED running bond (240/10) a turned brick clears a flat neighbour only at 10 deg or less or about 38 to 45, and a parallel turned neighbour at 16 or less or 28 and over; an open bond of 0.15 clears every angle. Also: a bat of a filled open bond that would not bear (it happens on a plan, where a side's courses are set out from its corner and the gaps stack) is left out (`r163Bats`, `batsLeft`). Designs (`R163_DESIGNS`, `r163Design(id)`, chips `data-b163` in a new block `.r163` 'Patterns by rule' after the pattern stack, Pattern step): Dogtooth bands, Dentil courses, Pleated wall, Recessed diamonds, Proud headers (Flemish), Scatter, Curve-turned, Corbel wave, Screen in a wall, Gradient twist, Recessed checker, Diagonal turns; one brick colour, no coping, no piers. `r163Design` goes through `brickFormHold`, so the form in hand stays, and it puts the form's openings back (`wins`, `winW`, `winH`, `sill`, `door`, `carry`); on a form with openings or on any plan the bond is laid closed (openings are cut only through a closed bond, and an open bond set out from a corner does not lap there: least lap 18 mm, older than this pass) and turns between 10 and 44 deg are held to 10 (`R163.closed`, `heldTo`, `screenLost`). The block lists each rule or chance layer as a card (rule, what it does, amount, the rule's numbers, the course filter, Another throw, alternate courses, remove) with Add a rule / Add chance; `r163Say()` joins the Check step's rules (before the section) as `ok`/`warn`/`note`: what the rules laid and the least bearing by exact overlap (a first check), the calm, eased and held counts, refusals on a one-brick wall, the ledge of a recess over 25 mm, the back of a half-brick wall standing proud by the recess (a skin's cavity that much wider), the closed bond. Measured (wall / corner / Low building / Mid-rise; 0 at risk, 0 straight joints, 0 bricks in an opening and 0 beds off a brick, on all 48): dogtooth 45 deg, least bearing 62 %; dentil out 35, 67 %; pleat 30 deg on the wall (58 %), 10 deg closed elsewhere (87 %); diamonds back 25, 75 %; proud headers out 30, 83 %, lap 62.5; scatter +-28, 73 %; curve 35 deg both ways (150 / 155 bricks), 40 %; corbel out 83 (the step rule held 25 bricks on the wall, 719 on the Mid-rise); screen 46 %; twist to 36 deg, 40 %; checker back 25, 75 %; diagonal turns 45 deg, 46 %. Sawtooth bands (32 deg in an open bond, bands alternating) were built and dropped: the bearing holds left irregular angles that read as noise. `window.PS_T163` {S, G, ex, build, run, designs, rules, design, stat, say, apply, clip, area, R}; `r163Stat()` adds bricks inside openings and beds that do not lie under and over a brick. QA `brick` p163-1. Pictures `review/examples-2026-11-18/110...126` (sheets of the twelve designs on the wall, the corner, the Low building and five on the Mid-rise; a raking close-up of each) and `review/brick-designs-2026-11-19.md` (how each was made). Looked at: every sheet and close-up; the pale mortar shows where a set-back brick exposes the side of a head joint (the same with the older course layer: a joint seen in the step, not a fault). Not yet: these patterns as real bricks on the Rhino example building's faces (`kinds160.js` / `massBuild`, pass 161's ground); an open bond cut round openings, so the turned designs keep their angle on a building; soldier bands, basketweave and stack-bond piers (bricks on end or with no lap: not in the coursed model); the rule cards inside the pattern-stack cards (`.bly` shows a rule layer with the generic fields); rule turns on the waving wall and the drawn line (`onHost`: skipped); dogtooth through a one-brick wall (turns refused there).
- **Pass 165, a simpler path to a design, and a tool that moves** (19 Nov, Opus 5.5, the improver agent in a sandbox; the user: "work on the ui and improve the steps to make a design, and check each panel has only the necessary controls needed", "add animations or fun elements to the tool, its too monotous", earlier "way too complicated", "too many controls"; then "rename the tab to coin facade and keep the ombrae credit"; the twelve changes are those of `knowledge/UI-AUDIT-2026-11-19.md`; `ui165.js`, `ui165.css` (inlined into the script as `<style id="ui165">`), `stage165-ui.py`, `p165-harness.py`; works with or without passes 161 to 164: blocks are found by class, `data-path` and heading, unknown blocks take the step of the block before them).
