- **Pass 164, paved grounds that read as built ground; nine grounds to check** (19 Nov, Opus 5.5, the improver agent, built in a sandbox; the user: "make sure the bricks and pavers are well developed and create designs from them to check", "Strengthen work on implementing slopes and creating paver designs"; `paver164.js`, `stage164-paver.py`, needs pass 159 only; backup `pattern-studio.before-pass164.html`). Found by building every paving Start case on a cross fall, a bank and a mound in grade and steps mode and looking: on a graded route each stone moved as one piece by the move at its centre (pass 141), so neighbours stood up to 275 mm apart (pavesite on a bank 175, Keio 214, invroof 275) and the stones tore where two legs of a winding route were equally near; benches, stools and tree crowns were sheared by the per-vertex site lift; the outline kerb stepped unit by unit; the permeable family's kerb was four long boxes and its gravel bed ran past an outline; the site channel was drawn on every edge whatever the water did; a ground on a slope floated (no earth under its edge); a graded route that cut below the lowest point sank under the view's floor. Now: **the regrade point by point** (`p164Delta(M,P,W,H)`: in grade mode the correction the route asks for at each of its points, `zd - ground`, spread by inverse distance (weight 1/(d^2+hw^2)^2), full inside the half width, faded by smoothstep over `clamp(1.5|D|/blendMax, 3600, 12000)`; `S.paver.blendMax` (12 %); steps mode keeps `M.delta`, and only stones in a flight's zone (`inZone`) move as whole stones); an outermost `paverGeometry` wrapper swaps `walkOn` off for the inner chain (so pass 141's wrapper stands aside), applies it to every vertex, appends `walkParts`, shears the outline kerb (`PS_BOUND.kerb*36` last vertices) to follow the ground, routes the permeable kerb through the `invKerb3D` marker (not with an outline), adds an earth skirt round the rectangle or outline (`[.36,.3,.24]`, down to -150; not under a parapet), and lifts the whole ground when the regrade cuts below the lowest point. **Things that stand** (`p164Lv(add,ax,ay,pts)`: each vertex pre-compensated by `F(v) - F(anchor)` so the later lift leaves the object rigid at its anchor; `PAVER164.F` is set only during the geometry call): `gardenTree`, `gardenShrub`, `gardenGrass`, `gardenPlant` (stools), `invBench3D` (anchored on its lowest corner), round benches (`gardenBed` q.seatV), the permeable benches. `permSeats3D` wrapped: the gravel bed is a subdivided sheet clipped to the outline and the terraces (`PAVER164.bed` {kept,out}). `paverDrain3D` wrapped: on a site a channel only along edges carrying at least 25 % and 0.03 L/s of what leaves, in 600 mm pieces, none with an outline or terraces. **Terraces** (`S.paver.grade==='terrace'`, an option added to the grading select in `.topo130`; `terrN` 0 = as the ground asks, `terrFall` 1.7 %, `terrSide` 1 %; `terrModel(P,W,H)`, cached by `terrKey`, stamped per generation): the uphill direction from a least-squares plane of `siteZ0`; the ground's profile up the fall in 80 bins; wall positions by dynamic programming over the profile minimising the tallest wall with every terrace at least 2.6 m deep (n = 2 to 5, stops when the tallest wall is 600 or less; under 120 refused); each terrace a plane at the mean of the ground over it, falling back to the foot of the wall above and sideways to its low end, so no water runs over a wall (the water check, which reads `siteZ`, sends it to the ends); `siteZ` is wrapped to return the terraces (`PAVER164.WH` holds the generation's size, since `topoWH` reads the previous G); `walkOn` is false on terraces. Where the route crosses a wall (or mid-wall if it does not) a flight is CUT INTO the upper terrace (`F.tw` the wall face, `F.ttop` the top riser, `F.cw` 200 cheek walls with copings either side, the wall stopped across it): n risers of about 155 (120 to 180 checked) on 300 treads, a dark riser face and a light nosing on each; one 44 mm round rail each side (`gardenRod`), level 300 at the bottom, parallel to the nosing line, level 300 at the top, a short return at each end, a post on the first and the last tread (a mid post only over 1.8 m). Beside it, past a 600 mm planted strip (`TERR.PL`, soil, coping edge, shrubs), a ramp runs along the wall face on the lower terrace (1 in 15, else 1 in 12, a landing over 9 m) on whichever side has room (`noRamp` counted and said), a 150 kerb 100 high each side and one continuous rail each side over the kerbs, level 300 at both ends, posts at most 1.8 m apart on the kerb; so flight and ramp rails never cross. `paverGenerate` wrapped: pavers are clipped to their terrace (whole stones of the permeable family dropped), removed under flights and ramps, re-levelled, recounted (`made`, `counts`, inventory `placed`, water). `terrParts`: walls 220 in a dark stone with light copings, channels at each wall foot and along the top edge, a gravel bed under each terrace, the flights, ramps and planted strips above. Plan: walls, channels dashed blue, flights with 'UP n x R', ramps 'RAMP 1:15', terrace levels; on terraces `siteSVG` draws the contours of the ground as it lies. `siteRouteRead` wrapped: a step across a wall reads as taken by the flight. **Measured**: `p164Lips(res,P)` (every neighbouring pair, the surface at their midpoint plus each paver's edge sag, its straight edge against the surface, since a paver is rigid; `perStone` is what moving whole stones by the same regrade would leave) and `p164Falls` (share of paving under 1 in 80, the steepest, the share steeper than `blendMax`, flight zones left out); both go into `p164Rules()` (appended to `siteRules`): flush or the worst step and where, puddles, too steep, the terraces, flights, ramps, a wall over 600 needs a guard. Measured on the Start cases with a graded route (cross 600 / bank 900 / mound 700): worst step 0 mm everywhere (whole stones by the old regrade: 175 / 155 on pavesite, up to 275); steps mode keeps steps at flights (up to 135) and says so; a bank of 42 % reads as too steep, honestly. **Designs**: `PG164` / `pgDesign(id)`, a chip row 'Paved grounds to start from' heading the Ground step (`.pg164`, `data-pg`), and a block 'Terraces and the graded edge' (`.terr164`: terraces, the two falls, the blend). `pgBase(W,H)` resets the ground, `pgPlane(P,W,H,fx,fy)` one even plane by four spot levels, `pgField`, `pgEnd`. **Features of four grounds** (one more `paverGenerate` / `paverGeometry` / `paverFallSVG` / `p164Rules` wrapper each): `S.paver.seatRing164` ('cx,cy,r,a0,a1', mm and degrees: a seat wall 400 wide, seat 450, timber top, on a contour, pavers under it left out, `res.paver.feat.seat`), `S.paver.swale164` ('u,width,cell': a planted swale strip down the plan, cut into planted cells for the water check, gravel where the route crosses it, stepping stones on the route, the gravel bed kept out of it, `feat.swale`; a rule says stepping stones are not step-free); two field kinds through a `fieldValue` wrapper: `contour164` (the site's own height in four rings: top .02, steep ring .07, .45, foot .92) and `bands164` (bands across x every `w`, a dark strip of `strip` along the low side with `pits` dips for tree pits); `p164Recount`; on this pass's designs `siteSVG`'s contour labels are drawn at 0.55 of their size. Nine: Opening court (perm 16 x 12, 1.5 %), Counted roof terrace (inv 18 x 12, parapet), Sloping street edge (inv 400, 30 x 6, 3 % and 2 %, bands across the fall, six street trees in pits in a planted rain strip along the low kerb, graded walk, the channel at the low end where the water leaves), Plaza before a long face (`plazaFront(W,H)`, inv 41 x 12, 1 in 60 from the face, path along it, benches, planted band with tree pits; self-contained for later use in front of the example building), Stepped terrace garden (16 x 14, 1 in 12, three terraces, walls 428, two flights of 3 x 139 cut into the terraces and two 5.4 m ramps), Herringbone lane (24 x 4.8, channel on the low side), Three sizes round three points (inv 18 x 14), Pocket square on a mound (inv 300, 14 x 14, rings stepping with the contours, a tree in planting at the top, a 7.7 m seat wall on a contour, a planted ring on the steepest part, worst step 2.9 mm), Rain court over a valley (perm, field by water, a 2 m planted swale down the valley line, three stepping stones). Each builds with its counts met, no step over 0.4 mm except the mound (2.9, rigid 600 slabs on its curve) and the valley (1.4), and the water passing. `window.PS_T164` {designs, design, plaza, report, lips, falls, terr, rules, geom, bed, lvTest, go, off, view, look, above, eye, world, cont}. QA `paver` p164-1 (the walk graded across the valley: 1.3 mm, whole stones by the same regrade 17); no older check restated (the full suite passed in the sandbox). Pictures `review/examples-2026-11-18/140...173` (146 to 148, 152 to 154, 161 to 166, 168, 169 and 172 re-shot after the coordinator's review: 168 and 169 from a lower three-quarter view); notes `review/paver-designs-2026-11-19.md`. Not yet: the steps-mode flights still move whole stones and leave unguarded drops beside paved flights; terraces with an outline or with a route that crosses a wall twice are untested; the ramp sits on the lower terrace along the wall (no switchback ramp); the kerb units straddling a terrace wall are sheared across it; walls are a first statement (no footing drawn, no drainage behind them); the counted layout does not know the terraces (counted modules can be cut or dropped at a wall); the 2D contour labels of pass 97 are still large; the earth skirt is a plain face (no strata, no sub-base drawn); looked at headless only.
- **Pass 166, a brick building as a kit of prefabricated elements; brick mixes; bricks that slide along the course** (19 Nov, Opus 5.5, the improver agent in a sandbox; the user gave a brick-design plugin's manual and three screenshots "to be studied and the logic synthesized into my tool without copying it"; what was taken is the 'Second reading, 19 Nov 2026' section of `knowledge/flux-logic/brick-plugin-and-chishe.md`, items 1 to 3; none of its names, tags, library or designs; `elements166.js`, `stage166-elements.py` (needs pass 163; does not need 161, 162, 164, 165), backup `pattern-studio.before-pass166.html`). Before this, pass 90's `brickElements` cut ONE straight panel by length and mass; on a plan or a building every panel was divided by itself and nothing knew about storeys, openings or corners. **The kit** (`S.brick.elems`, pass 90's switch and its `elemL` 4000 / `elemKg` 2500; new keys in `E166_DEF`, filled by `e166Keys()`: `elemEdge` 'toothed' | 'straight', `elemMj` 12 (10 to 15), `elemLeg` 1200, `elemView` '' | 'tint' | 'apart', `elemApart` 500). `e166Ctx()` reads the set as runs along faces (a plan's sides from `planLayout`; a straight set with a gap: each column its own run; one panel: one run), with each square corner (`|turn| = 90 deg`). `e166Model()` (cached on `G` and a key) collects every brick, the course behind and the soldiers with their place along the side `s = sideX0 + x`, course from the ground and storey (= the panel row); openings are read from ALL panels by their global height and de-duplicated (a panel's `ops` include an opening of the storey below with negative y, and `own` sat on the upper panel for a door: that had put the door in storey 2). Per face and storey (a strip): forbidden zones round every opening = the 150 mm lintel bearing plus half a brick, or a brick and a half from the jamb, whichever is more; an element is never narrower than two bricks and a joint; **square corners are L elements** (`legCut`: a leg near `elemLeg` from each corner, on a joint, clear of openings; a short side gives two corner elements meeting at one cut); the middle by a dynamic programme over candidate cuts at joint positions (fewest elements, overshoot of length or mass penalised by how much, then fewest bricks crossing the cut, then the middle of a pier between two openings, then even lengths); each cut is moved to the joint that crosses fewest bricks without coming within two bricks of its neighbours. **Edges**: toothed = a brick crossing the cut is left out of both elements and laid on site (`M.site`), so each element can be lowered straight down past its neighbour (about one brick a course an edge); straight = the brick is cut into two bats either side of a sealed `elemMj` joint and an end standing in the joint is trimmed back. **Floors**: hung on the frame (`carry` 'frame') an element stands on its floor's shelf angle with the soft joint above (`horiz` 'soft'); bearing on itself with more than one storey (`horiz` 'bedded') the elements stack on a bedded joint and Check says plainly that elements are usually hung on the frame, with a button 'Hang it on the frame'; one storey stands on the footing. **Tags** are ours: face compass from the face normal (the first side looks south, as the sun field assumes), side number, storey, place from the left seen from outside: `S1-2-04`; a corner `S1E2-1-C`. Per element: legs, bricks (soldiers counted per brick and leaf), cut bricks (bats and closers, not whole headers), face m2, mass (bricks, a mortar allowance at 2000 kg/m3, the lintel angle at 18.5 kg/m), its own frame (X along its main face from its left end, Y out of the face from its back, Z up from its lowest bed; a corner element uses its second leg's frame), centre of weight, two lifting points either side of it (an L: one on each leg, at twice the centre's offset so each takes half), openings held and beside, neighbours along and above, made order (face, storey, place) and hung order (storey, corners first, then face by face), the robot cell (`cell`: the arm 600 off the pallet on a track; stations along, the pedestal height, lifts when it cannot reach the top from one height, against `S.fab.reach`), limits over (`over`: length, mass, reach), `narrow`. **3D** (`brickGeometry` wrapped; it walks the geometry 36 vertices a brick, as the original builds it, and refuses with a console warning if the count differs): 'Show the elements' tints every brick by its element (six brick tones, `E166_TONES`, neighbours differ) and site bricks pale; 'Elements apart' draws each element out along its face normal by `elemApart` (an L along the bisector, each storey raised a little more) without its site bricks, its mortar built from its own bricks (`e166PerElement`; the slab mortar of a form over 45,000 bricks is replaced by joint mortar for the subsets, since a slab fills the teeth) and its window parts riding with it. **2D**: the elevation takes each element's tone in its brick fills (`rgb()` rewritten in drawing order), site bricks hatched, toothed or straight edges as step lines and tags in a band low in each element clear of its openings, drawn over the whole set after every panel (`e166Overlay` wraps `renderBrick2D` and `renderBrickOrtho`; per panel the next panel painted over them); a dashed floor line at each storey. 'The elements sheet' (`E166.sheet`, `e166SheetSVG`): every element drawn flat at one scale in made order, an L unfolded at its fold, with tag, made and hung order, size, mass, bricks, cut, centre of weight and the two lifting points, openings dashed. **Files** (`bundleFiles` wrapped; pass 90's `-elements.csv` is replaced): `-elements.csv` (tag, kind, face, side, storey, made, hung, x and y extents, length, height, depth, bricks, cut, m2, mass, steel, centre of weight, two lifting points, kN a point, left and right edge kinds, top and bottom joints, openings held and beside, neighbours, stations, lifts, pedestal, over; then site bricks, joint metres, masses), `-elements-robot.csv` (element, made order, unit, course in the element, place, length, X, Y, Z in the element's frame, A, kind, depth, mix), `-elements-robot.src` (KRL: one routine `E001()` ... per element calling `PICK()` and `PUT(P)`, two pallet bases used by turns, `WAIT FOR $IN[1]` between elements, the cell assumed said in the header), `-elements-sheet.svg`, `README-elements.txt` (a robot export is specific to one cell; this one assumes the tool's generic cell). `brickCSV` gains `element` (tag, or 'site') at the end, after `mix`. **Check** (`brickFirstRules` wrapped; pass 90's sentence and order row are suppressed while the kit is on): the kit and what a tag means; heaviest and longest, and every element over a limit (an opening wider than the limit cannot be split); no element crosses a floor, no edge in a bearing or beside a jamb, every opening inside one element; the floor joint; narrow elements to merge; site bricks or bats and joint metres; the pedestal and lifts for the tallest; lifting points and kN, mortared elements need bed-joint reinforcement, glue or a lifting frame; all first checks for the prefabricator and an engineer. Controls: in pass 90's 'Prefabricated elements' block (Wall step; its note corrected to 'built upright on a pallet'): edges, movement joint, corner leg, In 3D, drawn out by, The elements sheet, Hang it on the frame, a line of numbers and a list of the elements (tag, length, kg, bricks, hung). **Mixes** (`mixOn`, `mixes` '#hex name;...' 2 to 5, sorted dark to light, `mixBy` 'blend' (the tone's share, error-diffused course by course) | 'tone' (bands) | 'chance', `mixSeed`; `E166_MIXES` five chips: Red multi, Buff and grey, Blue brindle, Weathered stock, Red with white glazed; Brick step block 'Brick mixes'): `generate` is wrapped (`e166Post`, on the finished set so a blend runs on across panels) and each face brick gets `e166mx`; the 3D tint and the elevation fill are multiplied by the mix colour over the material's; the take-off (`o.mix`), the order table and the schedule (`mix` column) count them; the probe names the brick. **Slide** (a layer `{src:'slide', does:'slide', rule, amt mm, n, k, w, P, share, seed, alt}`; rules every course, every nth course, every nth brick, a checker, alternate courses, a wave up the wall, growing along or up, chance, the field's tone; Pattern step block 'Slide along the course' with cards and 'Open the bond for it'; `brickLayers` gives these layers no strength and `r163Rules` leaves them out): laid on the finished set face by face, course by course from the ground; calm at ends, quoins, jambs, turned bricks, the first and top course and round openings; the perpends are held by projection so no upright joint closes under 3 mm and, in a closed bond, none opens past the mortar joint plus 3.5 (a closed bond can barely slide, and Check says so); then the course's slide is scaled by bisection until every joint keeps at least a quarter brick (or the lap it had) over the course below and under the course above, and every brick and the one above rests on `bearMin` of its bed with its centre over what carries it (along the course, unturned bricks); a one-brick bond is refused; laps and straight joints are measured again afterwards (`brickPlanLaps` / `brickLaps`); `b.e166d` (laid) and `b.e166w` (asked) show in the probe. `window.PS_T166` {S, G, model, M, build, run, rebuild, csv, robot, krl, sheet, rules, readme, slide, stat, mix, mixes, ctx, sheetView, ex}. Measured (toothed / straight; every case 0 bricks in no element or two, 0 elements crossing a floor, 0 edges in a bearing, 0 openings split, element and site masses equal to the wall's (straight: less the joints' volume, under 0.6 %), a robot row for every brick of every element, the wall's own 0 straight joints and 0 at risk unchanged): Garden wall 2 elements, 14 site bricks / 28 bats; Corner (Flemish) 5 with 1 L, 112 site / 224 bats (English: 58 site); Low building (English, bearing on itself) 30 with 8 L, 522 site, one element over (S1-1-02, 4.74 m, 2,588 kg: the 2.26 m door and a window leave no pier wide enough to cut), the bedded-joint warning; Mid-rise (Flemish, on the frame) 119 with 20 L, 4,760 site (one a course an edge), heaviest 2,297 kg, longest 2.99 m, 9,520 bats and 386 m of joint when straight, arm on a 1,000 mm pedestal, one lift; Tower 168 with 48 L, 3,360 site, heaviest 1,678 kg. A one-brick wall 3.2 m high weighs about 1.7 t a metre, so at 2.5 t an element holding a 1.26 m window is near the limit: the limit is the user's, the tool flags it. Slide on a running bond opened 0.25: a 40 mm pleat moved 300 bricks, none held, least upright joint 30 mm, least lap 75; a 60 mm wave 263 bricks, least joint 11.5; chance (40 %, 50 mm, open 0.2) 127 moved, 78 held, least joint 3.0, least lap 60; closed bond: 24 moved by at most 4 mm, 396 held; Flemish refused. Mix, Low building, blended over a noise field: 929 / 2,103 / 3,205 / 2,056 of 8,293. QA `brick` p166-1 (the mid-rise kit toothed and straight, tags, every element lowerable, quoins inside corner elements, hung order, the three files, the rules; tower, corner, the bedded warning; the mix counts and schedule header; slides; 3D apart). Pictures `review/examples-2026-11-18/200...209` (the mid-rise apart, an L corner element close, tinted, the Low building's elevation with tags, toothed edges with site bricks close, straight joints close, the parts sheet, a blended mix, a slide in an open bond, a pleat with a mix as two elements apart); all looked at. Found and not changed: the mid-rise's corners read pale in 3D (over 45,000 bricks the mortar is one slab a panel and it shows at the quoins; older than this pass); the Low building's door is 2.26 m wide (`brickSnap` widens it). **Not yet:** a reveal that is brickwork (item 4 of the brief: not started); a setting-out point, middle plan lines, T and cross junctions, a picture carried on a drawn line; elements on a wave, a drawn line or a mitred (non-square) corner (the kit refuses a curve and stops at a mitre); a stagger of element edges storey to storey for a bearing kit; 'never more than a share of one mix in a row'; slide for turned bricks and in a one-brick bond; the lifting inserts, reinforcement and shelf-angle fixings drawn; the robot program tried in a cell's simulation; the kit on the Rhino example building (pass 160's painted brick faces).
- **Pass 167, the facade ribs get a hem and a crown, and can turn toward sight points** (19 Nov, Opus 5.5, the improver agent in a sandbox with its target given; the logic of a contoured louvre veil in `knowledge/flux-logic/rib-veil-from-contours.md` moves 1 and 2, synthesised, no building named; `hem167.js`, `stage167-hem.py` (takes the tool's path; needs pass 161 only), backup `pattern-studio.before-pass167.html`). Everything WRAPS pass 161: with the hem and the crown level and no sight point, `k160Ribs` returns pass 161's model untouched (p161-1 and the rest of `assembly` pass unchanged). New `S.mass` keys (`H167_DEF`, filled by a `massK` wrapper): `hemOn`, `hemPts`, `hemSet` ('level' | 'lift' | 'wave' | 'drawn'), `crownOn`, `crownPts`, `crownSet` ('level' | 'dip' | 'wave' | 'drawn'), `hemOff` (250, the hem/crown rail inside the cut), `taper` (350), `cantK` (15), `cantMax` (600), `sightPts` ('x,y;...' plan mm in the building's frame, at most 3), `sightSet` ('none' | 'corner' | 'street' | 'drawn'), `sightReach` (30000), `turnMax` (35), `tipGap` (50). **The curves**: `hemPts` / `crownPts` are 'u,v;...' with u the share of the building's unrolled perimeter (`r161FaceMap`) and v the share of the height; `h167At` is a PERIODIC monotone cubic (Fritsch-Carlson), so a curve closes round the building and a level stretch never overshoots. A hem counts only when it rises over 450 mm, a crown only when it drops 160 mm under the eaves (`h167HemOn`, `h167CrownOn`). Presets from the building itself (`h167Preset`): the hem lifted to 4,700 over the entrance window of W1 (the one pass 161 calls the entry) and to 2,300 at the four corners; a long wave (four waves round, 200 to 2,300); the crown lowered between the corners (1,150 on the long faces, 700 on the short); a long wave. **The model** (`k160Ribs` wrapper -> `h167Apply(R)`, own cache key `h167Key`; the pass-161 object is recomputed and then cut, so `KINDS160.ribs` and `RIBS161.last` are the cut one, flagged `h167`): (1) each piece is cut where the hem or crown passes more than 60 mm inside it (`pc.h7` {A0, A1, cF, cT}); a piece under `finMin` (450) is not made; (2) a cut end that lands within 100 mm of a rail is carried past it by 100 mm so the rail can hold it (`snap`; a cleat needs that much rib); (3) hem rails (crown rails) follow the curve `hemOff` inside the cut, 80 x 50 bent to the curve (`kind:'hem'|'crown'`, `pts` [[x, z]...], `h167RailZ`), on runs of ribs that need them: a free end past the rule (15 x thickness, at most 600: `cant`) or a piece with fewer than two rails across it; never across glass (`allOps`), never within 150 mm of another rail's level; a short run takes in the cut ribs either side until it is 600 long; at a corner whose fan needs it, both faces' rails are carried to the corner and an arc behind the fan joins them (`kind:'corner'`, `ofKind:'hem'`, drawn by pass 161's arc); in lengths of no more than `railMax`, brackets laid as pass 161 lays them; (4) where no such rail can run (in front of glass), the cut end is brought back to the rule (`raised`, `raisedTop`); (5) every piece is held again (`pc.h7.fx` [{z, id, kind, k: 'fixed' | 'slide' | 'clip' | 'tie', end}]): FIXED on the carrier above while the head is whole, else on the carrier below while the foot is whole (the piece stands), else on the highest stay, hem or crown rail it crosses; SLIDE on the lowest other rail (at least 150 mm from the fixed one), or, with no second rail across the piece, on a tie rod through a vertically slotted hole (`tieSlide`); the rest clips and ties; `pc.top`, `pc.foot`, `pc.rs` rewritten from it; a piece that cannot be held twice is not made (`unheld`, 0 in the presets with the bays open); (6) carriers and stays are cut back to the ribs that use them, split where users are more than 2 m apart or where the band runs between, never shorter than 600 mm, with end caps (`cap0`, `cap1`); rails no rib uses, trimmers whose jamb ribs no longer reach them or that nothing crosses, ties and corner ties nothing passes through are left out with their brackets; a stay kept only to clip one rib goes with its clip unless that clip is what holds a free end to the rule; corner pieces are re-spliced (`adj`) to the face rail that now reaches their corner; (7) the profile is resampled between the cut ends, thinned to `surfMin` over the last `taper` mm (smoothstep), the foot cut level with its front corner taken off 20 mm, a cut head sloped like the eaves pieces; (8) counts, weights and pass 161's first checks recomputed (`R.chk`, plus `sTurn`). `R.h7` holds the record (cuts, drops, rails, arcs, snaps, raises, trims, caps, `maxFree`, turns, `minGap`, `bands`, `bandM2`). **The band**: the wall the hem uncovers (and above the crown) is finished as a band in the backing colour, `P + 25` mm out from the wall line, its edge 120 mm clear of the rib ends, cut round every window (`h167BandQuads`, side faces only where the band stops; mesh `rib-band`; OBJ `plinth_band_W<n>`, `head_band_W<n>`). The building model has no door: under the lifted hem the entrance window and the wall are left as they are, and the rules say so. **Sight points** (`h167Sights`): each rib on a face turns about its BACK edge toward the nearest point in front of that face, by atan(along / out) x 1 / (1 + (d / reach)^2), easing to square over the last 1.5 m of a face, held to `turnMax`; then neighbours are relaxed until their tips (rib mid-planes from back edge to the deepest depth, `h167SegD`, less half their thicknesses) are at least `tipGap` apart; jamb and fan ribs stay square; `pc.turn` (deg). **Geometry**: `r161Emit` is wrapped: pass 161 draws rails, ties, corner arcs and brackets; every piece is drawn here, turned in its own frame (`W(s, r, z)`), with its cleats where its fixings are (a fixed or slotted knife plate square to the wall behind the rib and folded to the rib's angle inside the slot; clips at the side), the hem and crown rails as bent boxes, and end caps on every rail that stops short. **Files**: `-facade-ribs.csv` gains `foot_end, head_end, free_end_mm, turn_deg` at the end of each row and the fixing columns name the rail kind (or 'vertically slotted hole on the tie rod'); `-facade-rails.csv` gains 'hem and crown rails' (levels along each) and the end caps and folded knife plates; the detail sheet grows to 2,260 high: C shows the band and the hem rails, **E** a rib foot at the hem with its last restraint (section 1:5: band and flashing, the taper, the level cut, the hem rail on its bracket, the slotted plate, the free end against the rule), **F** a turned rib and its folded knife plate in plan. **Rules** (`r161Rules` wrapped, `h167Rules`, first checks said as such): the hem, the crown, how a cut piece is held (with snaps, tie slides, raises and the longest free end), rails cut back, the band and the missing door, the turns with the least tip gap, and the wind across the face on a turned rib's flat side (about sin θ of it; MPa between restraints). The header adds 'hem a to b m, crown ..., n ribs turned'. **Controls** (`.h167` after pass 161's block): the hem's and the crown's presets, a 'Curve in hand: Surface curve | Hem | Crown' row and a face row over an unrolled pad (`h167PadDraw`, `h167PadWire`: drag a point, double click adds, right click removes); the same row is put over every Field pad on the building (`h167AttachField`), where Hem or Crown swaps the pad's drawing; sliders for the rail inside the cut and the taper; sight presets (None, The street corner, Across the street from the entrance) and a plan pad with the building, its neighbours and trees (`h167SightDraw`: drag, double click adds up to three, right click removes; the reach as a dashed ring); sliders for reach, most turn, clear gap. `window.PS_T167` {ribs, use, preset, at, pts, on, rules, csv, rails, details, pick, setPts, reset, segD, meshes, stats}. Measured (bays open, all four faces): lift + dip: 1,866 pieces of 2,180, 178 cut by the hem and 354 by the crown, 10 hem and 12 crown rails, 4 corner arcs, longest free end 600, 0 pieces without one fixed and one slotted fixing, 0 under 450, 0 rails or rods no rib uses, 0 in front of glass, fans' roots 15 mm clear, band 82 m²; long waves: 28 hem rails, 6 arcs, the same zeros; with the bays veiled the same zeros, 12 pieces not made (cannot be held twice in front of glass). Sight at the street corner: 417 ribs turned, up to 35 degrees, tips at least 50 mm apart, no jamb or fan rib turned; across the street from the entrance (a farther point): 294 turned, up to 19.4 degrees. Cut model 0.2 s. QA `assembly` p167-1 (lift + dip open and veiled, long waves, two sight cases: the fixings, lengths, free ends, rails in use and not running on past their ribs (a rail is never shorter than 600, so a rail of 700 or less is let be), brackets, glass, fans, turn limit, tip gap, square jambs and fans, 3D under 600,000 vertices, schedules, details, OBJ, rules; back to level gives pass 161's model exactly); 375 checks in the sandbox, all passing (Testing section: 375 with p167-1). Pictures `review/examples-2026-11-18/230...237` (whole, the lifted hem over the entrance, the corner arch with the fan following it, the crown, rib feet and the hem rail close, the veil from the sight point (open) and from the other side (closed), the detail sheet's E and F). Not yet: the band is the backing colour and reads as plain wall, with only a hairline at its top (a darker plinth or a coping could be offered); the 'Curve in hand' row on the Field pad has not been driven with a real mouse (the pads and drags were built and drawn headless, not clicked); a hem rail at the steepest part of the corner lift falls about 1 in 1 and would be bent from short straight lengths in practice; a few 600 mm rail stubs hold one rib each where a cut head needs that clip; the 2D course drawing of the panel does not show the hem; a veil on its own head-and-foot frame off the wall, and a surface ruled between a drawn foot line and head line, are not built.
- **Pass 171, two tidies after the merges** (20 Nov, Fable; `tidy171.js`, `stage171-tidy.py`, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass171.html`). Nothing computes differently. `t171Grounds()` moves pass 164's row of paved grounds (`.pg164`) to right after Describe it (`.desc110`) in the Pavers Ground step (it had landed below the size and route controls); `t171Readout()` (from wrappers of `updateStats` and `showTab`) clears the cut pattern's light note in `#readout` while the example building shows (any kind: the note describes one loose panel, not the building). `window.PS_T171`. - **Pass 168, operable metal panels on the example building** (19 Nov, Opus 5.5, the improver agent with its target given; the user, with three pictures: "these are some operable panel examples that can be implemented next"; logic in `knowledge/flux-logic/operable-panels-and-fin-fixings.md` A1 to A3 and C, synthesised, no building named or reproduced; `oper168.js`, `stage168-operable.py`, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass168.html`; needs pass 159, independent of 160 to 167). Before this a face that ran OVER its windows (pass 157/159) only said that an opening window needs a panel that hinges or lifts off. Now, with `S.mass.oper.on` (default FALSE: nothing changes until it is switched on, so p155-1 to p161-1 are untouched), every window behind a sheet-panel face that runs over (cut, perforated or tiles: `lockSheet()`; `o168Faces()` = `overOnFace(i)`) gets an operable bay on the panel module, or a refusal with its reason. `S.mass.oper` (`OPER168_DEF`, filled by a `massK` wrapper): `type` 'bifold' | 'slide' | 'swing', `state` 0..1, `show` 'closed' | 'open' | 'lived' (seeded mix 0 / half / state) | 'sun' (closed where `lightDir()` falls on the face), `leafMax` (650), `which` 'all' | 'second' | 'chosen', `pick` (window ids `W1-1.3`, as pass 156), `lap` (50), `frameW` 40, `frameD` 50, `jv` 10. **The bay** (`o168Model()`, cached): the module joints round the window plus `lap` (`floor((w0-lap)/bw)`, `ceil((w1+lap)/bw)`), storey high; refused when it runs past the face or shares a panel with another bay. Leaves: a 40 x 50 aluminium box frame in the panel's own colour (`massHex()`), a transom at every panel row joint, the face's own sheet in it (uv = its closed place on the face picture, so closed the picture runs on); a head band of the fixed panels hides a continuous head rail at every slab line and a foot band a guide channel (leaves `y0` = slab + 95 (ground 220), `y1` = slab above - 130). **Bi-fold**: 2+0 (stack at the jamb with more room) or 2+2; each pair is exact kinematics on the leaves' mid-plane `m = P - t/2`: pivot at `J + d t/2`, hinge on the BACK faces (`Hk = A0 + R(theta)(La, -h)`), the trolley at `T = A0 + d(2 La cos + 2h sin)` stays on the rail; folded, a pair stands square to the facade inside the bay, 2t along, about 445 mm out. **Slide**: two bi-parting leaves, top-hung on two trolleys, parked in front of the fixed panels beside the bay, which are set back `t + 15` on a second plane (`b.park`, whole modules); refused (telescopic needed) when a parked leaf would cover a window, another bay or its parking. **Fold-out**: two leaves on top and bottom side pivots at the jambs (front corner), out to 90 degrees, two stays a leaf (`o168StayEnds`, re-posed live). Rails: head rail and foot guide continuous along the whole face at every slab line a bay touches; outrigger brackets (pad, slotted plate, arm to the rail back) on module joints, never within 80 mm of a window's width, chosen greedily so no span passes `min(2 bw, 2400)` where a joint allows; `R.maxSpan` reported. Behind every bay: window frame, mullion, glass and a room plane (lit at Night, `S.light.night`), and a guard bar at 1,100 when the sill is under 900. Mass (`o168Mass`): the sheet less its open share (`f159Open`) at the material's density and `S.thickness`, the frame, 2 kg of fittings; limits `O168_LIM` (bi-fold 40 kg / 2.4 m2 / 1000 wide, slide 120 kg / 6 m2, fold-out 60 kg assumed; product pages and general practice, said as first checks). **Checks** (`o168Check()`): closed leaf front in the panels' plane, leaves + joints = bay, uv = along / L at every leaf corner, and at states 0, .25, .5, .75, 1 no leaf (separating axes, 0.5 mm tolerance) meets another leaf of any bay on that storey, the wall, a window frame or guard (n < 92 over the window), the fixed panels' plane outside the bays or a set-back parking panel; no bracket in front of glass; a continuous rail over every bay and its parking. **3D** (`massBuild` wrapped outermost): the face is re-laid on its true footprint when pass 158 has not (with corner strips in the face colour), its picture is cleared at each bay and parking zone (bands kept); one `THREE.Group` a face in the face's own frame (x along, y up, z out from the wall line), each leaf a group posed by `o168Pose()` (the How far open slider moves them live, no rebuild); rails, brackets, pads, window, glass, room, guard merged by material; `userData.mass='oper'`, `window.PS_MASS.oper`. Hint: '· n bays of bi-fold shutters' (`o168Hint`, kept by a MutationObserver). **Files** (`massFiles` wrapped, while active): `-operable.csv` (a row a bay: face, storey, window, type, leaves, leaf size, kg, m2, bay, laps, head track, foot guide, trolleys, hinges, pivots, guide pins, stays, stack, projection, guard, state; then refusals with reasons, brackets, rails with their largest span, totals), `-operable-details.svg` (`o168DetailSVG`, also the 2D view with `S.mass.view==='operdetail'`: A a section at a slab line through bracket, head rail, trolley and both leaves; B a plan of a bay closed, at .35, .7 and open, with the stack and projection; C its elevation with leaf sizes and the window dashed; D the leaf frame corner; numbered callouts placed by height with a key; start-anchored labels; scale bars), a README paragraph; OBJ groups `operable_W<n>_leaves` (at their state), `_tracks`, `_brackets`. Rules (`o168Rules`, appended by a `massRules` wrapper; pass 157/159's 'needs a panel that hinges or lifts off' sentences are rewritten while it is on): bays and refusals, leaf sizes and weights against the hardware, how the type works, per-bay warnings (weight, width, a stack within 130 mm of the glass), the clash check, rails and bracket spans, the guard, wind (open leaves as sails, about q A at Beaufort 6; close above it), cleaning and escape, hardware for a maker. Controls `.oper168` inside `.over157`'s site block (so pass 165's fold carries it), shown only while a face runs over: the switch, Bi-fold shutters / Sliding panels / Fold-out bay, How far open, All closed / Open as set / Lived in / Closed in the sun, Every window / Every second / Chosen (+ ids), Widest leaf, Panels that follow the windows (`cutFitApply`), How the operable panels work (details), a count line and the rules. `window.PS_T168` {on, set, K, active, model, check, poses, rect, hit, csv, rules, details, obj, files, file, build (a full `rebuildScene`), built, pose, state, sync, leaves, scene, verts, faceUV, cam(face, along, y, out, r, theta, phi), setS, regen, night, light}. Measured on the Start case with W1 over, aluminium 3 mm (module 1000 x 2000, after 168b): bi-fold 20 of 20 windows, 80 leaves 490 to 740 wide x 3650, up to 35 kg, 63 brackets, longest span 2,000, 0 clashes; 16 bays warned (on this module the stack lap puts most windows in 3,000 mm bays: leaves of 740, over 650 asked and 2.4 m2); sliding 8 bays and 12 refused (parked leaves would cover the next window on this module); fold-out 20 bays, 40 leaves of 990 to 1,490, 12 warned for width. 46,802 vertices in the test scene. QA `assembly` p168-1 (off by default and nothing for a round face; all three types: every window a bay or a refusal with a reason, bays on the module and covering their window, no clash at five states, leaves fill the bay, closed in the plane within 2 mm, the picture continuous, no bracket in front of glass, rails continuous; 3D counts = model = schedule totals and bay rows; leaves move live with the state; closed, lived in, every second, chosen; OBJ groups without NaN; both files; the rules; the controls; under 600,000 vertices; nothing left when switched off); the full suite on the sandbox copy (passes to 163 plus 168): 375 checks, all passing. Pictures `review/examples-2026-11-18/260...266` (the face lived in by day, a closed and an open bi-fold bay close, closed bays with the picture running across leaves and fixed panels, sliding leaves parked, fold-out bays open on their stays, the face at night with rooms lit, the details sheet), all looked at. **168b, after the coordinator's review of 260/261** (20 Nov, the same files): the face picture now carries the fixed panels' module joints as shadow gaps (`o168Joints`: every panel joint, a deeper line at each slab, band lines and a shadow where the head rail is, drawn before the bays are cleared), so leaves and fixed panels read as one grid; perforated sheets blend their open share (alphaTest .02, transparent) so lit rooms read through the mesh; at Night shadows are off and the rooms are a warm unlit colour (#FFA84A, not tone-mapped). A bi-fold jamb now always laps at least the folded stack plus 30 mm (`lapE`), so no stack stands on the glass. Brackets are kept on module joints along the whole slab line (they stand above the window heads and below the sills, so never in front of glass; `o168Check` now tests that in elevation) and have two cheeks 80 apart and a pad on the wall face at the wall-behind's offset `wb` (the details sheet draws the same). New keys `oper.wall` (the wall behind repainted in that colour with its windows) and `oper.cutaway` (the head band and the upper foot band cut away over the first two bays of the lowest storey on each face: 'Show how it is carried'). **A module for the shutters** (`o168FitPlan(lo,hi)`, `o168Fit()`, button 'A module for the shutters'): the panel width L/n on the long faces for which every bi-fold leaf is 450 to 650 mm, every bay covers its window plus the stack lap and bays do not share panels; on the example it is 820 mm (50 a face; the short faces 800), every leaf 605 mm, every bay 3 panels (20 odd bays), and it says so. **Mesh shutters** (`o168Preset()`, button 'Mesh shutters (a design to start from)'): a uniform staggered slot (8 x 24 at 22 x 35, 20.5 % open) in 3 mm light-grey powder-coated aluminium (`MATERIALS.alumcoat`, added at run time: not a chip in the Material tab), design on both long faces running over (W1, W3), the wall behind #4E5155, bi-fold lived in, the module fitted: 40 bays, 160 leaves of 605 x 3650, about 29 kg and 2.2 m2 each, 0 clashes, 150 brackets, the longest span 1,640, no warning. Plan B's window label is folded into the bay line. p168-1 extended (the fit, the design: 40 bays, leaves 450 to 650 with no warning, clean checks, span under 2,400, the cutaway on 4 bays). Pictures re-shot with the design and looked at: 260 (day), 262 (closed, the joint grid), 265 (night), 266 (details), new 267 (cutaway: head rail, trolleys, pivots, foot guide, brackets); 261, 263, 264 are from the first round (Start-case picture, 1000 module). A stepped patch on the ground south-east of the building (pale by day, striped black at night) was the room floor and the example building's two ground discs fighting in the depth buffer at y = 0 from some cameras; it shows in the tool without this pass too (both long faces over, the same camera). `massBuild` now gives the site's ground discs a polygon offset while the example building shows; `PS_T168.boxes()` lists every mesh of this pass with its world box, and p168-1 checks they all stand above the ground and within the footprint grown by the cavity and the farthest open leaf (498 meshes, none outside). 260 and 265 re-shot from the same view: the ground is clean. Not yet: three pairs a side for wide bays and telescopic sliding leaves (refused instead); the mesh's slots alias into vertical streaks at street distance (the face picture is about 0.1 px a mm); the brackets read small even in the cutaway; 'Mesh shutters' changes the design in hand (material, perforation, faces) without an undo step of its own; no motor, lock or wind sensor drawn; the fixed panels round a bay are still a picture on one quad; composite faces cannot run over and get no bays; hardware sizes for a maker and an engineer.
- **Pass 170, the plaza in front of the example building rebuilt as a designed ground** (20 Nov, Opus 5.5, the improver agent in a sandbox; the user had rejected pass 160's plaza as a bland strip reading the facade's own picture, level, meeting the building with nothing, and pass 161 hid its controls; `plaza170.js`, `stage170-plaza.py` (needs `PS_T161` and `PS_T164`), `p170-harness.py`, backup `pattern-studio.before-pass170.html`; nothing of passes 167 to 169 touched). **Its own ground**: the plaza has its own paver state and field, `S.mass.plazaP` / `plazaF` (with `plazaSig` = kind | face length | depth; `Z170_DEF` {plazaKind 'counted' | 'perm' | 'street', plazaThr 900, plazaFall 60 (1 in N, 40 to 60), plazaEntry -1 (the middle window bay), plazaEdge 'kerb' | 'flush'}, filled by `z170K()`), laid by pass 164's own handles in `z170Run(kind,L,D)`: the counted plaza is `plazaFront(L,D)`, the permeable court PG164 'court' with a `bands164` field that opens the stones into a planted rain strip along the street edge, the banded street edge PG164 'street' with its counts scaled by area and its tree pits by length; during the run `startCaseReset`, `setPanelMode`, `fieldUse`, `paverGround`, `buildElementDyn`, `buildPlanDyn`, `refreshAll`, `schedule`, `toast` and `siteApply` are held off and every top-level key of `S` (and `S.omb`) is put back, so the facade's state, field and picture are untouched. `z170Patch(P,L,D)` writes onto it what meets the building: one plane by four spot levels falling from the face at 1 in N (`site:'spots'`, `fall`, `fallTo:'S'`), no route grading, the street edge (kerb or none), and two kept paths: one along the face 2.6 m out and one from the street edge to the entrance bay (`keepW` 1200). `z170With` swaps the plaza's state in round pass 160's `k160PlazaG` and `k160PlazaGeom`; `ombSample` is wrapped so that, while a plaza is generated or edited, the tone is `fieldValue(S.field, u, 1-v)` (the plaza's field, never the facade's picture). **Meeting the building**: `invKerb3D` is wrapped to leave out the kerb along the wall side; `k160PlazaGeom` is wrapped to lift the paving so its top at the wall line is the plinth top (140, pass 135's plinth) less threshold / N (measured from the geometry: each vertex within 700 mm of the wall edge, `z + (D - y)/N`, the 75th percentile), and `k160PlazaPlace` puts it `revDepth() + 40 + threshold` from the wall; `z170Thr()` is the threshold, or the deepest facade rib + 210 when ribs stand (`k160Mode()==='serial'`). `z170Parts()` builds, in plan mm with z up, for the 3D and the OBJ alike: a slot channel 150 wide against the plinth the whole face (grate bars, grate level with the plinth top), a gravel margin to the paving with a dark edge strip, stopped at the entrance; a landing 1.5 m deep at the entrance bay falling with the plaza, which takes the pavers whose centres it covers (`res.paver.z170.land`); a mat-well at the door. The site's ground, neighbours and trees are lowered to the street level the fall arrives at (less 125 for a kerb, 20 for a flush edge; `Z170.last.ground`), the ground disc is greyed, and the building's plinth is carried down on a socle (`userData.mass==='socle'`). `k160PlazaDims` is wrapped to hold the depth 600 short of the nearest neighbouring building in front of the face (`z170Free`: points every metre along the face, every 250 mm out, inside `MASS_SITES.emm.near`): in front of W2 a 12 m plaza is held to 4.15 m. **The entrance**: `z170Entry()` (the window column chosen, the middle one by default; a select 'Entrance at' lists the bays); benches only on the wall side of the path along the face (they face the square) and none within 1.5 m of the entrance either side (bench markers in `planted`, `INV_LAST.seats`, `res.paver.inv.seats`; `permSeats` wrapped for the permeable court); planted places under the landing are left out. No door is invented in the wall, and with facade ribs on the rules say the hem can be lifted over that bay in the rib pass. **The Pavers tab edits this plaza**: an outermost `showTab` wrapper keeps the facade's `S.paver` and `S.field` in `Z170.fac` and swaps the plaza's in on entering Pavers (all six steps, By hand included; pass 160's hand-off still sets the ground to the face length by the depth), and keeps the edits in `S.mass.plazaP` / `plazaF` on leaving; it also puts back the facade's `S.orient` / `S.mount` after entering (found by looking: the Pavers tab's ground orientation had laid the whole building on its back in pass 160's hand-off), and the room's `floor` plane is lowered with the site's ground (it had covered the half of the plaza below the old ground); `k160Ctx` is wrapped so the building's own functions read the facade's field meanwhile; `siteLeave` and `startCaseReset` give the facade's state back. **Controls**: pass 161's CSS hide of `.kinds160 [data-k=plz]` is overridden (`html body div.kinds160 ...`), and `.z170` inside it carries the three kinds (`data-zk`), the entrance select, 'Threshold at the wall' (600 to 1500), 'Fall away from the face: 1 in' (40 to 60), 'At the street' (Kerb / Flush edge) and 'Lay the plaza again' (`data-z="again"`, which loses edits made in Pavers). **Numbers**: `massRules` replaces pass 160's sentence with `z170Say()` (size, kind, pavers, the threshold, level with the plinth and the fall with the street edge's drop, a held depth, the entrance with its landing (warned when steeper than 1 in 50), water from the Pavers tab's own check, tree pits and benches, the worst step between pavers, the 3D cap, 'a first layout'); `massFiles` adds `plaza-W<n>-pavers.csv` (each paver in the building's frame with its top below the plinth top) and `plaza-W<n>-notes.txt`; `massOBJ` adds `plaza_W<n>_threshold` beside pass 160's `plaza_W<n>_pavers` (which now carries the lift). Measured on the Start case (W1, 41 x 12 m, counted): 716 pavers, the street edge 215 mm below the plinth top (-75), the paving within 6.4 mm of the plane falling from the plinth top in sixteen 500 mm bands and never rising toward the street, worst step between pavers 0 mm (pass 164's measure), 1.65 L/s of the 25 mm/h storm leaves by the street edge and none toward the wall, nothing ponds, 2 benches, 6 tree pits toward the street; the permeable court 5,426 stones, the street edge 2,124 pavers, both taking the storm. `window.PS_T170` {on, say, parts, last, top, entry, thr, dims, free, csv, kinds, kind, ensure, editing, G, state, fac, surface}. QA `assembly` p170-1. Restated with reason (`p170-harness.py`): p160-1's plaza box (its foot may now be down to -700 since the plaza falls to a street below the old ground, and it reaches 1.5 m further since a threshold of up to 1.5 m comes first), p161-1's 'the plaza controls still show' turned round (this pass shows them again). Sandbox full suite: 379 checks, 378 pass; the one failure is pass 167's check ('handle missing': `PS_T167` is not in the sandbox tool, which has passes to 166). Pictures `review/examples-2026-11-18/310` (the counted plaza from above), `311` (eye level from the street to the entrance bay), `312` (the channel, landing and mat-well at the wall), `313` (the Pavers tab editing the plaza, the building upright), `314` (the permeable court: pale and fine-grained at a distance), `315` (the banded street edge with its tree line). Not yet: the landing falls with the plaza (1 in 60 is within a landing's 1 in 50; 1 in 40 is warned, not levelled); the gravel margin and channel are boxes, not drained into anything drawn; the lowered site is in the 3D only (the OBJ's context groups keep their old level); the plaza's field is not edited from the building fold (only in the Pavers tab); the other faces' plazas and a plaza on two faces round a corner; the facade's hem lifted over the entrance (said, left to the rib pass); the permeable court reads pale and moiréd from a distance (its palette was darkened in the run but the stones' grades repaint it); isolated 'short' places read as small dark holes in the counted plaza; the gravel margin is close in tone to the paving; looked at headless only.
- **Pass 169, the brick building finished at its openings, its corners and inside** (20 Nov, Opus 5.5, the improver agent in a sandbox, its target given by the lead from pictures; `finish169.js`, `stage169-finish.py` (needs pass 166; stays inside brick code), backup `pattern-studio.before-pass169.html`). Measured before anything was drawn. (1) **The column in the doorway** was not the next wall's back course: the Low building's door (2.26 m, see 2) left one panel (`G.panels[2]`, 1503 mm) with no bricks at all; `buildGeometryFrom` gave null for it and `rebuildScene`'s `(geoms[k]||baseGeom)` fell back to the FIRST panel's bricks, so a ghost copy of panel 1, toothed at its quoin end, stood in the door. `buildGeometryFrom` is wrapped: an empty brick panel is an empty geometry (`PS_T169.ghosts()`). (2) **The door**: pass 121's gallery keys (`doorAt` .37, `doorW` 2300) are filled into every brick state by `galKeys`, so every building with a door had the gallery's 2.3 m door at 0.37 of its first side. `brickOpenings` is wrapped: unless `B.gal`, `doorAt` is null and the tool's own door (one bond module, 1010 mm, in the middle of the first side) is used; the gallery is unchanged. (3) **Pale flecks beside set-back bricks**: `brickJoints` carries a head joint down through the bed below it (`yj = y - j`) at the depth of the two bricks beside it; where the brick under it is set back (pass 163 recess) those 10 mm stood up to 21 mm proud of the recessed brick. `brickJoints` is wrapped (`f169Clip`): a head box whose foot lies on bricks is split at the course line and its lower part held to the depth range of the bricks under it (dropped if nothing is left). `PS_T169.stick()` counts joint boxes whose front stands past any unturned brick they touch: Low building with Recessed diamonds 132 -> 0, Recessed checker 448 -> 0, a garden wall with diamonds 96 -> 0; the plain house, mid-rise and tower were 0 and stay 0. Found while looking at a set-back window: where a course stops at a jamb on a panel whose next panel is flush, pass 83's joint 'to the next panel' was laid against the jamb whenever the brick there had lost its `jamb` flag (it does under a pass-163 design), a 10 mm strip of mortar up the reveal; `f169Clip` now drops every joint box whose middle lies inside an opening (Low building with diamonds or checker: 275 -> 0; plain designs 0). (4) **The pale stripe up every quoin of the mid-rise and tower**: over 45,000 bricks the mortar is one slab a panel (`brickSlabMortar`), whose end lay in the plane of the other wall's outer face, and whose cut round an opening lay in the plane of the brick jambs and sill. `f169Slab` replaces it (outside the 'Elements apart' view, which keeps pass 166's): the slab stops a recess short of a free end or quoin (not at a flush panel joint) and is cut a recess larger than every opening. (5) **Inside a closed brick building** (`S.brick.inside`, default on; `f169On()`: brick mode, a closed plan facing out, a flat host, not 'Elements apart'): `f169Interior()` runs after `rebuildScene` and gives each panel mesh a child lining in its own coordinates: across a 50 mm void, plasterboard on insulated battens for a one-brick bearing wall, or a 100 mm block leaf and plaster for a half-brick skin or a wall on the frame (`f169Geo`); the lining is cut round every opening from the window board (or the floor at a door) to the head (`st.covered` = openings a lining box covers: 0), with plaster reveals on both jambs and the head from the inner face to the back of the frame and a window board under each window. Floors (`f169Floors`): the plan's sides offset into the lining as pass 43's `planBuild` offsets them; a ground floor, a floor and a 200 mm dark soffit at every storey line, a roof slab a parapet below the top. Pass 43's rainscreen parts (inner glass, mullions, gratings, brackets, its slabs, rails, posts) are hidden in brick mode, and its columns too when the brick bears on itself; on the frame the columns are carried down from 250 to the ground floor (they had stood on pass 43's slab and floated once it was hidden). The window board sits at the frame's foot and 4 mm over the sill's back, and the lining is solid below it, so from inside no sill brick shows. Materials `F169_MAT`; the lining casts its shadow from its outer face (`shadowSide` front) and takes a small polygon offset. (6) **The reveal that is brickwork** (`S.brick.reveal` '0' | '.5' | '1', `S.brick.sillBrick`; Wall step under Openings, `.f169ui`, segment 'Where the window stands in the wall' and two switches): `f169OpGeo(o,B)` sets the frame's centre `bW - rv - 35` (`f169RevealD`: half a brick and a joint or a brick and a joint, held to the wall's thickness less 60), so in a one-brick wall the jamb shows the bond's own bats and closers returning through the wall course by course (no new bricks are needed for that; a half-brick skin can only set the window back within the skin); `brickOpeningParts` is wrapped (`f169Parts`, pass 85's parts drawn again with the frame line moved). The sill: headers on edge across the opening (`n = round((w + j)/(bH + j))`, joints gauged to fit), each a whole brick long, turned 8 degrees (`F169_FALL`) about the wall face so the top falls to the front and its top front edge stands 40 mm past the face, on a tapered mortar bed (`f169Prism`), with mortar between them; the frame stands on it (`OG.fb`). New keys of `brickOpenMat`: 'sill' (the brick colour) and 'smortar'. The schedule (`brickCSV`) gains a row per sill brick, `laid` = sill, and the take-off (`brickTakeoff`) `o.sill`; Check (`brickFirstRules`) says where the window stands, that the sill wants a tray, and what the inside is (a drawing of the idea, not a design of the rooms). The worked examples reset these keys; the Low building now opens with the window a brick back on a brick-on-edge sill, the mid-rise half a brick back on one. Measured (`PS_T169`): house 11,146 bricks (10,833 before: the door is 1010 wide now), 0 at risk, 0 straight joints, 0 proud joints, 0 ghost panels, 32 panels lined, 34 opening cuts, 0 openings covered, 2 floors, the frame's front face at z = bW - 125 (a brick and a joint back from the face), the sill's top front edge at bW + 40, 208 sill bricks in both schedule and take-off; mid-rise 46,177 bricks, 120 panels lined, 112 cuts, 5 floors, no slab end at a quoin; tower 47,766, 240 panels, 12 floors; the corner and the garden wall have no interior and are unchanged. Vertices: house 1,388,616 -> 1,442,580 (more bricks: the door; the lining and floors are about 1,200), mid-rise 1,741,560 -> 1,799,808, tower 1,795,860 -> 1,890,144. QA `brick` p169-1 (house: door width, no ghost, no proud joint, no joint inside an opening, inside lined, no opening covered, nothing past the back of the brick, floors, frame set back, sill projection, sills in schedule and take-off, no rainscreen part shown; diamonds and checker: no proud joint; mid-rise: no slab end at a quoin; a corner and a free wall get no interior); 379 checks in the sandbox suite (passes to 166 and this one), all passing. Pictures `review/examples-2026-11-18/290...298` (`*-brick169-*`: the house whole; the door with the room behind it; a window a brick back on its sill; inside a room; the house with recessed diamonds whole and close; the mid-rise corner; a mid-rise window; a diamonds jamb and sill close); all looked at. **Not yet:** the sill and the set-back frame are not drawn in the 2D elevation or the DXF/SVG; the robot list and pass 166's elements do not place sill bricks; a half-brick skin's reveal is held to the skin (a returned reveal into the cavity with a closer is not built); a door has no leaf and no threshold; the inside is one room a storey (no partitions, no stair); pass 43's flat brick-coloured coping is unchanged; in headless software rendering a lining seen at a grazing angle inside can show thin dashes where the brick ends behind it are edge-on to the view (nothing pokes through: measured, no vertex of a brick, mortar or window part lies more than 9 mm inside the lining's face); the gallery's own door was not looked at after this pass.
- **Pass 172, the Rhino example building built in real brickwork** (20 Nov, Opus 5.5, the improver agent in a sandbox, its target given by the lead from the user's queue: "improving the brick facade on the massing model ... bricks turned 45 or 30 degrees, pushed out or recessed by rule or random or attractor curves ... test the brick designs on the massing model and more traditional ones"; `emmbrick172.js`, `stage172-emmbrick.py` (needs pass 169; inserts before the last `showTab('Pattern')`), backup `pattern-studio.before-pass172.html`). Before this the example building (pass 155) was brick only as a picture painted on its faces (pass 160), and the brick tab's buildings were square towers with windows spaced evenly. Now `brickExample('emm')` (`b172Example()`; a chip 'The example building in brick' among the Wall step's worked examples, and a block `.b172` at the head of the Wall step, shown while the massing is the example building or this building stands, with 'Build it in real brick', 'Roof on / off' and a line of numbers `b172Line()`) lays it brick by brick. **The plan**: `S.plan.rect = [panels on a long side, panels on a short side]` on the four-sided tower plan (`b172Rect()`); `planLayout` and `planHoldCols` are wrapped so the sides are long, short, long, short (`rect:true`), and `brickFormInfer` reads such a plan as 'custom', so a design keeps it. `b172Dims()` picks the panel width (1315) and counts (31 and 12) that bring the outside nearest 41 x 16 m, the face standing half a brick (115) outside the plan line (measured): 40.995 x 16.010 m outside (-5 and +10 mm); a 40 m side closes in English bond by gauging its upright joints (pass 83), so it need not be a whole number of the bond's repeat. Two storeys of 3.969 m (49 courses; the massing's 4 m is not whole courses), English bond a brick thick, bearing on itself (the old rule of thumb: one brick for the top two storeys; the Check step says so), the window a brick back on a brick-on-edge sill and lined inside (pass 169, the Low building's settings). **The windows at the building's own places**: `S.brick.winList` (per side, [x from the outer corner, width, sill, head] in every storey, as `MASS_SITES.emm.wins`), `winRef` (the length the x is measured along: the example building's face), `door172` {side, k} (the bay that is a door). `brickOpenings` is wrapped: with a list on a rectangle plan each opening is placed about the side's middle (so a side that differs from the massing keeps them symmetric), its width whole half bricks (1558 -> 1510, 1400 -> 1385), sill and head whole courses, the door the fifth bay of the first long side run to the ground; the jambs then go onto joints of the first course as every opening does (`brickSnap`); the even-spacing path is untouched when there is no list. `b172Snap()` compares every opening as built with the example building's (from the side caches): 55 windows and the door, none refused, the largest move 182 mm along the wall (a jamb moved onto a stretcher joint: S1, storey 2, the fourth window), widths within 112 mm, sills and heads within 33 mm. **The roof** (`b172Roof()`, from a `rebuildScene` wrapper, meshes `userData.b172` 'eaves', 'eaves-mortar', 'roof', 'soffit', 'deck'): a course of brick on edge on the wall head, through the wall and 50 mm past the face, gauged to each side, with its bed and joints; over it the example building's hipped roof (four slopes from an eaves line 380 mm out to the inner rectangle at `ix`, `iy` scaled to this plan, a 4 m rise, the deck sunk 1 m), a fascia and a soffit closing the overhang; pass 43's rainscreen core, membrane and coping are hidden under it (`B172_HIDE`). The roof is drawn simply, to read as the same building. **Vertices**: fifty thousand bricks with every joint drawn came to 2.6 M vertices plain and 4.5 to 5.1 M with a pattern (the mortar boxes: 3.4 M); on this building the brick and joint meshes are welded after they are built (`b172Weld`: shared corners, the brick tint kept in the key, the material cloned flat-shaded), which looks the same: plain 764,398 vertices, patterned 0.96 to 1.13 M, the weld 0.3 to 0.5 s; skipped in 'Elements apart' (pass 166 builds that itself). Build headless: generate about 0.16 s, the scene 0.7 to 1.9 s. **On the building a design** (`brickFormKeep` / `brickFormGive` wrapped to carry the window list, door, roof and finish keys; `r163Design` wrapped): an open bond is laid closed (tried: with an open bond of 0.2 on this plan every window is lost, since openings are cut only through a closed bond on a plan, and the least lap is 24 mm: the open-bond variant does not work and is not offered); a half-brick bond is hung on the frame (the rule of thumb); a pattern that leaves headers out ('Openings') is refused (on the building its stretchers over the gaps bore on 22 %, 184 at risk); the line under the block says which. Because the bond is closed before pass 163's own hold, turns between 10 and 44 degrees are not held to 10 here: they are cleared brick by brick by pass 163's run rule (not separately measured for overlap in this pass). The designs on the building (`PS_T172.survey()`; every row 55 windows + the door, 0 at risk, 0 straight joints, 0 bricks in an opening; least bearing; asked / laid turn and push; bricks turned or moved; build ms; vertices; 'bricks' are the face bricks: a one-brick bond adds the course behind, 16,209 in plain English bond, 65,204 in all, which is the header's figure):

- **Pass 173, the worst findings of the design check on the example building fixed** (20 Nov, Opus 5.5, the improver agent in a sandbox, its target given by the lead: `review/design-check-2026-11-20.md`, findings 1 to 7, 9 and 10; `fix173.js`, `stage173-fix.py` (needs passes 170 and 172; inserts before the last `showTab('Pattern')`), backup `pattern-studio.before-pass173.html`). Nothing new is designed; what the building shows and says is made true. Every finding was reproduced on the untouched file first. (1) **The facade keeps its own tone picture** (finding 1, which blocked publishing): pass 170's plaza is laid with its own field, and laying it drew the plaza's raster into `OMB` (the tone picture every panel kind reads: `cv, cw, ch, lum, blur, srcEl, label, kind, fitKey, picEl, picLabel, faceRaster, auto`, `X173_OMB`) under the facade's own fit key, so after a visit to Pavers the perforated face read the plaza's gradient (11,621 holes / 15.3 % / 39.9 kg became 6,897 / 8.7 % / 43.0 kg; brick's eased bricks 20 became 3). Now `z170With` is wrapped to put `OMB` back after every plaza build (`x173Snap`, `x173Put`); a user's click on Pavers keeps the facade's `OMB` aside (`X173.facOMB`), the building's own functions read it while the plaza is edited (a `k160Ctx` wrapper swaps it in), and leaving Pavers gives it back and redraws it (`x173Redraw`: `OMB.fitKey=''`, `ombDrawField()`). Measured through the real clicks (Start case, plaza on, Pavers, Perforated image): the raster's hash 483838119 before and after, 11,621 holes and 15.3 % before and after. (2) **Serial sections gives the faces back** (finding 2): on entering Serial sections from another kind the raw face codes are kept (`X173.prevFaces`, `x173Raw()`: `S.mass.facesX` as typed), pass 161's ribs on all four faces still follow (every visit, not only the first), and a user's click on another kind tab gives the codes back with a toast that names them ('W1 design, W2 composite, W3 screen, W4 composite'). (3) **The header, the step bar and the note over the 3D view speak of the building** (finding 3), for the cut pattern, the perforated image, coin facade tiles, brick and kinetic units as pass 161 does for ribs: `x173Count()` reads the design faces (`d`, `w`, `1`, `2`, `3`), the panels on them (`massModel().faces[i].panels`), the panels cut round windows and the window area (`cutModel()`), and gives openings or tiles on the building = per panel x panels x the share left by the windows, bricks net of the windows (as pass 160's rule), units from the face pictures; weight in all; 'one panel W x H x t mm: n, kg' as a smaller last item (`.x173-one`). The step bar's Host reads 'Example building 41 x 16 m · 2 storeys' and its Category '<kind> on n faces'. `#readout` is cleared whenever the building shows, by `updateStats`, after `massBuild` and by a MutationObserver (pass 171 cleared only the cut pattern's light note). Measured: perforated 1,378,446 holes on 164 panels on 1 face, 104 cut round 20 windows, about 4,733 kg; brick (W1 and W3) 25,305 bricks, about 86.3 t; tiles 261,431; kinetic 2,000 units, 20 windows kept clear. When the building stops showing the step bar is rewritten once and a stale 'example building' hint goes. (4) **The corner post takes the faces' colour** (finding 4): after `massBuild` each `userData.mass==='corner'` mesh k (between face k-1 and face k) takes `revColour` of the design face it joins, or face k's when neither is a design face (`x173CornerHex`; not for ribs, which pass 161 colours). The dark #3A3D41 post is gone on every kind. (5) **Coin facade tiles and kinetic units are cut round every window** (finding 5): `f159Modes` is wrapped as pass 160 did for brick, so their design faces are always 'round' whatever Over asks; the over-mode sentences are taken out of the rules for them and a `pr-warn` says why (a solid backing sheet or frame would blind the room behind; Over applies to the perforated image and the cut pattern). (6) **Leave the example goes back to the panel** (finding 10): `siteLeave` is wrapped: `S.mass.show3d=false`, a model sheet view is cleared, the hint is reset, and the camera is framed on the panel again once it is built (`X173.reframe`, and once more after 1.5 s). (7) **A screen face on brick is brick** (finding 7): in brick mode `faceXCodes` reads `s` as `d`, so W3 is brickwork cut round its 20 windows (its own sentence in the rules); the person's codes are not changed. (8) **The plan and elevations sheet** (finding 6): `massPlanSVG` is wrapped: each elevation gets its face's own picture under its outline (`faceXCanvas(i)` as a JPEG, windows painted, composite and screen faces as they are), the face labels name the treatment ('composite panels', 'screen over glazing', 'one picture across faces', 'kept design n'), and everything under the title moves down 14 mm so W3's label no longer sits on the subtitle. (9) **The faces unfolded for brick** (finding 9): the note is 'brickwork, about 12,652 bricks net of 20 openings' (was 17,601 gross) and the held line 'Bonded brickwork: a half-brick skin tied back to the frame, on a steel shelf angle at each floor' (was 'on rails and brackets'). `window.PS_T173` {stat, count, stats, snap, corners, codes, raw, modes, rules, readout, hint, flow, header, live, leave, flowNow, hex, plan, planFaces, brickNet, held, omb}; note `PS_T155.leave` is the unwrapped function: tests that want the pass-173 Leave call `PS_T173.leave()`.
