- **Pass 174, a timber slat rainscreen on the example building** (20 Nov, Opus 5.5, the improver agent in a sandbox, its target given; the user's queue: "test out the wood facade types on the massing model"; `slats174.js`, `stage174-slats.py` (needs passes 161 and 165), backup `pattern-studio.before-pass174.html`). The pass-91 slat wall's rules put on the building as a ventilated timber rainscreen, independent of the panel kind in hand. State on `S.mass` (`S174_DEF`, filled by a `massK` wrapper): `slats` (off), `slatWin` 'round' | 'screen', `slatDesign`, `slatSrc` 'ripple' | 'field' | 'calm', `slatTurn` 'field' | 'alt' | 'none', `slatOpen` (field value under which a bay is left open, 0 = none), `slatPk` (the most a slat stands off its batten, 60), `slatEvery` (2: every 2nd course crosses a window in screen mode), `slatKickMax` (60), `slatStart` (200, the starter above the 140 plinth), `slatSp` (600), `slatPrev` (the face codes before, given back on leaving). The section, lap, stock and least kick are the slat wall's own (`S.lat.sbh` 95, `sbt` 21, `sLap` 30, `sStock` 4800, `sKickMin` 4). `s174Model()` (cached on a key of those, the face codes and, for the field source, `S.field`): on every design face (turning the slats on sets all four faces 'd', as the ribs do): **battens** 45 wide, as deep as the building's cavity less the slat and the largest packer (`bd`, 72 on the Start case, held 38 to 95), at each end, doubled at every window jamb, and between at no more than 600; a batten over a window column is split (`kind:'split'`) and stands only below the sill and above the head; battens are storey lengths with a 10 mm joint at the slab line. **Courses** are set out between datum lines (starter, under each sill board, each window head + 35, the eaves less 10) so a whole course meets each: in each zone `n` courses at an even pitch with a lap of at least `sLap`, the first course of each zone lapping the last of the zone below. A course in a window band stops 30 mm off each window (20 mm lining and a 10 mm gap). **Joints** only on battens: a run longer than the stock is split greedily at the farthest batten within it that is not within 30 mm of a joint of the course below, the first slat of each course shortened by a stepping share so joints stagger. **Open bays** (`slatOpen`, Porous screen): a bay between two inner battens, never the first or last, never beside another, never over one below, never on a joint below, never in a window band, never in the first two or the last course. **Stand-off**: each slat's packer at each end from the field (ripple, the building's field read round the unrolled length with `r161FaceMap`'s `per`/`off` so the attractor pad's face row works, or none), in 5 mm steps; a slat turns in plan by at most 25 mm a side ('field') or alternately 12 mm ('alt'). **The drip rule** is solved, not just checked: each slat leans (its foot stands out by `kick`), and against every slat it laps, at both ends of the overlap, its back at its foot is at least `sKickMin` proud of the face below it, and at the top of the slat below its back is clear of that slat's face; a kick over `slatKickMax` brings the whole slat forward on deeper packers instead (`held`; the raise is solved for the held kick, an earlier version that divided by the lap share compounded course on course and gave 1e23 mm packers). **Openings**: a 20 mm jamb lining as deep as the slats beside it, a head lining held under the course over it with a 2 mm flashing on top, a 30 mm sill board sloping 18 mm, projecting 35 past the slats below with a 22 mm drip; in screen mode every `slatEvery`th course whose height lies inside the window runs across it, carried on the doubled jamb battens, and the linings stop at the batten face. **Corner posts**: a square post outside every corner of a slatted face, the D x D square in front of both faces' cavities, D the deepest slat front that meets it plus 10 (230 to 240 on the ripple), in the slats' own timber and colour; both faces' slats butt to it (they run from 0 to L of their face, so none passes it). Stock: first fit decreasing, kerf 4 (`board` on each slat). `s174Check()` counts: ends off a batten, slats crossing fewer than two, over stock, a joint within 30 mm of a joint below, drip failures at both ends of every overlap, slats outside their face, slats in front of glass (round mode), battens in front of glass, slats standing proud of their post (`postShort`), least lap. 3D (`massBuild` wrapped outermost, `s174Emit` one emitter for the 3D and the OBJ, built with pass 161's `r161GB`): while the building shows and the slats are on, the design-face quads, the corner strips, every `win-*`, `rib-*`, `brick-reveal` and `wall-behind` mesh are hidden and six meshes added: `slat-timber` (slats, starter strips, posts, linings, sill boards), `slat-batten` (battens and packers: packers are wedges between the batten face and the slat's back, four sides only), `slat-wall` (a dark breather membrane with the openings cut, the insect mesh at foot and eaves, a dark room behind each window), `slat-flashing`, `slat-frame` (frame and mullion on the wall line), `slat-glass`. Over 680,000 vertices the packers are left out (`noPackers`). Numbers: `updateStats` and `flowVals` wrapped (header: slats, courses, section and metres, battens, boards and offcut, screws, kilograms; step bar: example building, 'Timber slats on 4 faces', section and larch, what the bundle carries). `massRules` wrapped: while the slats stand only the Rhino provenance sentence is kept and nine `s174Rules()` sentences follow (what it is, ends and stock, joints, the drip rule and how many were brought forward, battens, posts, screws and the span-to-thickness rule of thumb, the cavity's openings and packers, windows or the screen's span, durability, and a `pr-warn` that timber cladding over two storeys must be checked against the fire code). Files (`massFiles` wrapped, while `slats` is on): `-facade-slats.csv` (every slat: face, course, x0, x1, length, bottom, top, stand-off at each end, turn, kick, battens crossed, screws, how each end finishes (end, joint, open), stock board, kind course / pier / screen; then the totals), `-facade-battens.csv` (battens, corner posts, linings, sill boards, packer count), `-facade-slat-details.svg`, a README paragraph; `massOBJ` wrapped: `facade_slats_W<n>` (slats, posts, linings, sills) and `facade_battens_W<n>`. `s174DetailSVG` (2D view with `S.mass.view==='slatdetail'`, chosen when the slats are first turned on): A vertical section at the foot (wall, membrane, batten, the leaning slats from the model at one place, packers, starter, mesh, plinth), B plan at a window jamb, C plan at the corner post, D the first five metres of W1 in elevation with the battens dashed. On and off: `s174Set(on, design)`; the slats come off on a user's click on any tab but Fabricate & export, Material, Light, Bank and Record, on Leave and on a Start case, and the face codes go back. Controls: `.slats174` after `.kinds160` in the building block (the button, the details, five designs `S174_DESIGNS`: Louvre ripple, Twisted weave, Porous screen, Follow the field, Plain lapped; Cut round / Open screen over the window; 'Most a slat stands off its batten'; a line of numbers) and `.slats174t` in the Timber tab's Form step (`data-tstep="1"`, shown only while the example building is the massing; it turns every timber form off, puts the slats on and opens Fabricate & export; the Timber tab still sets the building aside for its own forms). `window.PS_T174` {on, off, model, check, design, live, last, csv, battens, detail, rules, obj, files, geomCount, win, meshes, cam}. Measured (Start case, perforated 1000 x 2000 in hand, all four faces): ripple 3,810 slats in 492 courses on 600 battens, 2,295 boards of 4.8 m (9 % offcut), 58,428 screws, about 14.8 t; 0 ends off a batten, 0 under two battens, 0 over stock, 0 joints over joints, 0 drip failures, 0 in front of glass, 0 battens in front of glass, 0 through or proud of a post, least lap 30; packers up to 79 mm (462 slats brought forward), posts 230 to 240 square; 590,112 vertices with packers. Weave 675 brought forward, packers to 60; porous 4,180 slats with 634 open bays; field 50 brought forward; plain 0, posts 170. Screen: 1,266 screen slats, 2,844 boards. QA `assembly` p174-1 (ripple, screen and four designs: every count above zero, four posts, laps, schedules and details without NaN, the OBJ groups, the three files, the drip and fire sentences, under 700,000 vertices, a panel tab sets the slats aside and gives the faces back). Pictures `review/examples-2026-11-18/370...377` (whole, eye level along W1, a window bay close, the corner post at eye level and from below, the screen over a window, the porous open bays, the details sheet). Looked at: all eight. **Not yet**: the header's mode pill still names the panel kind in hand (PERFORATED IMAGE) and the 2D Render button draws the panel when the details are closed; the dotted shadow patch on W1 is the sun's shadow map at a grazing angle on the leaning slats (from a neighbour), not geometry; the porous open bays read as missing boards with the membrane behind, in small stepped clusters, and want a UV-stable black membrane said and a better rule for where they fall; the screen's slats span 1.6 to 1.7 m between the jamb battens, which the rules flag but nothing stiffens; screws, cavity barriers at the floor line and the window head's cavity opening are said, not drawn; the corner post is a solid square (a two-board corner with a core batten would be lighter); no slat is turned or open on the starter or eaves courses by design, but nothing calms the field toward the corners; the slats do not appear on the laser sheets or the plan sheet (paused); pass 173's Leave (`PS_T173.leave()`) turns the slats off but leaves the faces at d,d,d,d (the face codes are given back only on a tab click). - **Pass 174b, the slat facade made to read at building distance** (20 Nov, the same agent, on the coordinator's review of 370 and 373: the building read as a flat pale box, a dotted patch on W1, no lap visible on the lower half). Measured on the live file with pass 173. (1) **Open joints** (`S.mass.slatJoint` 'open' | 'lap', `slatGap` 14): courses on the same battens with a gap between them and no lap, over the black membrane (said to be UV-stable, the weather line, with EPDM tape on the batten faces); set out per zone so the gap stays between 8 and about 2 x the gap asked (the first zone moves the starter between 150 and 260 so it fits whole courses: `Fc.zs0`, which the battens, mesh and posts now start from); no drip rule applies (said so). (2) **Louvres**: in open joints a slat may tilt out at its foot by `slatTilt` (to 60 mm, about 39 degrees) times the field, on wedge packers. (3) **Stand-off to 150 mm** (`slatPk`, plus a base `slatPk0`): the batten is as deep as the cavity less the slat and the stand-off (38 minimum); stand-offs over 60 mm are said to be blocks of 45 x 145 at every batten with two 8 x 200 screws (a first check). (4) **Thinned courses** (`slatOpenHi`): in open joints, every second course leaves out the bays where the field passes the threshold, merged into runs, each remaining piece still on two battens. (5) **Designs** (`S174_DESIGNS`): A rising band (the default: a band about 2 m high that rises and falls twice round the building's unrolled length, field kind `band`, crossing every corner: in it slats stand off up to 140, tilt up to 55 and every second course thins to louvres over the black membrane), Louvres (diagonal waves of tilt to 60 on 30 mm joints), Lapped ripple (lapped, stand-off to 100), Open joint flat, Porous screen, Follow the field; weave and plain stay in `S174_SET` for old designs. (6) **The dotted patch and the hatched windows**: the sun's `normalBias` goes to 24 mm and `bias` to -0.0002 while the slats show (`s174Bias`, back to 2 / -0.0006 otherwise), the neighbours cast no shadow while the slats show, and the membrane, the room plane and the window frames moved 3 to 6 mm off the wall (they had been z-fighting with the building's body: that was the hatching inside the windows). (7) **The lap at every height**: the renderer's shadow map cannot resolve a 25 to 60 mm kick at building scale, so in lapped designs a 14 mm shade strip (`slat-shade`, a mesh of its own, #6B4A2E) is laid on each slat's face under the foot of the slat that laps it: a drawn shadow, said here as one. (8) The header's mode label reads 'Timber slats' while they stand. (9) Leaving the example building gives the face codes back (the `massK` wrapper turns the slats off as soon as the site key is cleared, which `PS_T173.leave()` and `siteLeave` both do). Numbers (all four faces): band 2,478 slats in 280 courses, 1,053 boards, gaps 14 to 18, stand-off to 140, tilt to 55, 0 on every check; louvre 1,922 slats, tilt to 60; open 2,170; ripple 3,810 (873 brought forward, packers to 137); 608,544 vertices for the ripple with packers. QA p174-1 restated (open designs checked for gap >= 8 instead of lap; band relief >= 100 and louvre tilt >= 40; the mode label; Leave gives back the faces asked at the moment the slats went on). Pictures 370 to 380 re-shot. **Not yet**: the band is clearest where it crosses the piers; over the window bands it is carried only by stand-off and tilt, which read weakly from 50 m; Louvres and Open joint read as texture, not figure, at that distance; the drawn lap shade is a rendering aid, not geometry; a faint moire remains on W1 at some angles. (10) The row of white blocks under the upper windows in 370 and 371 was pass 135's concrete slab band (`slab`, a prism 30 mm outside the wall at each floor line) showing through the band's left-out courses; it is hidden while the slats show (the battens and membrane run past the floor line), and 370 and 371 were re-shot.
- **Pass 175, more findings of the design check on the example building** (20 Nov, Opus 5.5, the improver agent in a sandbox, its target given by the lead: `review/design-check-2026-11-20.md`, findings 11, 12, 13, 14 and 17; `fix175.js`, `stage175-fix.py` (needs pass 173; inserts before the last `showTab('Pattern')`), backup `pattern-studio.before-pass175.html`). Each finding was reproduced on the untouched file first. (1) **A design face reads its picture across the whole face** (finding 11: the panel repeated 41 times read as wallpaper with a band at every panel). New key `S.mass.faceRead` 'across' (the default, and what every load of the example building starts on: `siteLoad` is wrapped) | 'repeat'. While the example building is live and the kind is the perforated image or coin facade tiles (or the cut pattern when a field drives it, `S.grad.dir==='field'`), `faceXCodes` is wrapped to give a design face `d` as a picture face `w`, so pass 145's one picture across the run of faces, pass 159's cutting of picture faces round the windows (outline types, holes 'by panel') and the wrap list carry it with no new machinery; the person's codes are not changed (`y175On`). `faceXCanvas` is wrapped so the face picture reads the tone the right way up and a field at the RUN's own size (`fieldValue(S.field,u,v,runL,H)`, not the one panel's raster stretched 41 times); a photograph is still read through `ombSample` (stretched to the run). `faceXSet` is wrapped so a list read while the faces were mapped and written back later (pass 174's slats keep the faces aside and give them back) never turns a design face into a picture face for good (`Y175.lastRaw`, `lastMapped`). Not while pass 174's slats stand. A row in `.facex145` (`[data-y175=across|repeat]`, shown only on the example building): One picture across the faces / Repeat the panel; a rules sentence says which it is and that the face picture is a preview whose panels each carry their own share. Measured (Start case, perforated): the bay-to-bay spread of the face picture under the eaves 0.029 across against 0.000 repeated; header unchanged (1,378,446 holes, 164 panels on 1 face, 104 cut round 20 windows). (2) **Kinetic units set out from the windows** (finding 12): `k160KinPaint` is redefined: the face is divided by its window columns (`y175KinRects`: openings with their clearance grouped into columns; piers between and at the ends; spandrels above, between and below the openings in each column, less 60 mm under every opening for the sill's fall and drip), and each pier and spandrel carries whole units at the pitch, centred both ways, so the margins at the two jambs of a window are equal; `Y175.kin[i]` {n, piers, spans, mMin, mMax}. - **Films of 13 Nov (night, Opus 5.5)**, all in `review/`. `timber-block-wall-robot.mp4` (86 s): the KR120 laying the 3,654-block wall; made by `grasshopper/examples/timber-block-video/` (`make_core.py` -> `film_core_t.py`, then in Rhino `exec` with `FILM_DIR` and `__file__` set, `setup()`, `use_kr120(1300, 1250, 450)`, `look()`, `shoot2` in batches of about 1,100 frames; `compose_t.py` edits it). In that scene objects take their LAYER colour, so a layer or material left from the brick film keeps brick red: the timber core uses the layer `Blocks` and materials `oakblock*`. The brick film's scene is saved as `brick-wall-video/brick-film-scene.3dm`, the timber one as `timber-block-video/timber-film-scene.3dm`. `lattice-wall-assembly.mp4` (30 s): the lattice wall assembled lath by lath, no robot (laths are bent into place by hand; the film says so); `grasshopper/examples/lattice-video/lattice_film.py` rebuilds each lath from `nodes.csv` and `laths.csv` (exported from the tool: the five short corner laths with one crossing are not drawn), `compose_lat.py` edits it. Tutorials (`review/tutorial/tutlib.py` drives the tool in headless Chrome and records `shots/` + `timeline.json` + `steps.json`; `tut_comp_g.py <folder> <out.mp4> "<title>"` edits any of them, a step marked full keeps the whole screen): `tutorial-timber-block-wall.mp4` (`tut_timber_blocks.py`), `tutorial-permeable-pavers.mp4` (`tut_pavers.py`), `tutorial-lattice-wall.mp4` (`tut_lattice.py`), `tutorial-whole-tool.mp4` (`tut_tour.py`). `demo-timber-block-wall-full.mp4` joins the timber tutorial, the bridge card and the robot film. The QA-free way to read a bundle headless: `await PS_T108.bundle()` returns `bundleFiles()`.
- **Pass 101b, the layers on the wall as a list** (12 Nov, Fable; in `brick96.js`). The row under the brick elevation ends with one chip per layer of the pattern (`.bhx-lay`, `.bhx-chip`: its colour, 'L2 turn · paint'): a click takes that layer in hand (and switches the bar to the tool that made it: Paint, Curves, Select, Section, Rule, or Move for points and lines), 'on / off' switches it off without removing it (`L.off`), and the cross removes it. Found by it: the Rule layer's start course was stored as `off`, the same key that switches a layer off, so a rule made from the bar was never applied; the start is now `from` (an older `off` number is moved over), and p96-1 checks the layer is on. Looked at in the browser pane.
- **Pass 100, paint on the paved ground** (12 Nov, Fable; `pvpaint100.js`, `stage100.py`). A bar under the paver plan (`.pvbar`: Move, Lighter, Darker, Rub out, a brush size, Clear; `PV_MODE`, `PV_BR`) paints two grids, `S.paver.paintL` and `paintD` (the brick brush's 96 x 48 grid, `paintDab`), and `fieldValue` is wrapped once more so that, while paving is the mode and the value asked for is the main field's, the painted light is added and the painted dark taken away: lighter is the light, closed stone, darker the dark, open or planted one. It works over a field, not over a picture (the bar says so). The view's bottom padding follows the bar. QA `paver` p100-1 (339 checks in all). The whole chain is rebuilt from `pattern-studio.before-pass95.html` by running stage95 to stage100 in order; the browser pane on this machine sometimes shows a 1:1 crop instead of the scaled page, and then mouse coordinates do not land where the picture suggests: check a drag by dispatching pointer events at client coordinates.
- **Pass 99, Start and the catalogue lead with what builds well** (12 Nov, Fable; the user: "Update start tabs case study options that load badly ... maybe the catalogue needs to be refined to only case studies that are well developed"; `start99.js`, `stage99.py`). `START_CASES` is reordered and retagged at run time: Developed = brick wall, brick corner, brick building, permeable ground, **Paved ground on a slope** (`pavesite`: the permeable ground on the `bank` site), **Bent timber shell** (`bent`: `bentDesign('sail')`), perforated screen; Developing = leaning timber wall, stacked timber pavilion, serial rib screen. `startCase` is wrapped for the two new ids. The catalogue list is in three groups (`catGroup`, `CAT_GOOD`, `catRebuildPick` replaced): 10 cards that load well, 18 that load as an approximation of the rule (Aqua Tower and the Biosphere among them), 18 to read only. Nothing is removed: the deep cut waits for the user's word.
- **Pass 38, the loft form** (5 Nov; serial iterations: One Main, Rip Curl Canyon, Zero/Fold generalised): `S.loft` {on, w, d, h, pitch, t, bulge (how far the field inflates a section), twist, sway (mm at mid height), rods, samples} (`LOFT_DEF`; recipes and snapshots carry it). `loftSection(K,v)` is the ellipse w x d inflated by the field read at (u around, v height), turned by twist x v and slid by sway x sin(pi v); `loftSections()` makes one closed plate per pitch and places `rods` points at 0.35 of the smallest section radius about each section's centroid, counting which plates each rod passes through (`allThreaded`); `loftGeometry` extrudes each plate by t and draws the rods as a `kinRod` per pitch (they follow the sway); `loftBuild` hides the panel meshes; `loftCSV` is `-plates.csv` (one outline per row with the rod count). The rods are the connection: every plate is threaded. Compose's form list has 'sectioned loft' for revolved and single-curved-loft hosts. QA `host` p38-1 (the sway is read with the bulge off: the field's asymmetry moves the centroid). Not yet: nesting the plates on sheets (the serial mode's nest is the model), zero-waste complements, a second interleaved series (Foxtel).
- **Pass 39, the dome redesigned from construction** (5-6 Nov, Opus; the user's measure: on a par with the case studies). Replaces pass 37's geometry, keeps its counts. `DOME_DEF` gains `skin` 'tri' | 'hex' and `footing`; `strut` is now the smallest member (CHS outside diameter). `domeMesh` keeps faces whose centre is above ground and brings every rim vertex (and any below ground) along its meridian to the ground ring of radius `Rg`, so the rim is one level polygon; faces left flat on the ground are dropped. `domeFrame(D,tAt)` (called by `domeCells`, stored as `D.frame`) builds the double-layer space frame: tri = triangle vertices on the sphere, face centres at R - depth (the hexagonal dual inside); hex = face centres on the sphere (hexagons and 12 pentagons outside), vertices inside; diagonals from every face node to its three vertex nodes make tetrahedra; rim vertices are `ring` nodes in both layers; rim edges are `ring` members. Members are sized by length to the next CHS in `DOME_CHS` (chords L/40, diagonals L/45, so L/r <= ~120). Cells are the outer layer's triangles or the vertex rings (closing on the ring vertex at the base); `nb` is cell adjacency; openings: cells under `openBelow`, darkest first, never next to another opening, never in the bottom tier. Ring beam `ringW` x `ringH` from span/120 and span/60; `frame.maxwell` {m, r (3 per ring node), j, ok: m + r >= 3j}; `minDeg`. `domeGeometry` merges by role (`domeMerge` keeps normals): chord-out, chord-in, diag (open-ended tubes, `domeRod`), node (balls 1.1 d), plate (base plates on the ring), ring (steel, `domeRingSolid`: the rim polygon offset with mitred corners and extruded), plinth (concrete, 2.4 ringW, 450 above ground), frame (aluminium band 60 mm inside the members, 40 mm up, `domeInset`), glass (inside the frame, none in an open cell); everything is lifted by ringH + 450 so the plinth stands on the floor. `frameCamera` and the orbit target follow the dome (`domeActive()`), the room wall hides, the floor grows. `-struts.csv` is every member (kind, nodes, ends, length, OD). QA `host` p39-1 (Maxwell, degree, ring on the ground, sizing, slenderness, openings apart, hex dual 449 + 8 at level 3, plinth on the ground, schedule) and p37-1 updated (mesh and schedule counts). Rendered and looked at before publishing (`dev-tools/scripts/dome39-*.png`). Not yet: an entrance cut through the ring, operable vents, node detail beyond a ball, the dome in Rhino.
- **Pass 37, the dome form** (5 Nov; cellular clusters, the Biosphere's logic generalised): `S.dome` {on, r, level (icosahedron subdivisions 1 to 5), cut (share of the sphere below ground), depth (lattice), strut, openBelow (panels opened where the field is under it), panels} (`DOME_DEF`; recipes and snapshots carry it). `domeMesh` subdivides the icosahedron by edge midpoints (de-duplicated), projects to the sphere, places the centre at `R - 2R cut` so the dome stands on y = 0 and keeps faces wholly above the ground; `domeCells` reads the field at each face's centroid (u around, v height), opens the dark ones, and counts how many faces share each edge (2 inside, 1 on the rim); `domeGeometry` is a strut (`kinRod`) per edge on the outer layer and on the inner layer offset by `depth` along the sphere normal, a rod from outer to inner at every vertex (the webbing), and the closed panels as one triangle geometry; `domeBuild` hides the panels while it is on; `domeCSV` is `-struts.csv`. Every edge above the rim is shared by two faces: connected by construction. QA `host` p37-1 pins the counts (level 3: 1280 faces, 1920 struts, 642 vertices). Compose's form list has 'geodesic dome' for sphere and double-curved hosts. Not yet: a hex/tri dual (Eden), tapered cells, an opening rule by structure.
- **Pass 36, the voxel form** (5 Nov; pixelated fields, Rodovre's logic generalised): `S.vox` {on, mod, nx, ny, storeys, pull (eight percentages: each base corner's x,y pulled toward the centre), top (four percentages: the top corners as a share of the base), erode %, erodeBy 'uniform' | 'height' | 'field', balcony %, seed} (`VOX_DEF`; recipes and snapshots carry it). `voxFrame` builds the base and top quads; `voxInside` tests a point against the quad interpolated at its height; `voxCells()` keeps the modules whose centroid is inside, erodes by the rule (`rnd` seeded per cell; by height the chance rises with w, by field it rises where the field is dark), and marks `balcony` on kept modules with an open plan face; `voxGeometry` is one box per module (0.97 of the module so the faces read) plus a slab per balcony; `voxBuild` runs after the stack at the end of `rebuildScene` and hides the panel meshes; `voxCSV` is `-modules.csv`. Modules meet face to face on the grid, so the form is connected by construction. Controls under Host surface (3D) with a `.vox-note`; Compose's form list leads with 'voxel building' (storeys and length feed it). QA `host` p36-1. Also in this pass (v41): Compose's category chips are single-select; the Compose button still triggers the build. Not yet: openings cut in the modules, floors and a glass skin, a unit pattern on the faces.
- **Pass 35, the stack form** (5 Nov, from the Flux synthesis in `catalogue/sources/notes/new-pages-2026-11-05.md`: the 40 pages IMG_4598 to IMG_4641 read that day; Aqua Tower, Rip Curl Canyon, Zero/Fold, Rodovre generalised): `S.stack` {on, shape 'polygon' | 'rect', sides, w, d, storeys, storeyH, layers (bands per storey), band (depth), push (mm the field pushes the outline out at tone 1), hold (tone under which the base stays), taper, twist, samples} (`STACK_DEF`; recipes and snapshots carry it). `stackBase(K)` samples the base outline with outward normals; `stackOutlines()` makes one closed outline per band: the field read at (u around, v height) through `fieldValue`, the push along the normal, the taper scale and the twist turn per layer; `stackGeometry` extrudes each as a band plus a dark core slab; `stackBuild` runs at the end of `rebuildScene` and hides the panel meshes while the stack is on (`stackActive()`: not overhead); `stackCSV` is `-layers.csv` (one outline per row). A closed form built this way has NO corner joints: the band wraps. Controls under Host surface (3D) with a `.stack-note`; Compose's form list leads with 'stacked tower' (storeys, sides and length feed it). QA `host` p35-1: 20 bands, pushed out, tapered, twisted, no gaps, 40 meshes. Not yet: a unit pattern applied on the bands (the stack is the form; the panel modes are set aside while it is on), a loft between two outlines, openings.
- **The re-aim, step 3, first slice: Compose** (4 Nov): `composeDesign(hostKind, cats, seed)` makes a new design from `LOGIC`: it takes the principles of the chosen categories (all when none), picks by seed a host form (the user's, or one those principles were built on), a unit from the principles on that host (`CMP_UNIT`: library unit kind -> the tool's mode), a field and a mapping from the unit's own drives or the library's (`CMP_FIELD`: library field kind -> Field tab kind), a limit and a what-if from the matrix, then picks a FORM the unit can stand on (`CMP_FORMS`: wall, row, tower, courtyard, serpentine, any curve, vaulted hall (mirror + roof), cylinder, sphere, wave, ground; `CMP_HOST` maps each library host kind to the forms it prefers; `cmpForm`, `cmpApplyForm` set plan, host, mirror, roof, arrangement and counts by seed) and writes the tool's state (sizes, the field with seeded parameters, the mapping into the mode's parameters with the library's domain when it has one, the what-if applied where it names a parameter the tool has) and builds. `S.prov.source` names the systems; `COMPOSE_LAST` holds seed, mode, unit, field, drive, credits, notes and a sentence. `composeUI` in the Start tab: what you are making (the form list, tower selected), storeys (3 m each), sides, length in m, category chips, Compose and Another; `composeDesign(hostKind, cats, seed, opts)` takes a form name or a library host kind and `opts` {storeys, sides, length}; closed forms set `gap` 0 so panels meet on the plan line and bricks bond at the corners; drives are scaled to read (bricks turn 16 to 30 deg, ribs 150 to 300 deep, pots 300 to 1500). Same seed, same design; another seed, another. `COMPOSE_LAST.method` is the process of the principle the unit came from, shown in the readback as 'how this logic works, as learned from'. QA `flow` p34-1: nine compositions on nine hosts build across seven forms, seeds differ and repeat. What it does not do yet: layer two categories into one surface (it draws from their union), carve a volume, lean walls, or write the design's `.ghx`.
- **The re-aim, step 2, first slice: the Start tab** (4 Nov): `Start` is the first tab (`TAB_ORDER`, `FLOW` Source, `SUB`, `HUE`, `LEDE`), built by `startBuild()` after its code runs (then `orderTabs()` again: a `const` read by `orderTabs` at boot is in its temporal dead zone, and a `var` is undefined, so the tab is built late and the rail re-ordered). It holds the five steps (`START_STEPS`, each a tab to go to), four doors (Words -> Text, An image -> Image, Your geometry -> a typed plan line through `startPlanFrom`, which sets `plan.kind='curve'`, as many columns as chords of the panel width fit, and brick mode from cut; A host from the library -> `startHosts`, one card per `LOGIC.hosts` kind with the panelisations and units the cards pair with it and what the browser does today, `START_HOST_DO`: plane, cylinder, sphere and the wave hosts, pavers on the ground, a tower plan for a revolved form, a curve plan for a site polygon; volume says not yet). The welcome card reads 'One design, five steps' and sends to Start. OBJ/DXF/SVG uploads are the next slice. QA `flow` p33-1; `layout` holds the panel inside the rail (`minmax(0,1fr)` columns, `overflow-wrap:anywhere`).
- **The re-aim, step 1: the logic library** (4 Nov; concept in `Claude Projects/.../knowledge/CONCEPT-2026-11-04.md`): the tool is for a user's own design; the cards (46 since pass 74) are knowledge, never output. `catalogue/build-logic.py` reads the cards and writes `catalogue/logic.json`: `hosts` (10 surface kinds with the panelisations and units seen on each), `panelisations` (14), `units` (20 kinds with dims, materials, generators and merged parameters), `fields` (19 kinds with what they drive), `mappings` (73 field-to-parameter pairs with domains, remaps, effects), `fabrication` (16 methods with their constraints), `variants` (156 matrix rows), `compat` (168 pairs the cards show working), `categories` (the 10 taxonomy values with their units, fields, hosts and methods), and the synthesis itself: `principles` (one per card, the building forgotten: host, unit, `drives` = mappings, `limits` = constraints, `steps` = process, `when` = matrix) and `rules` (345 sentences: 77 drives, 112 limits, 156 what-ifs, each `from` a card id) plus `credits`. `build-embed.py` embeds it between `/*LOGIC-BEGIN*/` and `/*LOGIC-END*/` as `LOGIC` (342 KB; the tool is 1.73 MB) and `--check` covers it; `logicRules(cat,kind)` and `logicCredit(id)` are the first helpers. QA `catalogue` p32-1. Nothing in the interface uses it yet: steps 2 to 5 of the re-aim do.
- **The assembly definition** (3 Nov, Rhino): `grasshopper/assembly.py` + `assembly.ghx` chain `bricks_on_surface.py` (twice), `vault_between_walls.py` and `units_on_surface.py` / `tiles_on_surface.py` by exec-ing them from `Folder` with inputs in a fresh namespace (`run_generator`). Own rules: the arch line on each wall's outer face (`outer_edge`), the rib depth held so the flat-cut foot stays on the wall (`foot_width`, measured per end), the skin stood off the shell by `Standoff` on the side and facing that clear the ribs (`stood_off`, `ribs_hit`). The nave example checked in Rhino 8 (0 overlaps, feet on the wall, 0 vertices in a rib). `vault_between_walls.py` had `solid = solid(...)` in its rib loop: fixed. The `.ghx` is built by script (`ZuiPythonComponent` loaded from `GhPython.gha` by path after `_-Grasshopper _Document _Close`; outputs `['out'] + names`, since output 0 is the console stream) and was opened on a canvas and solved on 3 Nov (18 s, every output filled: 1,485 + 1,050 bricks, 21 ribs, 5,920 skin parts, no runtime messages); the first output must be the component's own `out` stream or the first result is lost.
- **Pass 31, Little Island's pots** (3 Nov, the user asked mid-session): `S.paver.pots` lofts every cell from a stem (`stem` of the cell, scaled about its centroid, at the ground) to the cell polygon at its own height `p.h`, read from the field between the smallest and the largest type height (`paver.hRange`), the spread in the top `flare` of the height (`potGeometry`: rings, side quads with the winding from the polygon's area sign, caps from `ShapeUtils.triangulateShape`). The placement list's `height_mm` is the cell's own height when pots are on. The Little Island recipe sets pots with heights 700 to 2100 on 700 mm cells; the card's regular grid inside the zone and Voronoi outside are unchanged. Planting (pass 29) now draws soil in every hole, a tapered trunk with a three-sphere canopy, tufts of three blades, and a mound with grass in a culled cell. Pots skip holes and planting. QA `paver` p31-1.
- **Pass 30, a roof between walls** (3 Nov): `S.roof` {on, profile 'catenary' | 'arch' | 'pointed', rise, pitch, depth, t} (`ROOF_DEF`; recipes and snapshots carry it), live only with a mirrored wall (`roofActive()`: `host.mirror > 0`, not overhead, no plan). `roofShape(kind,u)` is the profile's height at u across the span (1 at the crown; pointed is two arcs of radius span centred on the opposite springing); `roofRibs(TW,TH)` lays `floor(TW/pitch)+1` ribs centred along the wall, each a top and a bottom profile at 25 stations with the underside held at the springing line so it bears on the wall top; `roofGeometry` extrudes each rib as a plate in the y-z plane (Shape in (-s, TH+y), `rotateY(-pi/2)`, translated to its x) and displaces a PlaneGeometry into the shell; `roofBuild` adds them after `hostMirror()` with `userData.roof`; `roofCSV()` is `-vault.csv` (rib, x, span, rise, depth, t, profile, the top profile). Controls under Host surface (3D) with a `.roof-note`. No nest and no bricks on the vault: the Rhino generator does those. QA `host` p30-1.
- **Pass 29, Keio and Roche in the browser** (3 Nov): `S.paver.holes` (a hole diameter per type, biggest first, `paverHoles`) cuts a circle through each slab that can carry it (`paverFits`: the circle clears every edge by `d/2 + max(15, half joint)`; a zero-length closing edge is skipped; `holesHeld` counts the rest); `S.paver.plant` puts a tree (trunk and canopy sized to the hole) in the biggest holes and a grass cone in the others and in the culled cells; `S.paver.keep` (u,v polylines from the bottom-left, `;` between paths) and `keepW` keep pavers within that distance of a path from the cull (`paverKeep`, `paverNearPath`), and the culled cells come back as `paver.planted` outlines (earth-green in 2D, dashed in the setting-out). `hole_mm` is the LAST column of both lists (older checks anchor on `tilt_y_deg` and `name`; they now expect `,hole_mm$`). Keio's slabs are square: set `ratio` 1, or a 2:1 paver cannot carry the 375 and 300 holes. QA `paver` p29-1 and p29-2.
- **Pass 28, the generators in the bundle** (3 Nov): `build-embed.py` embeds the six `*_on_surface.py` / `vault_between_walls.py` generators in `GH_SCRIPTS` beside the five readers (the tool grows ~140 KB); `GH_FOR` gives each mode its generator (perf gets `tiles_on_surface.py`; `ghFiles` now knows `G.perf`) and the bundle README says which files read and which generate, and where the `.ghx` definitions and the four examples live. The `.ghx` files themselves (0.9 MB) are not embedded. The Image -> pattern lede says a picture is a reference only in the facade modes.
- **Pass 27, the kinetic mechanisms in 3D** (3 Nov): `kinGeometry` draws the linkage `units_on_surface.py` draws. Umbrella: petals folded by `(1 - s) x kin.fold` (new control, default 80 deg), the hub on the axis at the height the struts reach (`kinActuator`, the same formula as `umbrella_actuator`), a strut from the hub to 0.6 along each petal, a spider of three spokes and a collar behind the frame, the actuator body behind and its rod. Iris: a ring on the frame, blades on pins at its inner edge, each a crescent (`kinCrescent`: the aperture circle less its own circle turned by `2 asin(s/2)`), stacked with washers. Louvre: the flap on the top bar, a bracket down from the bottom bar, the push rod between. `kinRod(a,b,r)` is a cylinder between two points. `kinCSV` carries `actuator_rest_<hub_mm|ring_deg|rod_mm>` as its eighth column (the walker check counts 8 fixed columns now). Not a penetration-checked model: the browser draws, Rhino verifies.
- **Pass 26, a plan from any curve** (3 Nov): `S.plan.kind='curve'` with `plan.pts` (a typed polyline of x,z mm, any separator, `planCurvePts`, default `PLAN_CURVE_DEF`: three panels, a square corner, a kink). `planLayout` walks chords of the panel width along it by arc length (bisection; the last leg extended when the line runs short), each joint with its own turn, and returns `curve:true` beside `serp:true` so the corner code treats it as the serpentine: a square kink is a quoin, any other a mitred squint. `planHoldCols` leaves the count alone. A `textCtl` in the plan fold holds the points. QA `assembly` p26-1: 8 chords on a 5-point polyline, 0 overlap.
- **Pass 25, the tour of every tab** (3 Nov): `TOUR` has seventeen steps; after Perforated image it loads a catalogue card into each facade mode (Gantenbein winery, Aqua Tower, Little Island, Al Bahar towers) and the Light step lights the kinetic wall. `qa/record-demo.py` records 36 frames (`08-catalogue` to `16b-record`), `review/caption-and-cut.py demo` cuts `review/demo-captioned.mp4` (4:42) with its SCRIPT, and `review/build_tabs_deck.py` writes `review/deck-tabs.html` (21 slides: one per tab with its frame and, for the modes with a generator, the Rhino picture; four on the Rhino side) from `review/frames/raw`. The captioner writes its PowerShell to a file: 36 frames in one `-Command` string is longer than Windows allows. Pass 24's serpentine plan was applied with pass 23 (v30).
- **Pass 23, the sun field per face**: `fitKeyNow()` adds `|face<PLAN_FACE>` while a panel of a plan is being generated from a sun field (`OMB.sp` set, `S.omb.src` starting with `field`, kind sun), so `ombDrawField` rasterises again for that panel; `ombFieldCanvas` then fills only the panel's own columns of the set's canvas (`i0..i1` from `OMB.sp.ox` and `S.width`) and sets `OMB.faceRaster`, and the spanning loop's `finally` clears the key and draws the whole picture again so the preview and the next regen start from a full raster. The sun field is the share of a face's own sun each point gets under its overhang and reveals (not its orientation), so faces differ only when an overhang or a reveal is set. `frameCamera` multiplies the orbit radius by `1.4 / aspect` when the stage's aspect is under 1.4. The brick elevation caption names the quoin or the squints on a corner panel.
- **Pass 22, the sweep of passes 18 to 20**: `planHoldCols(cols)` holds a closed plan's columns to a count that closes (a tower: a multiple of its sides; a court: even and at least 4, laid a, b, a, b) and a corner or U to one a side; `planLayout` applies it and reports `held` and the signed `turn`; the column stepper, the plan control and the sides slider snap `S.cols` and the plan note says so. `PLAN_FLUSH` and `PLAN_FACE` are `var`s declared before `TAB_ORDER` (the generators read them before the plan code has run). `PLAN_FACE` (minus the face's heading in degrees) is added to the sun's facing in the sun field and in `kinStateAt`, so each face of a plan sees the sun from its own side; `kin.faceOff` carries it into `kinCSV`. The tab nav (`buildTabNav`) steps over mode tabs that are not the current mode and is rebuilt on a mode change (`rebuildTabNav`, from the header's `mark`), which also re-runs `buildPlanDyn` (and that `buildHostDyn`), so the plan and host notes follow the mode. Every mode's header leads with the set's total (`G.total`), gives panel 1 as a muted item, names the plan and the weight in all. `fabMinWidth(pts)` is the least over edges of the farthest vertex from that edge's line; `fabRouterCheck` uses it and `routerNoteText()` is refreshed in `updateStats` through `FAB_ROUTER_NOTE`. `objText` sets `OBJ_NOTE` and returns null over the limit; `exportBundle` toasts it. The KRL has an INI fold, `$TOOL=TOOL_DATA[1]`, `PTP XHOME`, picks under `$BASE=$NULLFRAME` and places under `BASE_DATA[(face-1)*stations+station]`; comments carry the length, squints and closers; `bundleFiles` adds -plan.csv for an assembly. The perforated README describes the perforated image. The three Pattern Studio cards drop the prefix in the list.
- **Pass 21, the robot for every unit mode, the readers in the bundle**: `robotUnits(Gk)` turns the mode's pieces into one list `{n, row, place, len, X, Y, Z, A, B, C, h, up}`: bricks as before (`up:'Z'`, lowered), pavers at their centre on the bed (X along, Y across, Z 0) or, with `S.paver.lay==='wall'`, on the face (Y 0, Z the height, `up:'Y'`, offered), A the turn of the longest edge of `p.pts` folded to (-90, 90], B and C the tilt; a paver's row is its line of centres when the layout is regular (distinct rounded `cy` values at most a third of the count) and a band of the module size otherwise; kinetic units at their grid point on the face (`up:'Y'`, length the unit size, h the fold depth). `robotRows` sorts by station, course (row), place, X and numbers the places a layout did not. `robotCSV` header is `unit,face,station,course,place,length_mm,X_mm,Y_mm,Z_mm,A_deg,B_deg,C_deg,station_X0_mm,kind` (the first column was `brick`; `robot_from_csv.py` reads either). `robotKRL` leads in along `up`. `GH_SCRIPTS` (between `/*GH-BEGIN*/` and `/*GH-END*/`, filled by `catalogue/build-embed.py` from `grasshopper/*_from_csv.py`, checked by `--check`) and `GH_FOR[mode]` give `ghFiles(files, enc)` the readers to put in the bundle under `grasshopper/` with a README. `bundleFiles()` returns the bundle's `{name, data}` list and `exportBundle()` zips it, so a test can read a bundle without saving.
- **Pass 20, corner bonds and the OBJ**: `PLAN_CORNER` is set per panel inside the spanning loop of `generate` (brick mode on an active plan): `{left, right, butt(c, r, isLeft)}`, where at corner c (between side c and c+1) side c runs through on courses with r % 2 == c % 2 and side c+1 butts on them. Since pass 22 the walls' back faces meet on the plan line, so at a convex square corner the running course reaches a full `bW` past the corner plane and the butting one stops at it (at a concave corner, facing in, the running course stops at the plane and the butting one a `bW` short); any other angle is mitred: both courses end at the plane on their back edge and the end brick carries `cutL`/`cutR`, the front edge's overhang `bW * tan(|turn| / 2)` (outward at a convex corner, inward at a concave one), drawn by `brickPrism` instead of a box and written to -bricks.csv. `PLAN_CORNER.turn` is the signed turn from `planLayout`. Under a brick plan `PLAN_FLUSH` lays every panel from joint to joint with the side frames and the gap held at 0; the corner cells are marked (`cells[k][3]`) and their bricks held flat (`brick.cornerHeld`); a sliver under 20 mm at a joint turns the last full brick into two bats. `planLayout` leaves no gap between the sides of a brick plan; `catLoad` reads `gap`. Facing in walks the plan the other way (negative turn) and never turns the panels: a half turn mirrors a panel and moves its corner courses to the wrong end. `objText()` rebuilds the scene and writes every mesh (not the backing or rim) as a group with world-space vertices, y up; the bundle adds `-assembly.obj` for sets, plans, hosts and the mirrored wall, and refuses over 900,000 vertices.
- **Pass 19, assemblies and fabrication**: `S.plan` {kind 'wall' | 'corner' | 'u' | 'court' | 'tower', sides, angle, face 'out' | 'in'} (`PLAN_DEF`; recipes and snapshots carry it). `planLayout(cols)` gives each column its side, start point, heading and distance along the side (whole columns per side, the first `cols % n` sides one more; closed plans turn 2pi/n, open ones 180 minus the angle; `face:'in'` turns the other way); `planPlace`, `planCentre`, `planExtent`; `planActive()` is a grid in the wall orientation with a plan. `rebuildScene` places and turns each panel from it; hosts, the mirror and the room wall step aside; `frameCamera` keys on the plan. `S.fab` {machine 'none' | 'laser' | 'waterjet' | 'router', kerf, toolD, reach, pick}: `fabKerf()`, `fabCut(pts,isHole)` (half a kerf in or out through `offsetPoly`), used by `svgText` and `dxfText` on the cut layers only (`data-kerf` on the SVG group); `fabRouterCheck(G)` counts openings narrower than the tool. `robotStations(Gk)` splits the face into stations of 1.4 x reach; `robotRows` sorts bricks by station, course and place with X, Y = shift + bW/2, Z = y, A = theta; `robotCSV`, `robotKRL` (a `$BASE=BASE_DATA[k]` section per station, LIN/PTP moves, `$OUT[1]` the gripper). QA group `assembly`.
- **Pass 17**: `S.host.kind:'sphere'` maps x to longitude and y to latitude by arc length about a centre a radius behind the facade's middle (the normal turned by both angles); `hostTess(g,maxL,budget,useY)` splits by the larger of the x and y runs for it. `S.host.mirror` (mm, face to face) adds a mirrored clone of every mesh in `hostMirror()` (negative z scale; the room's wall hides; the orbit centres on the gap). `S.paver.tilt` leans each piece by seeded angles up to it about its base (`p.tx`, `p.ty`; `paverGeometry` rotates before merging; `tilt_x_deg`, `tilt_y_deg` close the placement list). `S.paver.colGrid` ('x,y') and `colSnap` move a point-field column onto the nearest grid line within the snap (`G.paver.snapped`). Recipes may set `thickness` for modes without their own (the Biosphere's 1 m lattice).
- **Matrix variants**: `recipes.json` entries may carry `variants` {matrix row name: overrides}; `catLoad(id,vname)` lays them over the control model with `catMerge` (a deep merge; `null` removes a key, as a field a variant does without), and a `_note` replaces the card's note. `catShow` puts a Load button on each matrix row a variant covers (`[data-var]`). `scratchpad/variants.py` wrote the 55 variants.
- **Write a card** (Catalogue tab, the AI reading step): `cardPrompt(desc)` writes the card's shape with every schema enum spelled out (`CARD_ENUMS`, kept in step with `catalogue/schema.json` by hand), the tool's modes and the recipe keys taken from the tool's own defaults (`cardRecipeKeys`); `cardWrite()` sends it through `sampleNS.json` and falls back to `cardHeuristic(desc)` (a skeleton from mode words and mm values) when there is no model. `cardCheck(card)` returns plain sentences for the schema's rules and validate.py's cross-references (mappings and matrix changes naming real keys, printed values, enum options, defaults inside min and max). `cardRecipeClean(r)` keeps only keys the tool's defaults have, with matching types, and lists the rest. `cardAccept(card,via)` compacts the draft (`cardCompact`, the same form build-embed.py writes, with `draft:true`), pushes it into `CATALOGUE`, rebuilds the list (`catRebuildPick`) and shows it; the draft card downloads without its recipe and without the `draft-` prefix.
- **Stack bond, the point field and the cull** (Memorial, KAIT): `tiling:'stack'` is running bond without the offset. `tiling:'points'` takes `paverSites` (Poisson spaced by the tone, or jittered) and `paverColumns(P,sites,...)` puts a `colW` x `colT` rectangle at each site, turned by up to `colTurn` degrees, seeded; a point field has no joint. `S.paver.cull` (0 to 1) leaves out a piece when a seeded draw at its centre falls under cull x (1 - tone) (`G.paver.culled`). The joint is now held at 0.7 of the narrowest piece (it was 0.4), so aisles wider than the blocks fit. `paverUnitWord(tiling)` says panel, column or paver.
- **Nested modules** (Berlin Free University, *Flux* pp. 88-89): `S.paver.tiling:'nested'`. `nestComps(P,hb)` lists every ordered way to fill the band `band` with at most `nestMax` heights from `subs` (within 0.2%, the top piece taking up the difference; nine for 610/1219/1829/3048 in a 3048 band). `paverCells(P,W0,H0,toneAt)` lays floors of `band + spandrel` up from the frame's bottom; along each floor modules A (`modA`) or B (`modB`) in the typed `order`, each run of it shuffled by seed when `shuffle`; each module takes a composition in turn, by seed or by the tone at its centre (`nestBy` 'turn' | 'seed' | 'field', most divided where dark); a spandrel piece per module. Pieces carry `role` ('panel' | 'spandrel') and `mod`; `spanType` forces the spandrels' type; `raw.nest` becomes `G.paver.nest` {floors, mods, comps, exact, heights, ord, by}. `S.paver.lay` 'ground' | 'wall' (choosing Nested modules sets wall; `paverGround` lays flat only for ground). Type names (`S.paver.names`) pair with the colours as typed and survive the luminance sort; they are the last column of both CSVs. Nested layouts report panel area, not weight. A piece is cut when it loses more than half a millimetre along a side (area loss > 0.5 x sqrt(area)), so a module 2 mm short is counted. `catLoad` starts brick, serial, kin and paver from their boot defaults (`MODE_DEF`) and reads `frame`; Berlin's recipe uses exact feet (609.6, 1219.2, 914.4).
- **Host surfaces** (3D only): `S.host` {kind 'plane' | 'cyl' | 'wave', radius, side 'convex' | 'concave', waves, a0, a1, phase}. At the end of `rebuildScene`, `hostApply(TW,TH)` bakes each mesh's position into its vertices, subdivides with `hostTess(g,maxL)` (bisects the edge with the largest x-run until it is under 4% of the radius or a twentieth of a wavelength; every attribute interpolated; a 2.4M-vertex budget) and bends: cylinder by arc length (theta = x/R, the normal turned by theta), wave as z + A(y) sin(k(x + TW/2) + p) with A ramping from a0 at the base to a1 at the top (normal by the inverse transpose). Triptychs stay flat. The flat `baseGeom` still feeds the STL. `HOST_DEF` resets a recipe without a host; snapshots carry it (`applyPreset`). QA hook `T.hostScene()` rebuilds the scene and returns the bent geometries; group `host`.
- **Pavers, second pass** (from the 30 Oct paver sweep). `paverPaletteInfo(P)` -> `{colours,bad,added,given}`: hex via `hexNorm` (3 or 6 digits), sorted by `hexLum`, spread darkest to lightest when there are more colours than types, topped up from `PAVER_BASE` (now a grey ramp first) when fewer. `paverHeightsInfo(P,n)` accepts 0 and reports bad entries; `paverThick()` sets `S.thickness`. `paverCells` covers the frame's rotated bounding box plus two modules (corners turned back by the spin), so turned layouts on big panels build; Cairo sets `o.capped` at 40,000. The joint is held below 0.4 x the narrowest paver (`paver.jointHeld`), and every paver carries `net` (its area inside the half-joint offset): the CSV, schedule (`Pk.areas`) and weight use `net`, while `area` stays the module. `paverSetCounts()` totals a spanning set. `apart`: greedy in reading order, then up to 60 min-conflicts sweeps, then if pairs remain a DSatur recolouring (free type nearest the tone) kept only if it leaves fewer; hexagons with three types reach zero. Notes (`paver.notes`) carry what was held, added or not understood into the spec. `paverGround(on)` lays pavers face up (`orient:'overhead'`, `mount:0`; `rebuildScene` raises the group by t/2+1 so bases sit on the floor) and restores `PAVER_PREV` on leaving; a reopened design with no record falls back to the element's own orientation. The QA `reset()` clears `PAVER_PREV`. Voronoi zone: `paverZone(P,...)` reads `S.paver.zone` as u,v points; `paverSites` lays a `zoneGX` x `zoneGY` grid inside it and Poisson sites outside (several seeded restarts), drawn as `.paver-zone` in the setting-out. The 2D toggle's second button is relabelled per mode by a binding.
- **Bricks on the wave, and the third paver sweep.** `hostWaveCourse(h,W,H,y,xa,xb)` gives a course's depth, slope and an arc-length table (x from arc length by bisection); on a single panel with a wave host `brickGenerate` divides each course by its own arc length, adds the curve's depth to `shift` and turns each brick by -atan(slope) after the bearing clamp, and sets `G.brick.onHost` (so `hostApply` skips it) and `hostCourses`. Sweep fixes: `applyPreset` restores the orientation from `PAVER_PREV` when a proposal names none; `applyElement` keeps pavers on the ground; `modeThickness()` keeps the thickness of pavers, bricks and ribs through material and type changes; triptych leaves lie flat on the ground; the Material tab hides the sheet chips for pavers and bricks; `paverHeightsInfo` keeps a bad entry's place; `PAVER_BASE` is a grey ramp and top-ups fill the widest gap in tone; jittered Voronoi holds the joint to half the module; the Voronoi estimate samples the picture and a fill that reaches 6,500 sites is refused (`sites.capped`); `paverZone` reads numbers with any separator; the zone lattice is centred in the zone's box, keeps only whole cells, and is ringed by lattice sites so they are exact rectangles, with Poisson sites kept a half-diagonal clear (`G.paver.zoneCells`); pieces the joint swallows are dropped; `paverSetCounts()` multiplies a repeated set (`rep`; `paverScheduleCSV(list,rep)`); `capWrap` breaks the setting-out caption at its separators; the bank names designs with `pinTitle()`.
- **Kinetic units, third pass** (from the 30 Oct sweep): the sun motion is direct sun on the face, cos(elevation) x cos(azimuth off the normal), fully deployed at `sunFull` (0.5) of square-on, with optional shading by the Field > Sun overhang and reveals (`shade`); `blend:'max'` really takes the larger of picture and motion; `rest:false` lets every unit rest open (White Noise White Light is dark at rest); walker points are parsed by `kinPt` (bad input falls back) and typed with v UP like the attractor field; `kinHours` takes the two hours either way round and the CSV headers are HH:MM; a unit wider than the pitch is held to the pitch (`kin.unitHeld`) and drawn at that size; the 2D frame is a ring, not a plate; the DXF follows the hex grid and carries PETALS; state numbers show in the orthographic view up to 400 units; the mode swaps a dark finish for aluminium. `capFS(text,width,m)` sizes the two caption lines of every new orthographic view to fit; `modeWarn()` puts the generator's warning at the head of the stats for the new panel kinds; the stage headings are short so the One/All buttons do not cover them.
- **The Cairo "voids" were the solid fraction.** The sweep saw hexagon-shaped holes in Confluence Park's cut: pairs of cells left solid by the Pattern tab's default `solid: .38`. Recipes can now set `solid` and `radius`; Confluence Park opens every cell (163 openings, 84% open). The Cairo edge de-duplication was made order-independent at the same time (vertices snapped within 0.2 mm), which is sound but was not the cause.
- **Kinetic units, second pass**: `S.kin.grid` 'square' | 'hex'. On 'hex' the rows are 0.866 x pitch apart and odd rows shift half a pitch; `kinRim(K,cx,cy,h)` returns the frame a unit's parts hinge on (a pointy-top hexagon with flat-to-flat = unit size, or n points on a circle), and `kinUnitSVG` / `kinGeometry` draw every frame and petal from it (the hex frame in 3D is an extruded ring). `motion:'wind'` samples `fbm` at (u·gust + t·windSpeed, v·gust + 0.6·t·windSpeed). `perfCSV(G)` is the perforated hole list (perfGenerate now stores cx, cy, i, j, t on each hole). QA group `pwall`, 2 checks.
- **Cairo pentagons** (`PATTERNS.cairo`, a `net` pattern): base pentagon [(0,0),(−.866,−.5),(−.366,−1.366),(.366,−1.366),(.866,−.5)] × s where s = P/1.5; the tiling is grown by rotating 90/180/270° about vertices 1 and 4 (the 90° corners) only; shared edges are emitted once (a duplicate edge set overflowed `facesFromSegments`' pair Set). Rotating 120° about the 120° corners too over-generates: this pentagon is the snub-square dual, not the basketweave Cairo, and the two differ.
- **Serial sections, second pass** (sweep fixes): the mode takes plywood unless the stock is already timber (black steel was being kept, which blackened the elevation and made the mass 805 kg); a strip wider than the sheet now holds `dMax` to what fits (`serial.dMaxHeld`) and says so; waste is reported as `cutWaste` (inside the strips) and `sheetLeft` (sheet unused); the nest wraps its sheets into rows; the elevation draws each rib's edge light and the shadow under it dark by depth. Recipes may carry `arr`, `cols`, `rows` and `material`.
- **Attractor curve field** (`attractCurve`): distance to a polyline of u,v points; the Memorial's site border and Chi She's doorway.
- The catalogue (`catalogue/`) is the source of truth for what a system is; each card's `pattern_studio.status` says how much of it the tool does.

## Testing
`python qa/run-qa.py` builds a throwaway copy of the tool with `qa/harness.js` injected, runs it in headless Chrome and writes `qa/report.md` + `qa/report.json`. 388 checks in twenty-three groups, ~230 s, all passing (passes 162 to 181 merged, 20 Nov; passes 177 to 179 have no check of their own). Several sessions running the suite at once clash on the Chrome profile in the temp folder and a group then "did not report": run with `QA_TAG=<name>-` so the profile is this run's own. `--group <name>` filters what *runs*, not just what is reported. Exit 0 pass, 1 fail, 2 would not boot. The `pattern-studio-qa` subagent (`.claude/agents/`) runs it and explains the failures.
`python qa/run-prompts.py` is the second bench (42 briefs): every brief in `qa/prompts.json` through `heuristic()` → `validateProposal()`, checked against what the words say it should produce. It runs the **keyword** path, which is what a reader without Claude access gets, so it is a floor rather than a proof of the prompt. The `pattern-studio-prompts` subagent owns it and is allowed to fix `heuristic()`. Adding a bench case is cheaper than arguing about whether a brief was read right — the first run of it found nine real gaps (unknown materials, unknown motifs, no size parsing, a motif name read as a picture).
The one rule in `heuristic()` that keeps breaking is **ordering**: it is a cascade of `if`s and the last one wins, so `stainless` must precede `steel`, `basket weave` must follow `weave`, and `motifRe` must beat `subjectRe` or "a fern-leaf screen" becomes a photograph.
Three things cost hours to rediscover, so they are written down:
- `--dump-dom` needs `pathlib.Path(p).as_uri()`; the repo path has spaces and `!!` in it and an unencoded `file:///` URL silently dumps nothing.
- The suite must yield between cases (`setTimeout(drain,1)`). One long synchronous block starves the message loop and headless never gets round to dumping.
- `performance.now()` is frozen under `--virtual-time-budget`, so in-page timings all read 0 ms. Time from outside the browser.
- Headless Chrome on this machine often never exits after `--dump-dom` has written the page. `run-qa.py` therefore watches the dump file for the `RESULT` title and the closing `</html>` and kills the browser itself; `QA_EXTRA=<seconds>` adds wall-clock allowance per group. Any new headless driver must do the same, or a finished run looks like a 30-minute hang.
Metrics worth knowing: **fidelity** is the correlation between the tone each tile was asked for and the tone it actually returns under the design light — it tests the solve and the renderer agree, not whether the picture is nice. **toneSpread** is how much of black-to-white the design uses. **dial** is the range of the tone strip at the current design light; under ~0.18 the panel cannot hold a picture.

## Grasshopper
`grasshopper/` has two GhPython components and a README. `ombrae_from_image.py` does the whole job in GH; `ombrae_from_csv.py` rebuilds a panel from the `-tiles.csv` the web tool exports, so the two can be checked against each other. Written for IronPython 2.7 and CPython 3 both: no f-strings, `System.Drawing` for the image. `ombrae_from_image.gh` is that component wired up in Rhino 8 (Python 3 Script, inputs on the "Any" hint). Oct 2026, driven via the Rhino MCP: `ombrae_from_image.py` solves cleanly (3,308 tiles at defaults, solid ≈ 4.7 s) and reads an image the right way up (black disc → tone 0.01 inside, 0.99 outside). Still never run: `ombrae_from_csv.py`, the browser-vs-Rhino tile-for-tile check, IronPython 2, and a visual check in the viewport — say so in anything said about them.

## Navigation and state
- **The tab is the mode.** `TAB_MODE` maps Pattern / Ombrae tiles / Perforated image to cut /
  tiles / perf; `showTab(name, byUser)` calls `setPanelMode` only on a user click, so programmatic
  `showTab` (presets, the tour) never flips the mode behind someone's back. `orderTabs()` puts
  the rail in `TAB_ORDER` regardless of build order, which is why Pattern sits above the two
  picture tabs although its code is built after them.
- **What is read travels.** `carrySource()` runs after a successful `srcAnalyze`: the image
  becomes `OMB.srcEl` (so Ombrae and Perforated carry it) and the classic pattern of the detected
  group goes into `S.pattern`. Switching tabs then shows the same thing built three ways.
- **Panel size & arrangement is built three times** (`panelSetup(parent)`, one per panel tab)
  over one state; `EL_DYNS[]` holds each copy's dynamic rows so `buildElementDyn()` rebuilds all
  of them. Composition is a fold inside Pattern (`compHost`), not a tab.
- **Undo/redo** is snapshot-based: `historyPush()` runs at the end of `run()`, keys entries on
  the snapshot minus the tone-field data URL, collapses pushes within 700 ms (a slider drag is
  one step), and `HIST.skip` suppresses the push that an undo itself causes. `restoreDesign(p)`
  is `applyPreset` plus the two things it does not carry: which panel kind was on, and a picture
  for a perforated design. `snapshot()` now saves the field when either picture mode is on.
- **Bank** reuses the `pins` array (name, when, snapshot, thumbnail) and localStorage key
  `ps-pins`; `exportBank()` rebuilds each design in turn — awaiting the field image first — and
  restores the studio afterwards. Perf designs over 2,500 holes render in 3D as a `BoxGeometry`
  plate wearing the holes as a `CanvasTexture` alphaMap (`perfAlphaCanvas`), with a
  `customDepthMaterial` so the shadow pass knows about the holes; that is also what lets the
  backlit plane show through.
- **Tour** (`TOUR[]`, `window.PS_TOUR`) is the single source for the Demo button and for
  `qa/record-demo.py`. Seventeen steps: guide (opens the welcome card), read, describe, pattern, set (Across the set + orthographic One/All/Panel n of N), photograph as halftone cut, tiles, perforated, material, light,
  bank, export (the pattern step clicks `#srcClassic` so the classic screen of the read group is what shows).
  The film is 26 stills held 3.8-5 s by caption length, crossfaded, about 108 s. Since pass 18 every tab's `How to use this tab` steps and its long
  explanation are ONE help fold at the foot of the tab, opened by the `?` button by the title (the lede is `LEDE[name]`). The proposal readback tables are `table-layout:fixed`
  with wrapping cells; QA `layout` proposes a brief and fails if the readback is wider than the rail. Step 3 cannot call the model headlessly, so a recording shows the keyword
  reader's built-in picture and the caption says so; the live artifact draws from the words.
- **Spanning picture.** `S.omb.span` + `spanOn()` (needs tiles or perf and a non-single arrangement). The tone
  field is rasterised at `fieldW()×fieldH()` (the whole array, joints included; canvas cap 1400 px); `generate()`
  loops the panels with `OMB.sp={ox,oy,TW,TH}` and `uvFor()` maps a panel-local point into the field; the result
  is panel 0 plus `G.panels[k]` (k = col*rows + row, row 0 at the bottom), `G.span`, `G.total`. 2D renders, ortho,
  `rebuildScene` (one geometry + material per panel, `mesh.userData.Gk`), `updateMaterial` and `exportBundle`
  (files per panel, `-c1-r1`) all read `G.panels`. `span` defaults to TRUE (a photo on several panels is one picture); `applyPreset` restores that default unless the preset says `span:false` (the flower spandrel does).
  Built-in photo `skyline` and the two **North Shore skyline · six panels** presets are the test case; QA covers it
  in `perforated` and `tile-designs`.
- **Stand-ins.** `standInFor(txt)` picks the built-in that stands in for a described scene when no
  model is in the page (gondola, ridge before wave with forest words, flower, clouds only for cloud/sky/rain, rings, ramp,
  else `facets`). Both `heuristic()` image chains and both Accept placeholders use it; nothing
  defaults to the cloud bank any more. `WAVES_BRIEF` is the second worked brief (perforated, backlit).
- **Framing.** `S.omb.zoom/fx/fy` enlarge the fitted source about a focus point in `ombDrawSource`;
  `fitKeyNow()` is the raster cache key (size, fit, zoom, focus). Controls live in a `Framing` fold under
  the picture in both panel tabs. The tour's perforated step sets zoom 1.8 at (.46,.4) to frame the cabin.
- The Agent tool in a running session only knows the agent types loaded at start-up, so the
  cron prompts say `subagent_type "general-purpose"` and have the agent **read its own
  definition file first**. A fresh Claude Code session can call the five agents by name.

## Panel hierarchy
Each of the three panel tabs is built into named host `div`s created up front in design order
(`ombLead, ombPic, ombPanelHost, ombTiles, ombToneHost, ombSun, ombWorked, ombDialHost, ombAbout`
and the `perf*` equivalents), so source order no longer dictates screen order and a control can
be moved by changing one host name. `segc()` is a segmented control bound to state, `stepper()`
a minus/plus count, and every checkbox is styled as a switch; `buildPatternParams` renders a
select with three or fewer options as a segment. The Ombrae picture chooser is a four-way picker
(built-in / upload / lettering / describe) showing one block at a time over the same element ids,
with the fit select kept hidden so presets and the fit segment stay in sync.
**Backing in 3D:** with a perforated backing the room's own `wall` mesh is resized to the panel
and moved to `-(t+60)` with `wallGlow` (unlit) or `wallColour`; without one it goes back to
`light.wall` and `wallSurf`. Overhead panels keep an in-group plane because they have no wall.
Backlit, or the **Night** time-of-day chip (`S.light.night`), drives `night` in `updateLights`: sun x0.03,
lamp x0.22, hemi <= 0.025, camera fill 0.02, near-black floor and background. Night switches to the lamp.
Bank entries carry `pinned`; the strip shows only pinned ones, the Bank tab all of them. Unpin never
deletes; delete lives behind *Edit the bank*. QA group `layout` opens every fold in every tab and
arrangement at a 1500 px window and fails if anything is wider than the rail.
The tour card docks over the control column (`.tour` right:134px) so it never covers a viewport.
**Recording 3D frames headless:** under `--virtual-time-budget` the compositor presents a WebGL frame only
sometimes. `PS_TOUR.freeze()` renders synchronously, copies the drawing buffer with `toDataURL` in the same task
and lays it over the canvas as an `<img>` (id `psFreeze`); `PS_TOUR.gl()` reports context loss and draw calls.
`window.__PS_COARSE` (set by the recorder for frames after the tile step) makes the tour's tile step coarse and
skips its sun sweep. The recorder uses a fresh Chrome profile per frame: Chrome remembers GPU crashes per profile.
The film is sixteen stills, each a 3 s clip with a slow push-in (ffmpeg `zoompan`), crossfaded 0.5 s (`xfade`),
encoded crf 27 to stay under the artifact host's 15 MB file limit; `--video-only` rebuilds it from `review/frames/`.
Perforated plates in 3D use a 3072 px alpha map with blended transparency (alphaTest .04), so from a distance a
field of small holes reads as tone and a backlit sheet glows where the picture is light.

## UI density
The control panel is folded: each tab shows the five or six controls that are decisions, and
everything else sits in a `details.grp` collapsible built by `group(parent,title,hint,open)`.
The **Essentials / Everything** segment in the header opens or closes all of them at once
(`GROUPS[]`). **Panel mode** (through cut / emboss / carve / terrace) is the first control in
the Pattern tab, not buried in 3D panel, because it decides what everything under it means.
Thickness lives in **Material**, as a chip row of the stock gauges that material is rolled in,
because thickness is a property of the stock and not of the geometry.
Both viewports have a **Save image** button; `viewPNG('2d')` serialises the stage SVG, resolves
the theme's CSS variables into it, and redraws it into a canvas at 2x. `S` / `Shift+S` do the
same from the keyboard.

## Conventions
- Single self-contained HTML files; all CSS/JS inline except the two CDN libraries. Keep it that way so GitHub Pages works with no tooling.
- Fonts: Instrument Sans + DM Mono (Google Fonts). Palette tokens live in the `:root` block of each file; both light and dark are defined.
- Units are millimetres everywhere in Pattern Studio. DXF layers: OUTLINE / OPENINGS (through cuts) and CUTS / SLOT_CENTRELINES (map tool).
- Everything the detector or the text model produces is a proposal; UI copy must say so and the rule card / provenance record must travel with exports.
- Test in headless Chromium (Playwright). The three.js and Leaflet CDNs may be blocked in sandboxes; inline the library from `npm pack` into a test copy when that happens.

## Known limits / next steps
- Detector tuned on synthetic Gaussian-blob patterns (42/44) and now also passes all 17 crisp `SAMPLES` test cases; still not validated on real storefront or textile photos. Shape classifier is rule-based (circularity, solidity, lobes, aspect) and coarse at small repeats.
- Colour reading: k-means palette (≤6), per-layer tracing; the lattice from the whole image is reused for layer reads.
- Ombrae reflectance is Lambert + a Blinn highlight with a frontal viewer in 2D; it is not a measured BRDF for brushed or anodised aluminium, and inter-tile shadowing and bounce are ignored (the 3D view does cast real shadows). The 30° tilt and 3 mm standoff are read off a brochure section, so treat them as plausible, not verified.
- Not built: vector trace of a full motif into editable curves, Parakeet genotypes, irregular tilings, Rhino/Grasshopper live preview, the Grasshopper definition that would drive a real tile panel from `-tiles.csv`, a viewing-angle control for the 2D tile simulation (the 3D view stands in for it), Claude-vision worded description beside the geometric reading (artifact `sample` capability with images), the Punjabi Market motif library from user sessions.
- Dates: user match by Nov 6, peer reviews Nov 13/17 and Nov 27, final live review Dec 10.
